# Design volume 5 — Floating islands: the sky layer

> PENDING FABLE REVIEW — written by opus standing in for the Fable seat volumes/V5-sky; covered by the whole-design retrovalidation (J84).

## 0 In plain words

Some cities have land floating in the sky above them. This volume says exactly when, how big, what is on it, where it
hangs, how people get up to it, what it looks like from above, and what happens to it when the city grows poor.

**When there are islands.** Only cities and metropolises can have them, and only when the owner's fantasy setting for
islands is switched on (it is off by default, so the realistic map never has one). Even then it is a roll of the dice
weighted by wealth: a poor city gets none, a middling one sometimes one, a rich one may get none, one, two or three.
Magic is the resource that holds an island up and it has to be paid for all the time, so the richer the city, the more
likely an island, the higher it floats and the bigger it may be; and the richer the people living on it, the bigger it
may be too.

**What is on an island.** Either a very rich neighbourhood (one to three neighbourhoods, taken from the richest people of
the city, so the city's population stays the same — they have simply moved up), or one unique institution: a palace
looking down over the land, a cathedral, or a prison that keeps its prisoners far from everyone. The island is laid out
like any other part of the city — streets, plots, houses, gardens — by the same machinery that draws a neighbourhood
close-up, but with no road leading in or out. A large rich island is usually two or three neighbourhoods, a small one a
single neighbourhood. Its wealth is not flat: the richest take the high ground and the rim that looks out over the city,
and the island's less wealthy households live on the lower, inner ground. When the cathedral or the palace moves up, the
site the town kept for it beside the main square is not left empty: the town's largest church takes the old cathedral
close, and the old palace site becomes the town hall or the palace gardens. The island cathedral's burial crypt is cut
into the island's own rock beneath it — still the largest crypt in the town — and the island never has catacombs: those
lie only under the cemeteries on the ground.

**What it looks like.** Each island has its own shape (round, D-shaped, a lozenge, a crescent or a long spindle), a rocky
rim, its own gentle hills that echo the country below, and often a small magically conjured river that flows the same way
as the river on the ground and spills off the edge as a waterfall that turns to mist. An island's water is its own: the
oases and the pull toward water that shape a desert town below never reach it. It floats over water, fields or the
outskirts — over the city's heart only if someone asks for it there — high enough that nothing on the ground touches its
rocky underside. From above you see the island drawn over the city with the ground showing faintly through it (you can
switch it to solid, or to a "lifted" view that shows the rock hanging beneath it), and its shadow falls on the ground and
moves with the sun under your mouse pointer. At night the rim glows with small magical lights and a soft glow shines down
from beneath it onto the ground.

**How people get up.** By hoists — winch houses on the rim lowering cages on ropes to a landing yard below, the way some
real clifftop monasteries were reached — or, over water, by an inclined rope line to a tower on the shore. Where the
world has magic, a floating stair of stepping stones or a bridge of light may join them. Every island has at least one
way up, and every landing on the ground is a small yard on a street.

**The shade it throws on the ground.** An island blocks the sun. For a typical day the generator works out how long
each patch of ground lies in an island's shade — the same way it works out the shade of hills and walls — using the
island's real bulk: its thick rocky rim and the root tapering beneath it. Ground that spends much of the day in that
shade tends to be a little poorer, so cheaper lodgings and workshops gather in the island's "under-hang", while the rich
keep to the sunny side. It is a gentle tendency, never a rule: the shade alone never moves a patch of ground by more than
one step of wealth. If an island is moved, raised or lowered, the ground beneath catches up at the town's next period
rather than the moment you drag it. A shade map shows where each island's shade falls across the day, and the clerk can
answer "why is this quarter poor?" with "it lies in the shade of the Palace island for half the day". The map remembers
where each island stood when the ground last took its shade, so saving and reopening a map never changes the picture;
one button ("Update shade now") brings the ground under the old and new shade up to date at once.

**Who trades with whom.** Shops and workshops on an island follow the same siting rules as those on the ground, but a
trip to the ground means a ride down a hoist: an island's baker sits near the hoist that leads down towards the mill,
and a smith on the ground who serves the island stands near its landing yard. Each of an island's neighbourhoods counts
as a district when the rules ask for "an apothecary in every district". Smelly and dirty trades, the heavy works (mills,
smelters, breweries) and the warehouses never go up — they belong by the gates and quays — but a trade with a works and
a shop, like a butcher with a slaughterhouse and a stall, may keep its works on the ground and open its shop on the
island. A supply chain keeps its working steps together on the ground, while the island may hold the shop that sells
its last product or the people who buy it, the goods riding the hoist between them.

**Walls.** An island needs no wall: its cliffs are its wall. If you draw a wall on an island anyway, only the tops of its
hoists are walled, each with a gate, and the clerk explains that "the drop is the wall" everywhere else.

**Viaducts, raised roads, aqueducts and terraces.** They pass beneath an island and never reach one; the island floats
clear of them. The hoists' landing yards keep clear of the arches, a rope line passes well above any deck it crosses,
and a road you draw toward an island's edge is turned into the offer of a hoist instead: "A road cannot reach a floating
island; a hoist, rope line, stair or bridge of light can." An island on steep ground may be terraced, with its own
retaining walls, but never along its edge: the rim walk stays level, and a terrace wall you draw there is moved just
inside it. When an island falls, the arches, piers and terrace walls beneath it break with everything else it lands on,
and the shops standing on a covered bridge fall with the bridge.

**Where magic wells up.** If the settlement's own record says it sits on a magical node, the town pays less to keep
islands aloft, so an island is a little more likely, a little bigger and a little higher there.

**When the city declines.** If the city gets poorer, its islands shrink a little and sink lower. If the magic can no
longer be paid for at all, the people leave, the island sinks to its lowest height and stands empty; if nothing changes
for years, it finally comes down, leaving a field of fallen rock on the ground that people quarry for building stone —
the walls, towers, bridges and streets it lands on are broken or buried too, until the town clears them. If wealth
returns in time, an abandoned island is raised again before any new one appears. If the town's own record removes the
cathedral or the palace an island carries, the island finds a new purpose at the town's next period — another institution
of the same kind, or a rich neighbourhood — or, failing both, slowly sinks.

**Editing.** Everything can be changed: switch islands on or off, choose how many, draw an island where you want it,
drag it elsewhere, raise or lower it, make it bigger or smaller, reshape its rim, add or move a hoist or a stair, rename
it, paint its wealth, density or greenery with the same brushes as the ground, edit anything on it like any other
neighbourhood, or bring it down by hand. Every change can be undone, and changing an island never disturbs the city below
except the landing yards of its hoists. An island or a hoist you remove stays removed: the city never raises it again by
itself — the clerk says "removed by you" — unless you undo the removal, or draw an island yourself when every other place
is taken; asking for more islands opens new places only. If you switch islands on and none appears, the map says why in
plain words — the city is not rich enough to keep one aloft — and offers the two remedies: raise its wealth, or draw one
yourself.

## 1 Scope, the base hooks consumed, and what is out of scope

**Pointers.** A bare `§n` (like `II.n`, `D-n` and the `J…` rows) names the base, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, with files f
and g applied; a volume's section, this volume's own included, is written `Vnn §n` (`V05 §3.2`, `V01 §3.10.3`,
`V06 §2.1`), and a list or range that follows a volume prefix stays in that volume (`V06 §2.1, §3.2` is two sections of
volume 6). A base change this volume requested is cited by the base section that now carries it; V05 §10 maps each
request to its file f item. **Two kinds of tier** (J-V5.66): this volume's **underside tiers** are the rock beneath an
island (underside tier 0 the rim slab, underside tiers 1 … n the root, V05 §3.9 (2)); item 110 (c)'s **massing tiers**
are a stepped building's stacked wings (V02 §3.15). Wherever both could be meant, the text says which; a bare "tier" in
V05 §3.9 (2) and V05 §3.19 (2)–(5) is an underside tier. **Raised-way piers** are item 110 (b)'s supports on ground
part 0; a bare "pier" is a harbour pier (§4.8).

### 1.1 What this volume builds (to build depth)

1. **Existence and count** — the rung gate, the model setting, the dossier's coherence gate, the three ordered slots
   (V05 §3.2).
2. **The supply law made measurable** — the town and carried supplies on absolute field values (V05 §3.3).
3. **Purpose** — a prosperity island or an institution island, per slot, by rule or by assignment, with the institution
   rows (palace, cathedral, prison, tower) (V05 §3.4).
4. **The sky allocation** — S7b (5)'s fill and the population kept whole (V05 §3.5).
5. **Shape** — the outline in the island's own local frame (V05 §3.6).
6. **Placement** — one scored draw with a failure ladder (V05 §3.7).
7. **The island arena** — its frame, its L0 (the void, the land, the rim, water), its echoed terrain, its conjured river
   and waterfall (V05 §3.8).
8. **Altitude and the underside** — the rocky root, its profile and thickness, the clearance over the ground (V05 §3.9;
   the owner's J98).
9. **Access** — hoists, rope lines, floating stairs and bridges of light, with a landing yard at each end (V05 §3.10).
10. **The island's field and the unit inputs** — a gradient carrying each band on its share of the land, and the inputs
    to volume 1's unit run, which lays the island's districts (V05 §3.11, §3.12).
11. **Lights and names** (V05 §3.13, §3.14).
12. **Over time** — the island laws an epoch applies, the households they move through volume 6's typed channels, an
    island whose institution the dossier removes, the fall and its landfall over every covered kind (V05 §3.15, §3.16).
13. **Failure ladders and determinism** (V05 §3.17, §3.18); **every edge defined** (V05 §3.23); **editing, rendering,
    labels, exports, budgets, tests, build plan** (V05 §4–§8).
14. **The island's shade strip** (item 102 on the island's side, with J98's underside) — the shade each island casts
    across the model sampler's day, folded into the prosperity field's daylight term in field pass 2; the sky receivers'
    shade; its lag behind edits; the altitude fixed at SKY so the strip can be read before the plots are cut (V05 §3.9,
    §3.19).
15. **The siting rulebook across the island** (item 103) — the route distance the one placement solver reads between
    arenas, the island's districts as coverage units, the trades an island never carries, the landing classes' siting
    facts, and a custom institution (item 104) taken by its scene archetype (V05 §2.4, §3.4, §3.5, §3.11, §3.20).
16. **Custom supply chains across the island** (item 104 (h)) (V05 §3.5 (3), §3.20 (9)).
17. **The magical node** — the dossier's `magical_node` deposit as a term of the town supply (V05 §3.3), the consumer
    volume 8's deposits row names (V08 §2.6).
18. **The shade basis** — the persisted copy of the island as the field last read it, so the owner's lag (item 102 (e))
    survives a save, a load and a replay (V05 §2.1, §3.19).
19. **The ground reservation's hand-over and the island cathedral's crypt** (item 109 (k) as revised, J111; V05 §3.5,
    §3.10 (5)).
20. **The rim as a barrier** (item 106) (V05 §3.21).
21. **The supply web, spread and pull, and ladders across the island** (items 105, 107, 108) (V05 §3.4, §3.5,
    §3.20 (10)–(12)).
22. **Item 110 on, under and against the island** — casters under an island, the accesses against raised decks, a raised
    stroke to a rim bent into an access (J-VI.4 (d)), the island's own retaining walls, what the raised-way generator reads
    of the sky, the landfall over item 110's parts, the strip's receiver heights under levelling, and a removed island or
    access that stays removed (V05 §3.22, with §3.2 (6), §3.9, §3.10, §3.15 (5), (7), §3.16, §4.2).
23. **An island's own water under item 111** — the oasis, the water pull and item 111's climate cell do not apply on a sky
    island; its water stays this volume's (V05 §3.8 (4), §3.11).

### 1.3 Out of scope, and whose it is

* **Laying the island's districts, blocks, plots and buildings** — volume 1's unit run (V01 §3.10.3). This volume hands it
  the unit inputs (V05 §2.3) and reads back what it made.
* **Scheduling epochs** — volume 6. This volume states the island laws an epoch applies (`IslandEpochStep`, V05 §3.15)
  and the conversions they emit.
* **Screens, panels and gestures** — volume 9; this volume fixes the tools' parameters, the events and the bend keys
  (V05 §4). **The clerk's prompts** — volume 10; this volume fixes what the clerk reads and proposes (V05 §4.5). **Word
  lists and grammar rows** for island names — volume 11 (V05 §3.14). **Calibration runs** — volume 12; every
  calibrate-later constant here names its default, its measurement and its fallback.
* **The underground** — volume 4. Islands have no relation to it (item 2). Two consequences of item 109 (k) as revised
  (J111) are this volume's: a churchyard on a sky plot is a surface burial ground only, never a catacomb candidate and
  never ranked as the largest cemetery, while each church on an island still counts once toward the catacomb count
  (V04 §3.5.1 (3), `CatacombRule.count`'s `churchesPer`, V04 §2.3); and an island cathedral's crypt is a vault in the
  island's rim slab, part of its building (V05 §3.5, §3.10 (5); J-V5.43). The landfall's quarried rubble sources no
  underground quarry (V05 §3.16).
* **The daylight term itself** — its weight `w_sun`, the model sampler, pass 1's terrain and wall shade, the sunlit-side
  preference of S10b and its bars are §4.5's and §13's; this volume gives the islands' share. **Drift over epochs** is
  volume 6's (item 102 (e)); this volume states only when an island's strip is re-read (V05 §3.19 (6)). **The shade map
  view** is volume 9's; **every class's siting profile, supply edges, coverage rows and signature pairs** volume 11's.
  **Custom chains** — their discovery, activation and arrival volume 8's, a chain's street-scale closeness and any
  quarter it is named past item 72's threshold volume 1's (item 107 (e)), the chain rows volume 11's; this volume states
  only what the island changes (V05 §3.20 (9)). **The `magical_node` deposit's position** is volume 8's and volume 4's;
  this volume reads only its state (V05 §3.3). **The crypt's room plan** is volume 3's and its basement form volume 2's;
  this volume fixes only where an island cathedral's crypt may lie (V05 §3.10 (5)). **Item 110's own work** is the
  base's (§2.7, §4.3, §4.7), volume 1's (terraced wards, the island's levelling), volume 2's (massing, deck buildings),
  volume 4's (the vaults under raised ways) and volume 6's (all of it over epochs); this volume states only what an
  island changes about them and what they change about an island (V05 §3.22). **Item 111's water** — the oasis, the
  water pull and the climate cell — is §4.2, §4.4, §4.5 and §4.7's on the ground; this volume states only that none of it
  reaches an island (V05 §3.8 (4), §3.11).

## 2 Data model

### 2.1 The island record

`SkyIsland`, `UndersideProfile` and `ShadeBasis` are declared in `@plat/model` as this section gives them (§2.7 names
the fields the base reads).

```ts
// every length integer cm, every value on the field's [0, 1] scale
interface SkyIsland {
  id: Id                          /* 'sky<part>/island:<token>' (§6.3) */;
  slot: 1 | 2 | 3                 /* the count law's ordered slot it fills (V05 §3.2); a fallen island frees it; a removed one holds it closed until `removed.releasedBy` is set (V05 §3.2 (6)) */;
  key: PlatKey                    /* { layer: 'sky', part }: part taken from Document.nextSkyPart at creation, never reused */;
  anchor: PointCm                 /* world position of the arena's local origin: an island-arena point p stands over the ground at anchor + p */;
  rim: ChainRef                   /* ('rim', island id): the closed structural L0 chain bounding its land (V05 §3.8) */;
  land: FaceRef                   /* its L0 land face(s) inside the rim */;
  areaHa: number                  /* the target area the rim is sized to — the supply law's A or an edited value; the land face's area within ± 1 % of it (V05 §3.6, §3.15) */;
  altitudeCm: Cm                  /* the surface datum above groundRefCm — the law's altitude floored by the rim, the root and the clearance (V05 §3.9), or an edited value */;
  groundRefCm: Cm                 /* above the ground datum: the greatest, under the projected solid, of the ground's terrain height plus the caster ceiling there — fixed at SKY, before the plots are cut (V05 §3.9 (3)); raised only by SKY-F's clearance check, `reclearIsland` (V05 §3.15 (7)) or a ground edit beneath (V05 §3.9 (5), §4.3) */;
  // elevationCm is derived at read: groundRefCm + altitudeCm — the surface datum of the island's own terrain and the `z` base of every sky mark (§8.1, §8.4)
  underside: UndersideProfile     /* the rock beneath, which shapes its shadow (V05 §3.9; J98) */;
  terrain?: Terrain               /* §2.3: its own, key = this island's; datumCm = the ground datum + elevationCm, kept in step (V05 §3.8) */;
  terrainEcho?: { river?: Id /* the ground river it echoes */; relief: 'echo'|'own'|'none';
    reliefClass?: 'plain'|'rolling'|'hilly'|'mountainous' /* §4.2 S0's relief classes: the class SKY drew for an `own` relief (V05 §3.8 (3)); absent for `echo` and `none`; read by volume 1's `terraceState` (V01 §3.17.7 (2)) and V05 §3.22 (4) */ };
  river?: Id                      /* its conjured WaterBody on its own arena (V05 §3.8) */;
  districts: Id[]; allocation: Id /* its S7b (5) sky Allocation */;
  reason: 'prosperity'|'institution'; institution?: Id /* the Institution it carries */;
  placement: { over: 'water'|'fields'|'outskirts'|'core'; byAssignment: boolean };
  occlusion?: 'opaque'|'ghost'|'offset'   /* a per-island override of the sky LayerSpec's (§8.4); absent: the layer's */;
  supply: { town: number; carried: number } /* S_town and S_carried at the last evaluation (V05 §3.3) */;
  field: { prosperity: number /* the island gradient's mean (V05 §3.11); a setAttr makes it a declared target the gradient is shifted to */; density: number; greenery: number };
  shadeBasis?: ShadeBasis         /* the island as the field's shade last read it (V05 §3.19; item 102 (e)); written only by a shade evaluation (V05 §3.19 (6)); ABSENT on an island raised or declared since the last evaluation, which casts and receives no strip until the next (J-V5.72) */;
  accesses: Id[]                  /* its SkyAccess records, in ordinal order */;
  name?: NameRef;
  state: 'floating'|'waning'|'settled'|'fallen'|'removed' /* 'removed': an author removed it (`convert removeIsland`, V05 §4.2): nothing drawn, its arena archived */; stateSince: { epoch: number; week: number }; dwellUntilWeek?: number /* waning and settled only: the week its dwell ends (V05 §3.15) */;
  removed?: { seq: number /* the removeIsland event */; generated: boolean /* its lifecycle cause was a rule (a slot draw, an epoch's raise), not a declaration — read by removedSlots(doc) to word the hold */; releasedBy?: number /* the seq of the T5 `sky` declaration that re-filled its slot when no slot was free; never an adjust('islands.count'); absent: the slot held closed (V05 §3.2 (6)) */ };
  removedAccesses: number[]       /* the ordinals of generated accesses an author deleted, held absent at every re-siting (V05 §3.10 (1)); [] by default */;
  landfall?: Id                   /* fallen only: the ground Decoration its fall laid (V05 §3.16) */;
  declared?: { outline?: true; position?: true; altitude?: true; area?: true; field?: true } /* the paths a declaring event holds (V05 §4.2; §7.2) */;
  lifecycle: Lifecycle }

interface UndersideProfile {      // the island's rocky root (J98: a shadow "related to their size, shape and the angle of the global light")
  kind: 'cone'|'bowl'|'root'|'flat' /* the radius ratio ρ(z) at depth fraction z ∈ [0, 1] below the rim's base: cone 1 − z; bowl √(1 − z²); root (1 − z)², a stalactite; flat: no root, the rim slab only */;
  rimThicknessCm: Cm             /* the cliff face at the rim: the rim's base lies this far below elevationCm */;
  depthCm: Cm                    /* the root's depth below the rim's base, to its apex; 0 for flat */;
  apexOffset: VecCm              /* local: where the apex hangs relative to the land face's centroid — a lopsided root; (0, 0) for flat */;
  tiers: number                  /* the root's slab tiers below underside tier 0 (the rim slab) (V05 §3.9); 0 for flat */;
  roughness: number              /* 0–1: the displacement share of each tier's ring (V05 §3.9) */;
  source: 'rule'|'edit' }         /* 'edit' once a setAttr set any field: then held by resizes, which scale depthCm only by the area's square-root ratio */

interface ShadeBasis {           // the one sky input a field recomputation reads (§2.9) — a copy, never a vertex (§2.2 rule 5's precedent: Works.before)
  epoch: number; by: 'generation'|'rederive'|'epoch'|'update' /* the evaluation that wrote it; 'update': Update shade now (V05 §4.2), which writes the document's current epoch */;
  anchor: PointCm; elevationCm: Cm; groundRefCm: Cm;
  surfaceTopCm: Cm               /* underside tier 0's top as that evaluation read it (V05 §3.9 (4)): the terrain's surface at generation and at a re-derivation, the full surface top at an epoch and at an update */;
  underside: UndersideProfile    /* a copy */;
  rim: PointCm[]                 /* the rim chain's lattice points in the island's local frame, in chain order from its lowest-(y, x) vertex, ≤ `shade.basisVertexCap`: a longer chain is reduced by the kernel's simplification at a 1 m tolerance (§3.4), which moves no strip bit at the 32 m cell (T-V5-20) */ }
```

**Local coordinates, one world anchor** (J-V5.1). An island's arena is laid in its own local frame centred on (0, 0)
(V05 §3.8), and the island stands over the ground plan by one integer translation, `anchor`. A move is one attribute
(V05 §4.2); nothing on the island is re-snapped, re-keyed or re-derived by it. Every reader that meets the ground
converts by that translation alone — S14 (marks and label candidates), the slab list, exports, placement and the landing
points — and every logged position on the island is local, so a later move never invalidates it. No rotation: an
integer translation keeps every lattice point a lattice point (§3.2 (7)); a turned island is a reshaped one (a `warp`
on its key).

**`removedSlots(doc)`** (`@plat/sky`, draw-free) — `{ island: Id; slot: 1|2|3; name?: NameRef; seq: number; generated:
boolean; accesses: number[] }[]`: every island record in state `removed` whose slot is still held, and every island's
`removedAccesses`, in slot order. Derived, never stored; its readers are V05 §1b's. `generated` words the hold: "the
town won't raise this island again" for a generated island, "your island stays removed" for a declared one.

**`Document.nextSkyPart: number`** (§2.9) — the part the next island takes (1 at creation), never decreasing; an event
that creates an island records the part in the island's address, so a replay reads it there. The slot (1–3) is the
count law's; the part is identity. A fallen island's part stays spent (J-V5.2).

### 2.2 The access record

```ts
interface SkyAccess {
  id: Id                          /* 'sky<part>/skyaccess:<token>' — keyed in its island's arena, where its top vertex lies */;
  island: Id; kind: 'hoist'|'ropeway'|'stair'|'lightBridge';
  top: VertexRef                  /* ('sky_access', access id): its vertex on the island's rim chain (a vertex role, §2.2 rule 7) */;
  topLanding: Id                  /* the island-side landing Reservation, kind 'landing' (V05 §3.10) */;
  ground: { at: PointCm /* world: the foot, a point attribute (§2.2 rule 5) carried with its host under a warp (§7.4) */; plot?: Id /* the ground landing plot, once placed */ };
  role: 'main'|'service'|'guarded';
  state: 'open'|'closed'          /* stored: a hand closure only; the readings `unserved` (island waning past half its dwell, or settled) and `ruined` (island fallen) are derived, never stored */;
  knownTo: 'public'|'local'|'secret'; rule: string /* the SkyAccessRow it took */; lifecycle: Lifecycle }
```

**No access is a way** (J-V5.61). A `SkyAccess` is never a way, an arena edge between arenas or a portal, and it carries
no deck plots; a raised way never reaches an island (V05 §3.22 (3)). Its foot stands on ground part-0 land only — never
on a deck face, a raised-way pier or under a raised deck (V05 §3.10 (4); J-V5.60).

Derived at read, never stored: the run (`hoist` and `stair`: the vertical drop from the rim base to the foot's ground;
`ropeway` and `lightBridge`: the 3-D length from the top to the foot), the slope, and the foot's ground height. A row's
`maxRunM` limits the **horizontal** distance from the top's world point to the foot, for both slanted kinds (V05 §2.4).
A `stair`'s path is a helix of radius `stair.radiusM` about the vertical through its foot, from the foot's ground to the
rim base, rising `stair.stepM` per step with a landing every `stair.landingM` of rise, its last turn ending at the top;
the marks and the glTF steps are laid along it (V05 §5.1, §5.5). The ground landing of a `secret` access carries the
access's `knownTo`: its `sky_landing` use is fronted by item 63's `front` mode (a warehouse or a stable, the
`sky_landing` row's front column) and withheld from every key that does not admit it (§9.2's filter), as the access
itself is.

### 2.3 The island's unit inputs (a derived view; never persisted)

```ts
// @plat/sky exports skyUnitInput(doc, island): SkyUnitInput — draw-free, assembled from committed state; its one caller is
// volume 1's unit runner (V01 §3.10.3) when the NeighbourhoodRequest it serves has layer 'sky'
interface SkyUnitInput {
  island: Id; key: PlatKey; land: FaceRef /* L0 */; rimStrip: FaceRef /* L1, never cut, never plotted */;
  claims: Id[]                    /* the institution's Reservation (an institution island) and every landing Reservation — holes the fabric cuts around */;
  landings: { reservation: Id; attach: VertexRef /* ('attach', reservation, 0): the vertex a way must end at */; access: Id; role: SkyAccess['role'] }[];
  river?: Id                      /* its crossing sites are §4.2 S1 (5)'s on the island (V05 §3.8), qualified and spanned by the unit run (V01 §3.10.3 (4)) */;
  allocation: Id                  /* the S7b (5) sky Allocation the unit runner splits over its districts (V01 §3.10.3 (4)) */;
  field: { raster: { cellCm: number /* 3 200 */; origin: PointCm /* local */; w: number; h: number; values: Float32Array /* the island's prosperity before the brush strokes, V05 §3.11 */ };
    mean: number; density: number; greenery: number } /* what the unit runner's field step reads — never recomputed from h(S_carried) */;
  context: { reason: SkyIsland['reason']; institutionKind?: string;
    districtKind: string /* 'sky-island' on a prosperity island's faces; the institution row's `districtKind` on the claim's face (V05 §2.4) */;
    districtHa: number /* IslandRule.districtHa: the land per island district volume 1's count reads (V05 §3.12) */;
    fabricHint: FabricStrategyId /* a PRIOR for S7c on every island district: the hinted template's weight × fabricPrior, never a forced strategy */;
    fabricPrior: number /* IslandRule.fabricPrior */ } }
```

SKY builds, per island, the `NeighbourhoodRequest` of V01 §2.4 — `district` the island's sky pseudo-district, `key` its
arena, `layer: 'sky'`, `boundary` the land face's runs (every rim run a hard `sky` seam, no portal), `laid` empty (so
the mode is `unit`), `strategy` absent (S7c picks it per island district, reading `fabricHint` as a prior) — and calls
`NeighbourhoodStrategy.generate`; the unit runner reads `skyUnitInput(doc, island)` for everything the request does not
carry. The view is re-assembled from the committed record, arena and allocation whenever the unit runner runs
(generation, `raiseIsland`, New random on the island; V01 §3.10.3 (6)). **The partition is volume 1's** (J-V5.28): the
island's district count and its cut are V01 §3.10.3 (2)'s, with the island's own district size — k is
`skyDistrictCount(plotLandM2, districtHa)` (V01 §3.16), its share `hood.constants.sky.plotShare` (V01 §3.14), never a
copy here (J-V5.45).

### 2.3a The shade strip (derived with the prosperity field; item 102)

```ts
// @plat/sky exports islandShadeStrip(doc, island, samples, onto): IslandShadeStrip — pure and draw-free (V05 §3.19)
interface IslandShadeStrip {
  island: Id                      /* the caster */;
  onto: 'ground' | Id             /* the receiver: the ground's field raster, or a lower island's local 32 m lattice (V05 §3.11) */;
  basis: Hash64                   /* §6.2's hash of the caster's `shadeBasis` (and, onto an island, the receiver's), the sampler's digest and the receiver heights' version under the window — the UNLEVELLED heights (§4.7; J-V5.65) — the strip reads nothing of the live island */;
  evaluated: { epoch: number; by: 'generation'|'rederive'|'epoch'|'update' } /* the evaluation that computed it; read by the clerk ("the ground last took this shade at …") and T-V5-21 */;
  window: { i0: number; j0: number; w: number; h: number } /* in the receiver's 32 m cells */;
  bits: number                    /* per cell: the sampler's sample points × its sun positions (§4.5) */;
  mask: Uint8Array                /* w × h cells, row-major, ⌈bits ÷ 8⌉ bytes each; bit (point · suns + sun) set where the ray from that point toward that sun meets this island's solid — §4.5's layout, which volume 6's building shade mask shares (V06 §3.4) */;
  cells: number                   /* cells with any bit set — the "at least one sample" area the clerk and the CSV read */ }
```

**Where it lives.** The strips of every live island onto the ground are held with the prosperity field's rasters
(§4.5), beside the daylight term's per-sample mask they are OR-ed into, and hashed exactly as those rasters are (§6.2);
the strips onto an island are held with that island's field values (V05 §3.11). Its readers are V05 §1b's; nothing edits
one (J-V5.30). **A strip is a pure function of its caster's `shadeBasis`, the receiver (the ground's heights as the
field reads them — S1's raster with the T2 terrain strokes, unlevelled, §4.7 — or the receiving island's `shadeBasis` and
terrain) and the sampler**, never of the live island, so the load path, a replay and every field recomputation rebuild
the same bits (§2.9); only the shade view's pending outline and the clerk's "has the ground caught up" read the live
island (V05 §3.19 (8)). An island without a `shadeBasis` casts no strip and receives none. When strips are computed is
V05 §3.19 (6)'s one schedule.

### 2.3b The access obstacles (for item 110's raised ways)

```ts
// @plat/sky exports skyAccessObstacles(doc): SkyAccessObstacle[] — pure and draw-free, from the committed accesses; world cm above the ground datum
type SkyAccessObstacle =
  | { kind: 'column'; access: Id; at: PointCm /* the foot (a hoist's: its landing yard's centre) */; radiusCm: Cm /* a hoist: the yard's half-diagonal; a stair: stair.radiusM + 1 m */;
      floorCm: Cm /* the foot's ground */; topCm: Cm /* the rim base, elevationCm − rimThicknessCm */ }
  | { kind: 'segment'; access: Id; a: Point3Cm /* the top's world point at the rim base */; b: Point3Cm /* the foot at its ground height */;
      clearCm: Cm /* IslandRule.accessSiting.lineClearM */ };
```

One entry per open or `unserved` access of every island that is not fallen or removed: a `hoist` or `stair` a column (its
cage or its helix rises through open air), a `ropeway` or `lightBridge` a segment. Its callers (V05 §3.22 (3); §4.3)
keep a deck, its parapet and its raised-way piers outside every column and a deck crossing under a segment `clearCm`
below it. The list is rebuilt from committed state on every call; nothing stores it. **Its size** is the document's
live access count: the generated accesses are at most `accessCount.max` per island (≤ 9 per document); hand-placed
accesses (`placeSkyAccess`) have no cap of their own, each adding one entry, their feet kept `footGapM` apart and their
tops distinct rim vertices (V05 §3.10 (4); J-V5.84). A caller tests a candidate span against the list in O(entries)
(V05 §6).

### 2.4 The rule tables (data, editable by `editTable`, §7.6; every cell `{value, unit, provenance, calibratable}`)

`IslandRule` — one row per rung that admits islands, in `@plat/tables` (`islands.json`, registry row 56):

```ts
interface IslandRule {
  rung: 'CITY'|'METROPOLIS';
  chance: [number, number, number]                    /* II.6 (4)'s slot chances */;
  townSupply: { v: number; h: number }[]               /* S_town → h_town, piecewise linear on the absolute value (V05 §3.3) */;
  carriedSupply: { v: number; h: number }[]            /* S_carried → h_carried, likewise */;
  areaHaMax: number; minAreaHa: number; altitudeM: [number, number] /* E_min, E_max */; clearanceM: number;
  casterAllowanceM: number                             /* the caster ceiling where no taller caster is known at SKY (V05 §3.9 (3)) */;
  surfaceTopPercentile: number                         /* the island's surface top: this percentile, over the land face, of its terrain plus eaves (V05 §3.9 (4)) */;
  shade: { ringMaskM: number; stepM: number; basisVertexCap: number } /* the strip's tier-ring masks, its ray step, the basis rim's vertex cap (V05 §3.19, §2.1) */;
  routing: { transferM: number }                       /* the route distance's allowance per access ridden (V05 §3.20) */;
  underside: { kinds: { kind: UndersideProfile['kind']; weight: number }[]; rimThicknessM: number; depthPerRadius: number; maxDepthM: number; apexOffsetShare: number; tiers: number; roughness: number };
  placement: { prefs: { over: 'water'|'fields'|'outskirts'|'core'; weight: number }[]; gapM: number; frameInsetM: number; nearM: number; latticeM: number; topK: number };
  outline: { templates: { template: 'disc'|'d_shape'|'lozenge'|'crescent'|'spindle'; weight: number }[]; detail: string /* a detail row of §4.2 S1 (2) */ };
  frameMarginM: number                                 /* the island arena's frame margin beyond its grown outline (V05 §3.8 (1)) */;
  terrain: 'none'|'echo'|'own'; echo: { amplitudeShare: number; domeM: number; domePerRadius: number; tiltPerMille: number };
  river: { chance: number; minAreaHa: number; flowShare: number; widthM: [number, number]; widthMinorShare: number; springPondChance: number;
    sourceShare: number /* the source's place on the long axis, from its upstream end */; pondShare: number /* the spring pond's area ÷ A */ };
  institutions: IslandInstitution[]; access: SkyAccessRow[];
  accessCount: { perHa: number; max: number }          /* a prosperity island's generated accesses: min(max, 1 + ⌊areaHa ÷ perHa⌋) (V05 §3.10 (1)) */;
  accessSiting: { footGapM: number; turnStepCdeg: number; turnMaxCdeg: number; deckGapM: number; lineClearM: number } /* feet apart; F-SKY-ACCESS's direction steps; a foot's and a yard's gap to a raised deck's plan; a slanted access's clearance over a raised deck or raised-way pier (V05 §3.10 (4), §3.17, §3.22 (3)) */;
  stair: { radiusM: number; stepM: number; landingM: number } /* the floating stair's helix (V05 §2.2) */;
  landing: { yardWidthM: number; yardDepthM: number; rimStripM: number; wayReachM: number };
  fill: { batchShare: number; householdCapDistricts: number; coverageOvershoot: number } /* V05 §3.5 (2)–(3) */;
  institutionFit: { fitFactor: number; areaCapShare: number; prisonMarginM: number; siteLatticeM: number } /* V05 §3.4 (2), §3.5, §3.10 (5) */;
  cryptFloorM: number                                  /* an island crypt's floor above the rim slab's base (V05 §3.10 (5)) */;
  districtHa: number                                   /* the land per island district volume 1's count reads (V05 §3.12) */;
  fabricPrior: number; fabricRingHa: number            /* S7c's prior on the hinted template; a prosperity island's hint is `ring` from this area, else `bent-cut` (V05 §3.12) */;
  field: { epsilonShare: number; epsilonWavelengthM: number; greeneryBonus: number; rank: { elevation: number; view: number }; bandInset: number } /* V05 §3.11 */;
  carry: CarryRule                                     /* what the sky fill never carries, carries by share, or may split across arenas (V05 §3.5 (3)) */;
  node: { bonus: number }                              /* the magical node's term added to h_town (V05 §3.3); its value's one home is this row */;
  hysteresis: number; dwellYears: { waning: [number, number]; settled: [number, number] }; growthPerYear: number; altitudeRateMPerYear: number;
  redrawPerYear: number                                /* II.6 (4)'s per-year redraw rate */;
  landfall: { coverShare: number; stoneM3PerM2: number }; landingKernel: { weight: number; radiusM: number };
  naming: { lowBelowM: number; highFromM: number }     /* the altitude classes S13a reads (V05 §3.14) */;
  export: { gltfRingPoints: number; mistM: number }    /* V05 §5.5 */ }
interface CarryRule {                                  // item 23: a default, never a law — every list is editable
  never: { balance: string[] /* item 107 (a)'s balance profiles */; emitsTopStep: ('smell'|'smoke'|'waterPollution')[] /* item 103 (a)'s EMITS at the profile scale's top step */;
    chainRoles: ('resource'|'processing')[] /* of an active chain — custom (item 104 (b)) or the dossier's activeChains[] (item 105 (i)) — the island's own institution excepted */;
    hardNeeds: string[] /* a hard NEEDS row naming one of these features */ };
  share: { balance: string[]; splitShop: boolean /* the shop part of an item 105 (h) split class may go up by share, its works staying on the ground */ } }
interface IslandInstitution {
  kind: string /* palace, cathedral, prison … data */; tags: string[] /* the roster class tags it takes, in preference order */; requirementBand: string; areaHa: number;
  staffHouseholds: number; weight: number; requires: { government?: string[]; magic?: ('low'|'high')[] /* the `magic` circumstance the row needs */ };
  districtKind: string /* a `sky-*` district kind */; fabricHint: FabricStrategyId /* S7c's prior (V05 §3.12) */;
  archetypes?: { id: string /* a SettlementForge scene archetype, item 104 (a) (2) */; landmarkOnly: boolean }[] /* a custom institution whose resolved profile carries one counts as an instance of this row (V05 §3.4) */;
  handover: { tags: string[] /* who takes an S3 reservation this row's instance leaves on the ground: the first tag the roster demands on the ground, its largest instance */; else: 'park'|'none' } /* V05 §3.5 */;
  accesses: ('main'|'service'|'guarded')[];
  near?: 'plaza'|'precinct'|'richest' /* the placement's purpose term (V05 §3.7 (3) (c)): to the plaza; to the precinct where S3 reserved one, else the plaza; to the district of highest prosperity mean */;
  far?: 'plaza'; nearM: number; overWeights?: Partial<Record<'water'|'fields'|'outskirts'|'core', number>> }
interface SkyAccessRow { kind: SkyAccess['kind']; magic: ('none'|'low'|'high')[]; weight: number; maxAltitudeM?: number; maxRunM?: number /* the horizontal top-to-foot distance (V05 §2.2) */; needsLandBelow: boolean; extension: true }
```

**Default rows** — our own values (provenance `own`, `extension`: no reference has islands, II.6 (8)), each
calibratable; the three marked ⟨P0⟩ are set by M6-sky's P0 run before the table lands, with the measurement and
fallback of V05 §3.3 and §3.9 (5), and their provenance notes name that run. Every calibratable cell takes its register
entry from V12 §3.11's default line (V05 §8; J-V5.80):

| `palace` | `palace` | `rich` | 3.0 ha | 40 | 0.35 | government `crown` or `seigneurial` | `sky-palace` / `street` (an axial avenue from the landing to the forecourt) | main, service | near the plaza, 1 000 m | `martial-keep`, landmark only | `town_hall`, `guildhall`; else `park` (the palace gardens) |
| `cathedral` | `cathedral`, `great_temple` | `prosperous` | 1.5 ha | 20 | 0.25 | — | `sky-temple` / `landmark` | main, service | near the precinct where one is reserved, else the plaza, 800 m | `sacred-sanctuary`, landmark only | `church`, `temple`, `chapel`; else `park` |
| `prison` | `prison` | `prosperous` | 1.2 ha | 15 | 0.20 | — | `sky-prison` / `square` | guarded | far from the plaza (≥ 1 000 m); water 1.5 | — | none (sited by S10b; no S3 reservation) |
| `tower` | `mage_tower` (its lowest-ordinal instance), `mages_guild` | `prosperous` | 0.6 ha | 4 | 0.20 | `magic` low or high | `sky-tower` / `square` | main | far from the plaza (≥ 600 m); fields 1.2 | `exotic-tower`, any level | none (sited by S10b) |

The `tower` row is the owner's "unique institution" read for an arcane tower "on its own ground, apart" (item 104 (a)
(2)'s wording for that archetype): it takes a catalogue instance of `mage_tower` (the lowest roster ordinal that is not a
member of a collective record, item 108) or the town's `mages_guild`, or a custom kind of that archetype, and only where
the world has magic (J-V5.31). The cathedral row's second tag is the great temple of a town whose religion row names one
(volume 11's `great_temple`, "as cathedral"), so a non-default religion's seat can float as the cathedral does
(J-V5.58). A prison's band is its paymaster's: the town's rulers keep it aloft, so it reads `prosperous`, not its
inmates' band (J-V5.24). A hand-over tag is taken by the **largest** instance of that class the roster demands on the
ground — the greatest planned plot area at its band (§4.2 S2), ties by roster ordinal — so the cathedral's close passes
to the town's greatest parish church, which becomes the mother church in the old close (V05 §3.5; J-V5.43).

| access row | magic | weight | limits | land below |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `hoist` | none, low, high | 1.0 | altitude ≤ 400 m | yes |
| `ropeway` | none, low, high | 0.8 | horizontal run ≤ 350 m | no (chosen where the ground below the rim is water) |
| `stair` | low, high | 0.3 at `low`, 0.4 at `high` | altitude ≤ 120 m | yes |
| `lightBridge` | high | 0.5 | horizontal run ≤ 600 m | no |

**The landing classes** (facts this volume fixes; their full class rows are volume 11's, compiled from these facts, V11 §3.5.13, §10.2's schema): `sky_landing` on
the ground — a yard plot of 150–400 m² fronting a way (or reached by a trail), one building by kind: a two-storey hoist
house under a gantry (6 × 8 m), a rope-line tower (6 × 6 m, 18 m), a stair's foot arch, a light bridge's plinth; staffed
by two `modest` households, allocated to its host district or the hinterland (V05 §3.10). `sky_landing_top` on the
island — the landing yard's plot on the rim with its winch house and crane arm (8 × 10 m), rope-line tower, stair
terrace or bridge pylon; no households of its own. **District kinds:** `sky-island` for a prosperity island's
districts; `sky-palace`, `sky-temple`, `sky-prison` and `sky-tower` from the institution rows on the claim's face (data
rows of §10.2's district-kind table, keyed apart from every ground kind, so the table has one row per key; J-V5.48).
Volume 1's unit runner types the island's faces with these kinds (V05 §3.12); each row carries the affinities of its
ground counterpart for S7b's split and S7c (`sky-island` those of the town's richest ground kind), rows volume 11's.

**The landing classes' siting facts** (item 103 (a)'s profile columns; the rows volume 11 writes must carry them):
`sky_landing` — sited by its access, never by a pull (its allocation row is positioned, V05 §3.10 (4)), so its NEEDS row
is satisfied by construction (a way frontage or a trail); EMITS heavy traffic at the middle of the profile scale and
noise at its lowest non-zero step (the winch, the carts at the yard), so the classes SENSITIVE-TO those keep a little
away; CLIENTELE town-wide; SUPPLY: none of its own — it is the island's gate for the route distance (V05 §3.20);
ALIGNMENT: neutral, except a `guarded` landing, which takes its prison's law-and-enforcement alignment, so crime keeps
its distance from a prison's hoist; COVERAGE: none. `sky_landing_top` — the same facts on the island arena.

### 2.5 Identity

| kind | lineage unit and creation index | address | anchor (§6.3) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `SkyIsland` | the town; its slot at generation; at an epoch the town, the slot and the epoch component (J-A8.61); a declared island its declaring event's `seq` | `sky<part>/island:…` | its slot (base) |
| its `Terrain`, its river, its arena's L0 and L1 pieces | the island's `SKY` run, by §6.3's canonical order | `sky<part>/…` | the base rows of their kinds |
| `SkyAccess` | the island; its ordinal in siting order (V05 §3.10); a hand-placed one its event's `seq` | `sky<part>/skyaccess:…` | (its island's address, its role, its ordinal); same island (§6.3) |
| landing `Reservation` (island side) | the island; the access's ordinal | `sky<part>/reservation:…` | its access's address |
| ground landing plot | an ordinary ground plot (its block); its use `sky_landing` names the access | ground | the plot's base anchor |
| landfall `Decoration` | the fallen island (its address) | ground | its island's address |

The unit run's districts, blocks, plots and buildings on the island are ordinary entities of the island's arena under
§6.3's rules, their lineage continuing the island's (volume 1).

### 2.6 The lifecycle of every state field this volume adds or writes

One row per field: its producer, its readers, persistence, regeneration, undo and migration. For a field another unit
owns and this volume only writes, the row gives this volume's writer and cites the owner's row for the rest.

| field | create | read | persist | regenerate | undo | migrate |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `slot` | S7b (5)'s slot draw; a declaration takes the lowest free slot, or the lowest held slot when none is free (V05 §3.2 (6)) | the epoch redraw (V05 §3.15 (5)), `raiseIsland`, the anchor match, `removedSlots(doc)` | model digest | kept by succession (slot anchor); a slot a `removed` record holds is never drawn or filled (V05 §3.2 (6)) | restored from the undo record | — (new) |
| `key` / `nextSkyPart` | island creation takes `nextSkyPart` and bumps it | every island reader; `PlatKey.part`, the `sky<n>/` prefix | model digest; the counter in the document | a successor keeps its predecessor's key (§6.3) | the counter never decreases (spent) | older documents: `nextSkyPart` = 1 + the highest sky part present, 1 if none (§7.5) |
| `anchor` | placement (V05 §3.7) or a declaration; `setAttr` (a move) | S14 (marks, label candidates), the slab list, exports, the landing points, placement's gap test, `skyAccessObstacles`, the pointer mapping (V05 §4.2) | model digest | kept by a scoped regeneration; recomputed only by Re-derive from S7b | inverse `setAttr` | a record with the base's world `outline` takes as anchor the outline's area centroid rounded to the lattice, its polygon less the anchor becoming the rim (below), so the local frame centres on the island |
| `rim`, `land` | SKY's L0 batch (§2.2's sky levels) | the unit run (the land face, through `SkyUnitInput`), the rim draw, exports, the resize and reshape (V05 §4.2), the rim strip, `undersideTiers`, `ShadeBasis.rim` | geometry (the arena) | re-derived by New random on the island; kept by every other regeneration | the batch's undo record | the base's `outline: Polygon` becomes the rim chain by one `insertChain` in a fresh arena |
| `areaHa` | the supply law or an edit | the resize, the fill, the reader, the invariant (± 1 %) | model digest | re-evaluated only at an epoch | inverse | — |
| `altitudeCm`, `groundRefCm` | V05 §3.9 at SKY (the caster ceiling), before field pass 2; SKY-F's clearance check; `reclearIsland` (V05 §3.15 (7)); `setAttr(altitudeCm)`; a move; V05 §4.3's ground edits beneath | `elevationCm` (the island terrain's datum; the `z` base of every sky mark), the slab list, marks, access runs, `skyAccessObstacles`' column tops, `ShadeBasis`, the clearance invariant | model digest | kept; a raise of `groundRefCm` keeps `elevationCm` and sets `altitudeCm` = `elevationCm` − the new `groundRefCm`, raising `elevationCm` only where V05 §3.9 (4)'s floor then fails (a listed fate); New random re-checks the floor after re-drawing the underside (V05 §3.9 (5), §4.2) | inverse | base `elevationCm` → `altitudeCm` = `elevationCm` − `groundRefCm` recomputed |
| `underside` | SKY (V05 §3.9 (1)) from the rule row and `island.place`; `setAttr(underside.*)` | `undersideTiers` (the slab tiers, the offset view, glTF), the strips' smooth tiers through `ShadeBasis`, the altitude floor, the crypt test's rim slab, `skyAccessObstacles`' rim base | model digest | re-scaled by a resize; re-drawn by New random unless `source: 'edit'` | inverse | a base record without it gets the rule's default profile, drawn on its slot's key; a `flat` profile carries `depthCm` 0 and `tiers` 0 |
| `terrain`, `river` | SKY (V05 §3.8) | the unit run's context vector, the island field's ranks, the surface top, `levelledGroundCm`'s fallback on the sky key, the waterfall mark, the sky surface layer, exports | model digest (the raster in the arena's chunk) | New random on the island re-derives; T2 edits with the island's key | inverse | — |

## 3 Generation

### 3.1 Where the sky runs in the stage graph

| step | when (§4.1's graph) | worker | writes |
|---|---|---|---|
| **S7b (5)** — gate, slots, reasons, fills, outline and placement per slot (V05 §3.2–§3.7) | inside S7b, before S7b (6) and (1) | generation, eager, town-wide | the sky `Allocation`s; data only (no batch) |
| **SKY** — `SkyStrategy.place` (II.6), per island in slot order: the arena (V05 §3.8), the underside, the ground reference and the altitude (V05 §3.9 (1)–(4)), the access tops and feet, the island's claims, the ground landing claims (V05 §3.10), the field (V05 §3.11); then, once every island has its solid, its **shade evaluation** (V05 §3.19 (6)) | after S7b (its S7c hook included, V01 §3.1) and before S12a, S8 and S10a, by its declared I/O (below); S7c reads none of SKY's outputs — a landing claim is a hole S8 cuts around (V01 §3.4.1) | generation, eager | on each island's arena one L0 batch, then one L1 batch; on the ground one L1 batch of landing claims (`claimSubFace`, as S7b (3)'s claims) |
| **S10a — the strips onto the ground, and field pass 2** (§4.5) — computed once per shade evaluation at their first read, held, and OR-ed by pass 2 into the daylight term's mask (V05 §3.19 (6)–(7)) | S10a, after SKY and before the pool | generation, eager, town-wide | the strips, held with the field's rasters (V05 §2.3a); at a re-derivation starting after SKY and at or before S10a, first the bases and the strips onto islands (V05 §3.19 (6)) |
| **the unit run** (volume 1, V01 §3.10.3), one per island | after the ground's S10 (0); concurrent with the pool, on its own overlay, merged in lineage order like the hinterland unit (§4.1) | generation | only its island's arena |
| **SKY-F** — the clearance check (V05 §3.9 (5)), the landing check and repair and the crypt re-test (V05 §3.12), the sky lights (V05 §3.13) | after the pool's merge, before S12c and S13a | generation, eager | island records; `LightSource` records; at most one L1 repair batch per island |
| S12c, S13a, S14 | as the base; S12c gives the districts on a sky island's arena their character and identity (§4.5) | — | the island's names and labels (V05 §3.14); its marks (V05 §5) |

**Declared I/O** (§4.1; J-V5.70). SKY's `StageDecl` reads S7b's outputs — with S7c's, since §4.12's hooks are united
into their host's declared I/O (V01 §3.1) — and writes on the ground only its two data keys, `reservations.landing` (the
ground landing yards, whose L1 claims are those reservations' geometry) and `allocations.landing` (the positioned
`sky_landing` rows, V05 §3.10 (4)); on each island it writes that island's arena, records and field. No stage or hook
before SKY declares either landing key (V01 §3.1's hook table holds S7c to S3's and S7b (3)'s reservations); S8, S10a
with its hook S9b, and S12a declare both, so §4.1's sort puts SKY after S7b and before S8, S10a and S12a, with no cycle
(T-V5-32). SKY rewrites no row S7b wrote: a landing's two staff households belong to the landing row, and S10a takes them
from the host district's `modest` residential demand when it reads the two keys together (§2.5). SKY is one of the
ground's L1 writers in §4.1's order. Nothing of the sky runs on the one-click path: with `recipe.layers.islands` off
(D-3) S7b (5) makes no allocation and SKY registers but writes nothing under either key — the ground model is
byte-identical to the realism golden (T13-1).

**Item 110's raised ways in the order** (J-V5.63). Every generated raised part is committed before S7b (1) — §4.3's
slot, a viaduct inside S4's route solve, an elevated road or an aqueduct at S6's end — so it exists when S7b (5) places
the islands and when SKY fixes the ground reference, and it reads no island (the generated raised-way set is the same
with islands on or off, T-V5-30). A raised part committed after SKY — by an epoch's raised-way pass, a new stage's S4 in
an epoch, or a T4 stroke — is read by the epoch's clearance re-check (V05 §3.15 (7)) or by the adding edit's dry run
(V05 §4.3), and its course keeps clear of the accesses through `skyAccessObstacles` (V05 §2.3b); SKY-F's clearance check
reads whatever stands under the solid after the pool's merge (V05 §3.9 (5)).

### 3.2 Existence and count

1. **The gate.** Islands exist only if all hold: `recipe.layers.islands = 'on'`; the rung admits them
   (`RungProfile.layers.islands`, §1.1); the recipe is not a parity preset's (`MFCG` and `FTG` hold `off`, J-V5.25);
   and, for a dossier-bound document, the circumstance `magic` that DR27 derives from `site.magic` is not `none`
   (V05 §4.4). A failed gate makes no allocation and reports `islands: off`, `gated: rung` or `gated: dossier magic`.
2. **The town supply** `S_town` and `h_town` (V05 §3.3).
3. **Forced slots.** Every declared island of the creation prefix (T5 `quarter` with `sky`) and every assignment whose
   criteria carry `sky: true` (V05 §4.2) forces the lowest free slot open, in log order. A fourth is bent at its dry run
   to join the nearest open island as a district on it (`bend.island.fourth`, V05 §4.2; J-VI.4 (a)).
4. **The count.** `recipe.params['islands.count']` — `auto` (default), or 0–3, the `adjust` of V05 §4.2. At an integer
   `n`, exactly `n` slots are open (the forced ones counted first, a slot held by a removal counted as one of them, (6);
   `n` below the forced count is bent up to it at its dry run, `bend.island.count`), with no draw. At `auto`, each slot k
   above the forced ones, in order, opens with probability `chance[k] · h_town` and only if slot k − 1 is open: one
   uniform on `island.place` keyed by the town's lineage with creation index `slot:k` (V05 §3.18).
5. The report lists the count, the forced slots and each slot's outcome (V05 §3.4–§3.7), a held slot's outcome being
   `removed by you` (6). With the setting on, the gate passed and no island present, the report carries one plain
   sentence for the author and the clerk — "Islands are on, but the city is not prosperous enough to keep one aloft" when
   `h_town` is 0 or every slot closed `no supply`, "… none rose this time" when the slot draws came up empty — and names
   both remedies: raise the town's wealth (T1 `intend`), or place an island by hand (T5 with `sky`, held against its
   supply, J-V5.10).
6. **Removed slots** — the island form of §2.8's suppression (J-V5.62; E-02 keeps the two forms apart). A slot held by an
   island record in state `removed` whose `removed.releasedBy` is absent (`convert removeIsland`, V05 §4.2 — a generated
   or a declared island alike) is **closed with the outcome `removed by you`**: no draw on its `slot:k` or `reason:k`
   index, no fill, no outline, no placement, no SKY run. It **counts as open and filled** — for the slot chain of (4),
   so a higher slot draws on its own creation index exactly as before the removal, and toward an integer
   `islands.count`, so the removal is never replaced by an island in another slot (the owner removed that island, not the
   town's quota of islands). It is released by §2.8's one lift rule alone: the removal's undo, or a T5 `sky` declaration
   when no slot is free — the user's re-add serving that key, which takes the lowest held slot and sets
   `removed.releasedBy`. An integer `islands.count` counts every held slot as filled and opens only free slots, and
   `auto` never draws a held slot; an adjust whose integer exceeds the slots it can open opens what it can and lists the
   fate "slot k is held by your removal of <name>; undo the removal or draw an island to use it". The removed island's
   households were relocated by its removal, so S7b (1) allocates them to the ground at every re-derivation and the
   population stays whole (T-V5-29).

### 3.3 The two supplies, on absolute values

A mean over the town compared with its own per-town quantile measures the distribution's shape, not the town's wealth,
and never reaches the field's top (RV-pc-3, `docs/retrovalidation/pipeline-content.md`); both supplies are therefore
read on the field's **absolute** value, which the dials, `wealthLevel`'s character offset (§7.7 layer 1) and the epochs'
drivers move (J-V5.3):

* **`S_town`** — the mean prosperity value `P` over the settlement's built land: the cells of §4.5's 32 m field raster
  whose centres lie in a district face (the hinterland, underground, undercity and sky pseudo-districts excluded), less
  the cells in water and in reservations of kind `park`, `harbour_basin`, `moat` or `canal`; at S7b the value after field
  pass 1, at an epoch the island-free value after that epoch's re-evaluation (V05 §3.19 (7)).
* **`S_carried`** — the mean, over the households an island carries, of their band's value `v(b)`: the midpoint of the
  band's two cuts on the absolute value (`FieldBands.cuts`), the lowest band's lower cut 0 and the top band's upper cut 1.
  An institution island's `S_carried` is `v(requirementBand)` of its row.
* `h_town(S_town)` and `h_carried(S_carried)` interpolate the rule's `townSupply` and `carriedSupply` rows linearly along
  the value, 0 below the first row and 1 above the last. The **supported area** `A` and the **law's altitude** `E` are
  II.6 (5)'s.
* **The magical node** (J-V5.41). Where the dossier digest's `site.deposits` carries a `magical_node` in state
  `available` — or `discovered`, from the epoch after the one that discovered it — `h_town` is `min(1, the interpolated
  value + node.bonus)`. It is one addend however many such deposits the dossier lists; `depleted` and `removed` add
  nothing. It acts only where the interpolated value is above 0 — the owner's "if there is no prosperity, there is no
  floating islands" — and reaches every reader of `h_town` (the slots, `A`, `E`, the epochs). A map without a dossier has
  no deposits and no node term; the report and the clerk's `why` name the deposit when it acts. **Where the node stands
  is not this volume's:** item 109 (f) puts it at the heart of an underground ruin where one exists (its position volume
  4's and volume 8's), and the term stays positionless: it reads the deposit's state alone, never the underground arena
  (item 2). The clerk names "the node beneath the old ruins" from the report's `ruin.node` line (V04 §3.5.0a (9): the
  deposit, the ruin system and the ruin's `NameRef`, so a renamed ruin reads its new name), never by reading the ruin;
  with no such line it names the deposit alone.

**Calibration ⟨P0⟩** (V05 §8). On 200 CITY and 100 METROPOLIS seeds at their targets, at `wealthLevel` ∈ {0, 0.25, 0.5,
0.75, 1} with the setting on, record `S_town`'s median `m_w` per level; set `townSupply` to (m_0.25, 0) (m_0.5, 0.5)
(m_1, 1). Bars: the median `h_town` at `wealthLevel` 0 ≤ 0.05, at 0.5 within [0.35, 0.65], at 1 ≥ 0.9, all on
standalone intents, so with no node term. **Pre-decided fallback:** if `m_1 − m_0` < 0.10 (the field does not tell a
rich town from a poor one), `S_town` becomes `0.5 · (the field mean) + 0.5 · w`, `w` the intent's `wealthLevel` (with a
dossier its derived value, DR6), the rows are re-fitted by the same rule, and the change is recorded as a declared shift.
`carriedSupply` is checked at the same run: `h_carried(v(rich))` ≥ 0.7 and `h_carried(v(modest))` = 0 at `wealthLevel`
0.5 on ≥ 90 % of seeds; else its rows are moved so that the midpoint of `v(modest)` and `v(comfortable)` maps to 0 and
`v(rich)` to 1, re-measured once.

### 3.4 Purpose — a prosperity island or an institution island

For each open slot, in slot order (J-V5.4):

1. **A forced slot** takes its declaration's kind (an institution kind makes it an institution island; any other kind a
   prosperity island) or its assignment's institution.
2. **Otherwise the candidates are:** `prosperity`, weight 1.0, where the roster's households of the bands with
   `h_carried(v(b)) > 0` not yet carried by an earlier slot give at least `minAreaHa` of plot land; and each
   `IslandInstitution` row, at its `weight`, where the roster demands at least one eligible instance of a class carrying
   one of its `tags` (the first tag in the row's order that has one, its lowest roster ordinal) — or the dossier carries
   a custom institution whose resolved profile's scene archetype is one of the row's `archetypes` (a `landmarkOnly` entry
   admitting only `landmarkLevel: landmark`; item 104 (a) (2)), the lowest `localUid` first — that no earlier slot carries
   and no creation-prefix `placeAt` holds on the ground, its `requires.government` (when present) holds for the town's
   `Government.kind`, its `requires.magic` (when present) admits the recipe's `magic` circumstance, and `areaHaMax ·
   h_carried(v(requirementBand)) · h_town` ≥ `institutionFit.fitFactor` × the row's `areaHa`. **An eligible instance is
   one building or one compound** (item 108 (a); J-V5.57): a singular record, or a record whose ladder step is SIZE, or a
   roster instance bound to neither — never a COUNT, GUILD or QUARTER record, nor a member `#k` of one. A dossier
   institution counts as a roster instance (DR9, DR24): the island moves where it stands, never whether it exists (§7.7).
   The archetype is read at this draw only: a later revision of a custom kind re-resolves its profile and keeps it on its
   island unless a hard row of the new profile fails there, when item 104 (e)'s least disruption re-sites it on the
   ground; the island keeps its reason and its claim, the vacated building steps down the base's building ladder, and
   the regrowth-first order re-occupies it with the next instance of one of the row's tags or its archetype before any
   ground site (§4.11; V05 §3.15 (4)).
3. **The draw:** one weighted choice on `island.place` (town lineage, index `reason:k`). With no candidate the slot
   closes, reported `no supply` or `nothing to carry`.

### 3.5 The sky allocation (S7b (5) at depth)

**A prosperity slot.**
1. The eligible bands are those with `h_carried(v(b)) > 0`, richest first.
2. Households are taken from the roster's residential demand of the richest eligible band, in batches of
   `fill.batchShare` of that band's demand (at least one household), each batch split over the classes by the band's
   class mix at its centre (item 71; largest remainder); after each batch `S_carried` and `A` are recomputed. **Held
   shares are never taken:** the households a dossier district-defining record's raised share holds on its ground
   district (item 72) — a QUARTER record of item 108 (a) among them — are subtracted from the band's demand before the
   fill, so the island never drains a quarter the dossier names. The fill stops at the first of: the carried plot land
   (Σ over the carried rows of `plotArea(c, b)` × count, S2's plot areas at the band centres) reaching `plotShare · A`,
   `plotShare` volume 1's `hood.constants.sky.plotShare` (V01 §3.14; §4.5 S7b (5)), the one share the island's district
   count divides by, so an edit of it moves the fill and the count together (J-V5.45); the carried households reaching
   `fill.householdCapDistricts` × `H_d`, `H_d` the town's households ÷ its district count after S6; or the band's demand
   exhausted, when the next band is taken only if the batch would leave the carried plot land within the recomputed
   `plotShare · A`. `A` falls as poorer households join, so the fill converges (II.6 (5)).
3. **What goes up with them — the carried workplaces, read through `IslandRule.carry`** (J-V5.32, J-V5.39, J-V5.51). For
   each workplace or institution class demanded at the carried bands, the carried households' share of their band's
   households × its count, by largest remainder, except:
   (a) **never carried** — the `IslandInstitution` tags (their own slots); the town-unique classes (§10.2's `unique`
   column: the principal market, the castle, the government's seat); every class whose balance profile (item 107 (a)) is
   in `carry.never.balance` — the heavy processors and wholesale and storage, which belong by the gates and quays; every
   class whose siting profile (item 103 (a)) emits one of `carry.never.emitsTopStep` at the profile scale's top step,
   since nothing on an island can lie downwind or downstream of its homes; every class with a hard NEEDS row naming a
   `carry.never.hardNeeds` feature — the conjured river and the spring pond satisfy no NEEDS row (V05 §3.20 (1)); and
   every resource or processing node of an active chain — custom (item 104 (b)) or one of the dossier's `activeChains[]`
   with its processors resolved by kind family (item 105 (i)) — the island institution of V05 §3.4 excepted. Their
   demand stays on the ground, where the chain keeps its working steps together (V05 §3.20 (9)).
   (b) **carried by share** — every class (a) does not exclude: the everyday services and the town-wide specialists of
   `carry.share.balance` above all, a chain's final seller and its consumers among them.
   (c) **split across the arenas** — a class whose row declares `split` (item 105 (h)) and whose works (a) excludes (a
   butcher's slaughterhouse emits at the top step; a brewer's brewhouse is a heavy processor): with
   `carry.share.splitShop` on, its **shop** may go up by the share while its **works** stays on the ground — one item on
   two plots on two arenas, the shop's building carrying a `Use` whose `branchOf` names the works' use. Item 105 (h)'s
   split threshold is read with the route distance (V05 §3.20 (1)) between the island's demand and the works' ground
   site; below it the class stays whole on the ground. Every split is reported; off in the parity presets, as the
   islands are.
   (d) **collective members** (item 108 (b), (c)) — a member `#k` of a COUNT, GUILD or QUARTER record carried by the share
   keeps its record and its key; the record's range check (T73-4) counts its members on both arenas together; a lesser a
   greater subsumes is carried as that greater's member, never as an addition.
   **Coverage** (item 103 (f); J-V5.37). For a class with a coverage row, the island counts as **k units** for a row
   counted by district or neighbourhood — k from `skyDistrictCount(plotLandM2, districtHa)` (V01 §3.16) on the
   allocation's plot land, the call the unit runner makes — **one unit** for a row counted by walking radius (the route
   to the ground passes every walking radius, V05 §3.20 (3)), and **⌈its carried households ÷ N⌉ units** for a row counted
   by N households. The proportional share above stands, and the island's units then join S7b (1)'s coverage step as
   candidate units beside the ground's districts, which may add an instance of the class to this allocation from the
   demand it distributes — never remove one — unless the add would carry the plot land past `fill.coverageOvershoot` ×
   `plotShare · A`, when that unit is skipped for that class (reported). Inside the island, the unit run's S10b spreads a
   coverage class over its districts and spaces its members by item 107 (d)'s same-kind row (V05 §3.20 (12)).
4. If `A` < `minAreaHa`, the slot closes (reported) and its rows return to the demand.
5. The island's district count and its partition are volume 1's (V01 §3.10.3 (2), read with `districtHa`): this volume
   counts none (J-V5.28).
6. One `Allocation` (level `district`, target the island's sky pseudo-district) holds the rows; `S_carried` and `A` are
   written to the slot's record-to-be.

**An institution slot.** Its row's instance (V05 §3.4 (2)) with `claim` set once SKY makes the claim (V05 §3.10), plus
`staffHouseholds` households of the requirement band; `A` = min(the law's `A`, `institutionFit.areaCapShare` × the row's
`areaHa`) — a compact island, its surround the unit run's gardens, close or yard; its claim's face is typed by the row's
`districtKind` (V05 §3.12). Then, in order:
1. **A greater goes up alone** (item 108 (c); J-V5.57). A greater that subsumes lessers — a cathedral over the parish
   church, the resident priest and the wayside shrine — takes only its own building up. Its lessers stay separate
   buildings, members of its collective each keeping its `#k`: on the ground by their own rules, and on an island only by
   a prosperity slot's share; each counts once, never as an addition.
2. **The ground reservation's hand-over** (J-V5.46). Where the instance holds an S3 reservation — the temple or cathedral
   precinct (§4.2 S3 (2)), or the reservation a palace takes by the castle's siting rules (§4.2 S3 (8)) — the
   reservation stays exactly as it is (no L1 change) and passes to the row's `handover`: the first of its `tags` the
   roster demands on the ground, its largest instance (V05 §2.4), which S10b places into it as its claim (the
   reservation's `owner` becomes that instance and its `handedFrom` the institution that went up, §2.5); a reservation
   sized for the greater keeps its size, the surplus becoming the incoming instance's churchyard, close or garden by its
   template's yard rows (§4.9). With no such tag demanded, `else`: `park` — re-typed `park`, owner `commons`, laid out by
   S12b as the old palace gardens or the old close's green — or `none`. An institution the creation prefix sends up (a
   T5 `sky` declaration of its kind, a T6 `criteria.sky` assignment) leaves nothing to hand over: S3 never reserves it
   (§4.2 S3 (2)). After generation — a T6 assignment or an epoch's `raiseIsland` taking a ground institution up — its
   vacated building steps down the base's ladder, and the regrowth-first order re-occupies it with the row's hand-over
   classes first. The report and the clerk name the hand-over ("the old cathedral close — now the mother church").
3. **Burial and the crypt** (item 109 (k) (2)–(3) as revised, J111; item 2; J-V5.43). The churchyard part the base gives
   every church and temple plot (§4.9's churchyard row) — the island cathedral's, and any church's a prosperity island
   carries — is a surface burial ground only: it is left out of item 109 (k) (3)'s catacomb candidates and out of its
   "largest cemetery" ranking (volume 4's candidate list skips every sky-arena face). Each church on an island still
   counts once toward the catacomb count (V04 §3.5.1 (3), §2.3), since the dossier's church records and the roster set
   that count wherever the churches stand. The island cathedral's crypt — always the settlement's largest (volume 11's
   crypt column, `always`) — is a vault cut into the island's rim slab under the cathedral (V05 §3.10 (5)): part of its
   building, never an `UndergroundSystem` part or an item 109 (j) (3b) link, and no catacomb grows beneath it. The ground
   church that takes the old close keeps its own crypt by the crypted-class rows and is never ranked above the
   cathedral's.

**The rest.** S7b (1) allocates the remainder to the ground's districts, so the population stays whole (II.6).

### 3.6 Shape (S7b (5), per open slot, in the island's local frame)

1. **Template.** A declaration's points form gives it (a lasso is the outline itself, one point a disc of area `A`, two
   to four corners the routine fitted to their hull); otherwise one weighted draw of `outline.templates` on
   `island.place` (the island's lineage, index `template`).
2. **Bearing.** The long axis takes the ground's principal river's bearing (its course from inlet to outfall) where the
   site has a river; otherwise a uniform bearing on `island.place` (index `bearing`).
3. **The routine.** §4.2 S1 (2)'s closed-outline routine on the template's base curve, with the rule's detail row and a
   uniform envelope (no town side), from `island.place` (index `outline`); the base curve's scale is set by one secant
   step so that the displaced outline's area is `A` within 1 %. F-OUTLINE's ladder applies (§5.2).
4. The outline's centroid is the local origin, and the arena's frame is sized from the outline now drawn (V05 §3.8 (1)).

### 3.7 Placement — where the island floats (S7b (5), per open slot, in slot order)

1. **The class raster.** A 64 m raster over the ground frame classes each cell: `water` (its centre in an L0 water face,
   an oasis's included — item 111's oasis is water like any other); `core` (in a district of growth stage 1);
   `outskirts` (in any other district); `fields` (land outside the settlement perimeter). It is built once per S7b from
   the ground plan that exists then (L0, L1, the districts, the perimeter).
2. **Candidates.** The centroids of a `latticeM` lattice over the frame whose translated outline lies inside the frame
   inset by `frameInsetM`, does not meet any placed island's projected outline dilated by `gapM`, and **can be reached**
   (J-V5.54): at least one of its rim's cells on the class raster lies on admissible ground (for a hoist or a stair) or
   within the horizontal run of an admitted slanted access row (`ropeway`, and `lightBridge` where the `magic`
   circumstance admits it) of admissible ground. Admissible ground is the raster's land cells outside water,
   reservations and wall strips, and outside every committed raised part's raised-way pier footprints, its deck's plan
   dilated by `accessSiting.deckGapM` and every deck face of item 110 (d) — V05 §3.10 (3)'s admissible faces at the
   raster's cell (a cell is admissible when its centre is); the test reads one chamfer distance transform to it (integer,
   3–4 weights on the 64 m cells), built once with the class raster (J-V5.60).
3. **The score** of a candidate, over the outline's cells on the class raster: (a) the mean class weight — the rule's
   `prefs`, with an institution row's `overWeights` over them, and with `core` at 1.0 when the island is placed by
   assignment or declaration — × 1.0; (b) proximity, − 0.5 × min(1, d ÷ `nearM`), `d` the distance from the outline to
   the settlement perimeter (0 over the town); (c) purpose, 0.5 × one term, read from the row's `near` or `far` (V05 §2.4)
   for an institution island: `near: 'plaza'` 1 − min(1, d ÷ the row's `nearM`) to the plaza; `near: 'precinct'` the same
   to the precinct where S3 reserved one (then the hand-over church's close, V05 §3.5), else to the plaza; `near:
   'richest'` the same to the district of highest prosperity mean; `far: 'plaza'` min(1, d ÷ the row's `nearM`) from the
   plaza; a prosperity island 1 − min(1, d ÷ `nearM`) to the district of highest prosperity mean (ties by district
   address) (J-V5.34).
4. **The draw.** The `topK` best (ties by lattice y, then x), then one draw weighted by score − min score + 0.01 on
   `island.place` (index `place`). The chosen centroid is `anchor`; `placement.over` is the class of largest share under
   the outline (ties in the order water, fields, outskirts, core); `byAssignment` is set for a forced slot (J-V5.5).
5. **F-SKY-PLACE** (V05 §3.17) when no candidate passes.

### 3.8 The island arena: frame, land, rim, terrain, the conjured river (SKY)

1. **Frame.** Computed after the outline (V05 §3.6 (3)): a square centred on the local origin of side 2 × `r_max` ×
   √1.5 + 2 × `frameMarginM`, rounded up to 10 m, `r_max` the outline's greatest distance from its centroid — so the
   outline grown to 1.5 × its area by the buffer resize (V05 §4.2) stays inside whatever its template and aspect, a
   spindle as surely as a disc; a declared or edited area past that grows the frame by §3.2 (9)'s single-level batches.
2. **L0 batch.** The arena's outer ring (the `margin` role on the island's `Terrain`), then the rim — the outline as one
   closed chain carrying the structural `rim` role (§2.2 rule 7) — making two faces: the **void**, L0 kind `sky`,
   between the ring and the rim (§2.2 rule 1); and the **land**, owner the island. Every later batch on this arena lies
   inside the land face.
3. **Terrain.** The island's own `Terrain` (§2.3): the ground's terrain cell; a `hydro` grid at half the ground's hydro
   cell (12.5 m at CITY, 16 m at METROPOLIS: an island's catchments are small); `geology: 'ordinary'`. Heights: a dome
   `(domeM + domePerRadius · R) · (1 − (r ÷ R)²)`, clamped at 0 (`R` the equivalent radius √(A ÷ π), `r` the distance
   from the centroid); plus, for `echo`, §4.2 S1 (1)'s fBm at the ground's relief class with its amplitude ×
   `amplitudeShare`, for `own` the same at a class drawn by S0's relief weights on `island.place` (index `relief`), which
   SKY writes to `terrainEcho.reliefClass` (J-V5.78), for `none` nothing; the noise on `terrain.noise` keyed by the
   island's `Terrain`. Where a conjured river is drawn, the surface tilts by `tiltPerMille` along its bearing, downhill
   toward its fall. Void cells hold 0 and are masked. The terrain's `datumCm` is the ground's datum plus the island's
   `elevationCm`, rewritten whenever that changes (V05 §3.9).
4. **The conjured river** (the owner's "magically conjured river"; J-V5.23). Where the ground has a river, `A` ≥
