   `river.minAreaHa` and a draw on `island.place` (index `river`) is below `river.chance`: the source is the point on the
   long axis at `river.sourceShare` of its length from its upstream end (upstream: against the ground river's flow); the
   course is traced and carved by §4.2 S1 (3)'s rivers by descent on the island's `hydro` grid until it meets the rim;
   its width is `w0 · (flowShare · acc)^0.4` — the ground's width law, `acc` the ground river's accumulation at its reach
   nearest the island's anchor — clamped to [`widthM[0]`, `widthMinorShare` × the outline's minor axis]. Its water face
   enters L0 with `shore` edges, bank kinds by §4.2 S1 (7)'s table; where it meets the rim the short stretch between its
   two banks carries both `rim` and `shore`, and the `WaterBody` carries `fall: true` (§2.3) — its outfall is the
   waterfall. Its crossing sites are §4.2 S1 (5)'s, on the island, built by volume 1's unit run (V01 §3.10.3 (4)); a
   bridge there may carry deck plots by item 110 (d)'s mechanism like any bridge, and they fall with the island (V05
   §3.22 (6)). No raised part stands on an island (V05 §3.22 (3)). With no ground river, a **spring pond** (`pond`,
   `river.pondShare` × `A`, at the terrain's highest basin, ties by lattice y then x) by a draw below
   `springPondChance`; an oasis on the ground is no river for this rule. `terrainEcho` records the ground river, the relief
   mode and, for `own`, the drawn class. **An island's own water under item 111** (J-V5.85): item 111's oasis option, its
   water pull and its climate cell reach no sky island — no oasis is drawn on an island's arena, no well there is placed
   by ground yield, the island's water is this conjured river or spring pond alone, and an arid ground changes none of
   this section's draws or values (T-V5-37).
5. **F-SKY-TERRAIN** (V05 §3.17) when the descent stalls.

### 3.9 The underside, the ground reference and the altitude (the owner's J98)

1. **The underside profile** (SKY; J-V5.15): `kind` drawn by the rule's weights on `island.place` (index
   `underside.kind`); `rimThicknessCm` the rule's; for every kind but `flat`, `depthCm = min(maxDepthM, depthPerRadius ·
   R) · (0.8 + 0.4 · u)`, `u` a uniform on `island.place` (index `underside.depth`), `apexOffset` a uniform point in the
   disc of radius `apexOffsetShare · R` (index `underside.apex`), pulled toward the centroid until the apex lies over the
   land face, and `tiers` the rule's; for `flat`, `depthCm` 0, `apexOffset` (0, 0) and `tiers` 0 with no draw on
   `underside.depth` or `underside.apex` (so a flat island's altitude floor counts no root); `roughness` the rule's;
   `source: 'rule'`.
2. **The underside tiers** — a pure function `undersideTiers(island)` (`@plat/sky`), view data computed by S14, never
   stored: for tier i = 1 … n (`n = tiers`; none for `flat`) at depth fraction `z_i = i ÷ n`, the ring is the rim eroded by
   `R · (1 − ρ(z_i))` — the kernel's buffer on a transient arena (§3.4, §3.7), integer — translated by `apexOffset · z_i`
   and each vertex moved radially by `roughness · R ÷ n` × a `dmath` noise keyed by the island's render key and the tier;
   tier i spans from `elevation − rim − depth · z_(i−1)` down to `elevation − rim − depth · z_i` (`rim` the rim
   thickness). Underside tier 0 is the rim slab: the rim itself, from the island's **surface top** (below) down to
   `elevation − rim`. A tier whose ring falls below 1 % of the land's area ends the stack; the last tier then runs to the
   apex. So the solid is a stack of nested prisms whose silhouette follows the profile: a `root` hangs a narrow
   stalactite, a `bowl` a rounded belly, a `cone` a straight taper, a `flat` a thin slab.
3. **The ground reference** (SKY, before any plot is cut; J-V5.29): `groundRefCm` = the greatest, over the ground's 32 m
   field cells (§4.5) whose centres lie under the island's projected land face or any projected underside-tier ring, of
   the terrain height at the cell centre plus the cell's **caster ceiling** — the greatest of `casterAllowanceM`; the top
   of every caster already placed whose footprint meets the cell (a wall's band, a tower, a gate of any kind, the castle,
   a bridge or pier deck — §8.4's casters — and every raised part committed before SKY: its deck top with its parapet, or
   an aqueduct's channel wall, over the deck's plan, and its raised-way piers; any retaining wall and levelled platform
   committed before SKY at its high-side top); and the `towerCm` of the heights row of every class with a tower (a church,
   a keep; §4.7 S11) allocated to the district holding the cell (the hinterland's allocation outside the districts) —
   above the ground datum. It reads only what exists at SKY, so the altitude is final before S10a reads the island's shade
   (V05 §3.19 (6)).
4. **The altitude** `altitudeCm = max(E, rim + depth + clearanceM)` — the law's altitude, raised where a deep root needs
   it so the apex clears the tallest thing beneath by `clearanceM` (J-V5.6) — or the edited value, which the same floor
   bounds (below it the edit is bent to the floor, V05 §4.2). `elevationCm` is `groundRefCm + altitudeCm`. The island's
   **surface top** — the `surfaceTopPercentile` percentile over the land face of its height at each point: the levelled
   ground there, `levelledGroundCm` on the island's key (§4.7: the platform datum where item 110 (a) levelled the plot or
   block, else the terrain), plus the eave of the building standing there — for a stepped building the eave of the
   **highest massing tier over that point** (its wing's `baseCm + eaveCm`, V02 §3.15.2) — is computed with the underside
   tiers (underside tier 0's top). The model's strip (V05 §3.19) reads underside tier 0's top from the terrain alone at
   generation and at a re-derivation, and from the full surface top at an epoch and at an update, since item 102 (b) lets
   buildings cast the daylight term only across epochs.
5. **The clearance check** (SKY-F, after the pool's merge and the unit runs): where a caster placed after SKY under the
   projected solid — a building's ridge or tower (a stepped building's top from volume 2's stack envelope, V02 §3.15.2), a
   tree's crown, a raised part committed after SKY (its deck top with its parapet or channel wall, its raised-way piers),
   a retaining wall's high-side top, or the levelled ground itself where S10 or S11's levelling raises a platform under
   the solid by fill (`levelledGroundCm` above the raster) — stands above `groundRefCm`, `groundRefCm` is raised to its
   top **keeping `elevationCm`** — `altitudeCm` becomes `elevationCm` − the new `groundRefCm` — and only where (4)'s floor
   then fails is `elevationCm` raised to meet it (a listed fate), so an island whose clearance already holds does not
   climb; SKY-F never writes `shadeBasis`, so the island's strip keeps its basis until the next shade evaluation, across
   saves and loads alike, reported `shade basis behind: clearance` (F-SKY-CLEAR, V05 §3.17). **Measurement (P0):** over
   1 000 islands at CITY and METROPOLIS, with item 110's terrace, raised-way and deck-plot rows on at their defaults, the
   share raised here ≤ 1 %. **Pre-decided fallback:** `casterAllowanceM` becomes the measured 99.9th percentile of the
   caster tops found under islands (item 110's included), rounded up to 5 m, and is re-measured once. The clearance also
   holds after every epoch (V05 §3.15 (7)).

### 3.10 Access — the tops, the feet, the landing claims (SKY)

1. **How many.** A prosperity island: `min(accessCount.max, 1 + ⌊areaHa ÷ accessCount.perHa⌋)`, the first `main` and
   the rest `service`; an institution island: its row's `accesses`. **A removed ordinal holds** (J-V5.62): at every
   re-siting (a Re-derive from S7b or SKY) the ordinals in `SkyIsland.removedAccesses` are skipped — no top, no foot, no
   claims — and counted toward the number, so no replacement is sited; the lowest surviving generated ordinal takes `main`
   (an institution row's `guarded` role kept by its own ordinal), the rest `service`.
2. **Directions.** `d_1` is the bearing from the island's world centroid to the nearest ground district's centroid;
   `d_k = d_1 + (k − 1) · 360° ÷ n`. Each top is the point where the ray from the centroid at `d_k` last leaves the land
   face.
3. **The kind at each top**, among the access rows admitted by the recipe's `magic` circumstance (a `guarded` access is a
   `hoist` only): `hoist` or `stair` where the top's world point lies on ground land in an admissible face — a district's
   L1 face that is no reservation, claim or wall strip, or a hinterland L1 face, less every raised-way pier footprint,
   every raised deck's plan dilated by `accessSiting.deckGapM` and every item 110 (d) deck face (J-V5.60) — and the
   altitude is within the row's limit; `ropeway` or `lightBridge` where an admissible land point lies within the row's
   horizontal run of the top (the point nearest the town's main plaza for a rope line; the highest ground for a light
   bridge; ties by lattice y, then x). One weighted draw among the admitted kinds on `island.place` (index `access:k`)
   (J-V5.8). **A slanted access over a raised part:** a `ropeway` or `lightBridge` whose straight segment from its top to
   its foot passes over a raised part's deck or a raised-way pier keeps `accessSiting.lineClearM` above its top (the deck
   with its parapet or channel wall, the pier's head) along the whole crossing; failing that, the candidate foot is
   refused and F-SKY-ACCESS takes its next direction step.
4. **The foot.** A `hoist` or `stair` foot is the top's world point; a `ropeway` or `lightBridge` foot the chosen land
   point. Feet keep `accessSiting.footGapM` apart: a later foot inside the gap of an earlier one moves to the nearest
   admissible point outside it (ties by lattice y, then x). A foot and its landing yard stand on part-0 land only: never
   on a deck face, a raised-way pier or inside a raised deck's plan dilated by `deckGapM` — a yard that would meet one is
   slid along the spine to the nearest position clear of it, else the foot moves as a gap violation does. **"The nearest
   way"** here and in V05 §3.12 is a **walkable part-0 way**: a raised part's deck counts only at the ground vertex of its
   ramp or stair foot, an aqueduct never. **In a district**, the foot is moved along the perpendicular to the nearest L1
   way spine within `wayReachM` — or for a vertical access the landing yard is stretched from the foot to that spine —
   and SKY's ground L1 batch claims the landing yard (`claimSubFace`, `yardWidthM × yardDepthM`, touching the spine) as a
   ground `Reservation` of kind `landing` (data key `reservations.landing`) with its attach vertex on the spine
   (J-V5.17). **Where no spine lies within `wayReachM`** (at SKY only S3–S7's arterials, boundary ways and gate plazas
   exist), the yard is claimed at the foot itself, its attach vertex on the yard's side facing the nearest way, and the
   attach is a **destination** S8 must reach (§4.6), which SKY-F checks (V05 §3.12). Either way SKY writes, under
   `allocations.landing`, the positioned row `{ cls: 'sky_landing', band: 'modest', count: 1, claim, at: foot, access }`
   for that district — a row of its own beside the district's `Allocation`, never a rewrite of a row S7b wrote; its two
   staff households are the row's, and S10a takes them from the district's own `modest` residential demand (§2.5). **In
   the hinterland**, the landing is an outside site: the same row under `allocations.landing` for the hinterland's
   `Allocation`, sited by S12a at the foot and given its trail to the nearest way by the hinterland unit, as a
   decoration's trail is (§4.9). A `secret` access's ground landing is fronted (V05 §2.2).
5. **The top.** One `splitThrough` at L0 on the rim places the access's `('sky_access', id)` vertex, pinned; SKY's
   island L1 batch then lays the **rim strip** — a closed L1 line `rimStripM` inside the rim carrying the `setback` role
   on the island, its strip face typed as a block interior `rim_strip` (§2.5; J-V5.26) — and claims each top's **landing
   yard** (`yardWidthM × yardDepthM`, from the rim inward across the strip) as an island `Reservation` of kind `landing`
   with its attach vertex on the yard's inner side, and, for an institution island, the **institution claim**: one
   `claimSubFace` of the row's `areaHa` (§4.5 S7b (3)'s compact claim shape) on the land face less the rim strip and the
   landing yards, searched over the sites of an `institutionFit.siteLatticeM` lattice (the claim's centre) whose claim
   fits there, by a named score: a **palace** the greatest mean terrain height under the claim among the sites on the half
   of the land facing `d_1` (the site's projection on `d_1` from the centroid ≥ 0; the whole land if none fits there); a
   **cathedral** and a **tower** the least distance from the site to the land face's centroid; a **prison** the least
   distance to the centroid among the sites whose claim dilated by `institutionFit.prisonMarginM` still fits. Ties break
   by lattice y, then x (J-V5.12's rule for every search here).
   **The crypt test** (item 109 (k) (2) as revised, J111; J-V5.43). The crypt lies **under the building's lowest massing
   tier, inside underside tier 0 (the rim slab)** (J-V5.66): a stepped cathedral or great temple of volume 2's `stepped`
   rows keeps its crypt beneath massing tier 0. For an instance whose class carries a crypt `always` (volume 11's crypt
   column: the cathedral and the great temple), a site passes only if, at every point of the claim, the island's terrain
   height less the full basement depth (220 cm, volume 2's basement form) lies at least `cryptFloorM` above the rim
   slab's base, `elevationCm − rimThicknessCm` — so the crypt is a vault cut into underside tier 0, which exists for every
   underside kind, `flat` included. With the default rim this holds wherever the terrain lies above −6.8 m, so it
   excludes only an `own` relief's deep basin. If no site passes, the best site by score is taken and the crypt takes the
   half-sunk form (volume 2's basement ladder, full → half → raised), listed as a fate ("the rock is too thin for a full
   crypt here") and reported by F-SKY-CRYPT. The crypt is part of the cathedral's building — its basement record, whose
   rooms volume 3 plans — never an `UndergroundSystem` part, never an item 109 (j) (3b) link and never a catacomb source
   (V05 §3.5; item 2). Every later re-claim (a resize, a rim reshape, New random, a terrain edit on the island) re-runs the
   test, a change of form listed as a fate. **After levelling:** the claim is searched at SKY on the terrain; SKY-F
   re-runs the test on the cathedral's plot at its levelled platform datum (`levelledGroundCm`) where the unit run's S10 or
   S11 levelled it (item 110 (a)), since a platform cut into a slope lowers the floor — a form change listed as a fate,
   F-SKY-CRYPT's ladder as before.
6. **The ground's field** gains, at field pass 2 (S10a), a positive kernel at each ground landing (`landingKernel`;
   §4.5): the land by a landing trades with the island above.
7. **F-SKY-ACCESS** (V05 §3.17) where a top has no admitted kind.

### 3.11 The island's field and its context vector

**An institution island** is uniform: its prosperity is `v(requirementBand)` of its row (one building and its staff).
**A prosperity island** carries a **gradient** that gives each carried band a share of the island's land equal to its
share of the carried plot land, the richest on the best ground (J-V5.18; the owner's "prosperity is a gradient and a
band"). On the island's 32 m lattice (the cells whose centres lie in the land face less the rim strip), SKY computes

`P_I(x) = smooth(Q(r(x)) + ε(x)) − w_sun · shadeSky(x) + brush(x)`, clamped once to [0, 1] and banded by the town's
`FieldBands` cuts (the island belongs to the town's band set):

1. **The site rank** `r(x)` ∈ [0, 1]. Each cell's elevation rank `e` (the percentile of its terrain height among the
   cells) and view rank `v` (the percentile of `view(x) = (1 − d_rim(x) ÷ R) · (1 + cos(θ(x) − θ_town)) ÷ 2`, `d_rim` the
   distance to the rim, `R` the equivalent radius, `θ(x)` the bearing from the land centroid to the cell and `θ_town` the
   bearing from the land centroid's world point to the town's main plaza) are combined as `field.rank.elevation · e +
   field.rank.view · v` and re-ranked to `r`, ties by lattice y, then x: the high ground and the rim that looks over the
   city rank first. On a `none` terrain every `e` ties and the view decides.
2. **`Q`** maps rank to value by the carried bands' shares. With the carried bands `b_1` (richest) … `b_m` and their
   shares `s_j` of the carried plot land (Σ `plotArea(c, b_j)` × count over each band's rows, V05 §3.5 (2)), the ranks from
   `1 − Σ_{i ≤ j} s_i` to `1 − Σ_{i < j} s_i` map linearly onto band `b_j`'s inner range `[lo_j + ι, hi_j − ι]`, `lo_j`
   and `hi_j` its two cuts on the absolute value (the lowest band's lower cut 0, the top band's upper cut 1) and `ι` =
   `field.bandInset` × `(hi_j − lo_j)`, so ε and the smoothing move a cell within its band far more often than across a
   cut.
3. **ε** — the base's low-frequency fBm (§4.5) on `field.chance` keyed by the island, wavelength
   `field.epsilonWavelengthM`, share `epsilonShare`; **smooth** — the ground field's Gaussian of one block width (§4.5's
   O(N) three-box blur) over the lattice.
4. **`shadeSky`(x)** — the share of the sampler's samples in which a higher island's solid blocks the sun at x (the OR,
   sample by sample, of the strips onto this island, V05 §3.19), and, after an epoch, the island's own buildings (volume
   6's building shade mask run on the island's lattice, V05 §3.19 (7)); the island's gentle relief casts nothing on
   itself (J-V5.33). `w_sun` is §4.5's, as item 111 (c)'s climate cell sets it for the town's climate row.
5. **`brush`(x)** — the island's prosperity brush strokes (`setField` with `key`, V05 §4.2) as §7.2's stroke overrides; a
   declared target mean (`setAttr(field.prosperity)`, `declared.field`) adds one uniform shift so the lattice mean of
   `smooth(Q + ε)` equals it.

**No water term** (item 111; J-V5.85): `P_I` takes no term of §4.5's water pull — the island's gradient is the rank, the
bands, ε, the shade and the brush above, and nothing else; the island's water enters its context only as `dist.water`
below.

With ε, the shade and the brush at 0, each carried band holds exactly its carried share of the lattice cells (within the
smoothing's boundary cells), so §4.7 S10a's band capacity on the island houses every carried band on its own ground and
its band-range step re-homes none of them (T-V5-9). `SkyIsland.field` records `prosperity` = the lattice mean of `P_I`,
`density` = the mean of the ground's density field over the cells whose prosperity band is one of the carried bands, and
`greenery` = the same mean of the greenery field plus `greeneryBonus`; `SkyUnitInput.field` carries the raster before the
brush (the unit run applies the strokes as the ground's field step does). The raster is rebuilt on load (V05 §2.6).

**The context vector** on the sky arena (`arena: 'sky'`, §2.5) reads: `prosperity` = `P_I` at x (bilinear on the lattice);
`density` and `greenery` as `field` gives them; `dist.centre` to the land's centroid; `dist.plaza` and `dist.market` to
the institution claim on an institution island, else the route distance (V05 §3.20) to the ground's plaza and to its
nearest market; `dist.gate` to the nearest landing attach (a landing is the island's gate); `dist.wall` to an author's
island wall (V05 §3.21), else none; `dist.water` to the conjured river or pond; `dist.shore` to the rim; `slope`,
`aspect`, `upstream` and `terrainClass` from the island's terrain (`high`, but `damp` within 10 m of island water);
`upwind` from the ground's wind; `growthStage` 1; `arena: 'sky'`. Volume 1's field step on the island (V01 §3.10.3 (3))
reads these values through `skyUnitInput` — they replace a character offset from `h(S_carried)`, since a supply fraction
is not a prosperity value.

### 3.12 The unit inputs, and what the unit run must honour

SKY builds each island's `sky` request (V05 §2.3) and calls the unit runner, which reads `skyUnitInput(doc, island)` —
the claims, the landings, the river, the allocation, the field and the context. **The fabric hint is a prior, never a
strategy** (J-V5.48): on every island district, S7c's template draw multiplies the weight of the hinted template by
`fabricPrior` and chooses as it does anywhere; the hint is a prosperity island's `ring` from `fabricRingHa` and
`bent-cut` below it, and an institution island's row's (`street` for a palace — an axial avenue from the main landing to
the forecourt; `landmark` for a cathedral; `square` for a prison or a tower), always a valid `FabricStrategyId` (V01
§2.1).

**The unit run's contract** (V01 §3.10.3 states the run; these inputs are this volume's, J-V5.28): no S1 — SKY has
committed the island's L0, terrain and river (V05 §3.8); the unit's outline is the land face less the rim strip, the
landing yards and the institution claim, each landing's attach a gate-ranked destination a way must reach; the field
step reads `SkyUnitInput.field` (V05 §3.11) and applies the island's brush strokes; every face is typed
`context.districtKind` — `sky-island` on a prosperity island, and on an institution island the row's `sky-*` kind on the
face that receives the claim and `sky-island` on any other; S7c reads `fabricHint` × `fabricPrior`; the district count is
`skyDistrictCount(plotLandM2, districtHa)` (V01 §3.16), so a full CITY island is two or three districts and a full
METROPOLIS island three (J-V5.45). The run must: write only the island's arena; never cut or plot the rim strip, which
volume 1's `plantRimWalk` plants once, after the districts' S12b, as the rim walk (V01 §3.10.3 step 4; §4.9's rim-walk
row: one `esplanade` `Greenery` on the strip's face, no way on it, and one `street_trees` `Greenery` along its inner
setback line; J-V5.26); reach every landing attach with a way; cut the institution claim as a hole and place its
institution there by S10b; use the river's crossing sites as V01 §3.10.3 (4) qualifies and builds them; split the
allocation over its districts (V01 §3.10.3 (4)); measure every pull to a class on another arena by the route distance
(V05 §3.20); level island ground by item 110 (a)'s thresholds, laying each retaining wall on the island's arena and
never on the rim strip or along the rim (V05 §3.22 (4)); leave `SeamReport.portalsTotal` 0; and return its district ids,
which SKY-F writes to `SkyIsland.districts`.

**SKY-F checks** that every landing yard holds a `sky_landing_top` plot, that a way meets every island attach, and that
a walkable part-0 way meets every ground landing attach after S8 (V05 §3.10 (4)); that no island `RetainingWall` stands
on the rim strip or along the rim (V05 §3.22 (4)); and it re-runs the crypt test at the levelled datum (V05 §3.10 (5)).
A missing island way is repaired by one island L1 batch laying the shortest lane from the attach to the fabric (S8's
lane width), a missing ground way by one ground L1 batch in the landing's district doing the same (the district's own
lane rule), and an unreachable landing is reported by F-SKY-ACCESS's last rung.

### 3.13 Lights (SKY-F; the sky rows of §4.7's light table)

| host | what | defaults (own, `extension`) |
|---|---|---|
| `rim` | magical orbs along the rim strip's outer edge | every 40 m, 2.5 m high, 60 cd × `h_carried`, radius 18 m, orb 30 cm, cool, steady |
| `keel` | one downward magical source at the root's apex (a `flat` island's: the rim slab's base under the land centroid): the glow beneath the island | 4 000 cd × `h_carried`, radius the apex's clearance + the row's `radiusMarginM` (30 m), size 3 m, cool, steady |
| `skyAccess` | a lantern at each top | 25 cd, 3 m, radius 12 m, fire, flicker |

The island's ordinary fabric — its ways, doors, squares — takes the ground rows (arena `ground`) as any district does;
each ground landing's lantern is its `sky_landing` class's `door` row. The `magic` circumstance gates no sky row: the
island is the magic that holds it up (J-V5.9). A waning island's magical lights scale with its `h_carried` at each epoch;
a settled island's are 0.

### 3.14 Names

S13a draws each island's name on its `naming` stream (§6.3's cases), reading the features: the reason and the
institution's kind; the echoed river's name; the name of the ground district nearest below (ties by district address);
the altitude class (low below `naming.lowBelowM`, high from `naming.highFromM`); the town's culture row (§10.4). The
grammar rows are volume 11's (the slice `naming.island`). Each island is the root of its own `QuarterNode` (members its
districts, `cityCut` true), named with the island's name, so its `quarter` label draws at the city band; an institution's
own name is its `landmark` label; a landfall's `place` label takes the grammar's fallen form of the island's name
(V05 §3.16).

### 3.15 Over time — the island laws an epoch applies (`IslandEpochStep`)

`IslandEpochStep.apply(doc, epoch) → ConversionOp[]` (`@plat/sky`; §4.11's island kinds) is called at each epoch after
S6's re-evaluation of the field and before S7b's delta allocation, so the households it releases or takes enter that
allocation. `@plat/sky` registers it in §4.12's `StrategyRegistry` as volume 6's `EpochParticipant` `sky.islands`, step
`afterField`, order 0, stage `SKY` (V06 §2.1, §3.2): its `run(ctx)` calls `apply(ctx.doc, ctx.epoch)` with `years` =
`ctx.years` and `rung` = `ctx.rung`, draws on `island.place` as below (never on `ctx.stream`, so a history with or without
the engine's other participants draws the same island values), and returns the conversions as `EpochOps.ops` in island
slot order. A second participant, **`sky.clearance`**, runs step 7 at `afterGrowth`, order 50 — after volume 6's
`hood.fabric` (order 40), so every raised part, retaining wall, building and massing tier the epoch committed is read —
stage `SKY` (V06 §3.2's table). **Households move through volume 6's typed channels** (V06 §2.1, §3.8.1): every household
the step releases — a wane's leavers, a settle's remainder, a fall's covered homes when the fall happens in an epoch —
enters volume 6's displaced pool with cause `island` (placed first at the `siting` step, their addresses kept, each
placement a `relocate`); every household a refloat or a raise takes up from the epoch's delta demand is reserved by
`EpochOps.demandTaken: { band: string; count: number }[]`, which `siting` subtracts from the delta demand before it houses
the rest; existing households relocated up are `relocate` ops in `EpochOps.ops`. So ground plus sky households equal the
epoch's target after `siting` (T-V5-21 (g)). A fall in an epoch commits its ruined raised spans and raised-way piers in
the epoch's lifecycle, so volume 6's `EpochScope.raised` carries them to that epoch's raised-way pass (V06 §3.19.2 (4)).
`years` is the epoch's span (§4.11 (5)); every rate below is per year, applied over it. For each island that is not
fallen or removed:

1. **Evaluate.** The rung row of the epoch's rung — the fold's (`ctx.rung`, V06 §3.1 (3); §4.11 *The rung at an epoch*;
   J-V5.74): `none` makes the target area 0. Otherwise `S_town` from the re-evaluated field's island-free value (V05 §3.19
   (7): the supply law never reads the islands' own shade), the node term from the deposits as they stand at the epoch
   (V05 §3.3), `S_carried` from the island's households' bands under the new cuts (an institution island's requirement
   band), and the targets `A_t` and `E_t` by V05 §3.3. `supply` is rewritten.
   1b. **The institution has left** (J-V5.53; item 20 (h): a dossier removal is truth). If the island's reason is
   `institution` and its institution is gone — removed by a dossier event at an absorb (the institution leaves at once:
   its building vacated, its uses closed, §7.7), its custom kind deactivated (item 104 (e)), or closed with no successor
   for a whole epoch — the island re-reasons now, in order: (i) the next eligible instance of one of the row's `tags` or
   its archetype (V05 §3.4 (2)) relocates up into the vacant claim (a `relocate`; its ground site handed over by V05 §3.5's
   rule); else (ii) the island becomes a prosperity island where `h_carried(v(rich))` > 0 and the rich band's demand
   admits it — the claim's building re-let as a mansion (a `relet` to the `rich` band's residential class), rich households
   taken up by the delta (`demandTaken`) and by relocation within the churn cap as a refloat takes them, the claim's face
   keeping its `sky-*` kind until a re-derivation re-types it `sky-island`; else (iii) `A_t` = 0 and the island wanes by
   step 2. `reason` and `institution` are rewritten by the conversion; the report and the clerk name the cause. **Under a
   lock** (J-V5.82): the institution still leaves at once (a lock never holds a dossier fact, §7.2); a locked island
   keeps `reason: 'institution'` with `institution` cleared and its claim standing vacant — the fate "held without its
   institution" listed at the absorb and at every epoch — and (1b) runs at the first epoch after the lock is lifted; a
   lock on the `state`, `areaHa` or `altitudeCm` path alone does not hold (i) or (ii), which write none of them, while
   (iii)'s wane is held as any wane is (below).
2. **Floating.** If `A_t = 0`: `waneIsland` (floating → waning), `dwellUntilWeek` = the week + a draw in
   `dwellYears.waning` × 52 on `island.place` (the island's lineage and the epoch component, index `dwell`). Otherwise, if
   |`A_t` − `areaHa`| > `hysteresis` × `areaHa`: `resizeIsland` toward `A_t` by at most `growthPerYear · areaHa · years`
   either way (magic accretes and erodes slowly), and its altitude toward `E_t` by at most `altitudeRateMPerYear · years`
   metres, never below V05 §3.9's floor; a resize stops at the institution floor of V05 §4.2, listed in the epoch's
   report.
3. **Waning.** Each epoch the altitude falls linearly from its value at the wane's start to V05 §3.9's floor (with
   `E_min`) by the dwell's end, and the area toward half its value at the start, both by `resizeIsland`; the households
   leave in reverse ordinal order (the suffix, as §4.11's step (c) empties buildings; J-V5.22), ⌈H · years ÷ dwell⌉ each
   epoch, each a `relet` down or a `vacate` on the island and the household entering the displaced pool with cause
   `island`; the island's vacant buildings then step down the base's building ladder. If `A_t > 0` again:
   `refloatIsland` (waning → floating), the dwell cleared. When the dwell ends: `settleIsland` — the altitude at the floor,
   every remaining household into the displaced pool, the magical lights at 0, `dwellUntilWeek` = the week + a draw in
   `dwellYears.settled`.
4. **Settled.** If `A_t > 0` (and the rung admits islands): `refloatIsland` (settled → floating) — regrowth-first: its
   vacant stock is reoccupied before any ground plot, by the rich bands' delta demand first (reserved by `demandTaken`)
   and then by relocating existing rich households up, within the epoch's churn cap (§4.11) — its altitude climbing at
   the rate. When the dwell ends: `fallIsland` (V05 §3.16).
5. **Empty slots** (J-V5.13). A slot holding a live, unfallen island is never redrawn. An empty slot re-opens with II.6
   (4)'s per-epoch probability `(1 − (1 − redrawPerYear)^years) · chance[k] · h_town`, drawn on `island.place` with the
   epoch component, only if every lower slot is filled. **A slot a `removed` record holds is not empty** (V05 §3.2 (6)):
   never re-opened by an epoch, it draws nothing and counts as filled for the slots above it. A slot that opens refloats
   a settled island first (the settled island with the highest `h_carried` for its households, ties by slot, then
   address) and otherwise `raiseIsland`: S7b's delta fill for the sky (V05 §3.5, taking the rich bands' delta demand first,
   reserved by `demandTaken`, then relocating existing rich households up within the churn cap), then V05 §3.6–§3.14 on
   the new island, its districts laid by the unit run at the epoch; it has no `shadeBasis` until the next epoch's
   evaluation (V05 §3.19 (6)).
6. **A rung crossing downward** makes every island's target 0: they wane, settle and fall over their dwells, never in one
   epoch (T8-6).
7. **Clearance after growth** (`sky.clearance`; J-V5.68). After the epoch's growth, every live island (not fallen, not
   removed) is re-checked by SKY-F's clearance rule (V05 §3.9 (5)) against the casters the epoch committed under its
   projected solid: the raised parts of volume 6's raised-way pass and of a new stage's S4 (their deck tops with parapet
   or channel wall, their raised-way piers), the retaining walls' high-side tops and the levelled platforms, and the
   buildings — a stepped one at its stack envelope's top, and a building whose Works starts later in the epoch's weeks at
   its planned finished height, since it is committed now (V06 §3.2's `works` step). It reads volume 6's
   `EpochScope.built`, `.raised` and `.levelled`. Where one stands above `groundRefCm`, the participant returns one op
   `{ op: 'reclearIsland'; island; groundRefCm; altitudeCm; elevationRaisedCm }` (V05 §4.2) that raises `groundRefCm` to
   it **keeping `elevationCm`** and, only where V05 §3.9 (4)'s floor then fails, raises `elevationCm` to the floor
   (`elevationRaisedCm` > 0, a listed fate: "raised clear of the aqueduct" naming the caster). It **never writes
   `shadeBasis`** (item 102 (e)'s lag, J-V5.27). A locked island, or a lock on its `altitudeCm` path, holds its
   `elevationCm`: `groundRefCm` and `altitudeCm` are still rewritten, and where the floor then fails the island stays
   where it hangs, the conflict listed (`clearance held by your lock`) and the island exempt from the clearance invariant
   as a held island is from the supply invariant (§5.3; T-V5-31 (f)). The step draws nothing, so the same history clears
   the same islands (T-V5-31 (b)).

A locked island, or a locked `state`, `areaHa` or `altitudeCm` path, is held by these steps (§7.2) — the report lists it
as `held against its supply` when its supply is 0 (J-V5.10).

**The shade over time** (item 102 (e)). Each epoch's evaluation (V05 §3.19 (6)) reads every live island as it stands when
the epoch begins — before these steps change it — so a resize, a sinking, a raise, a fall or an author's edit since the
last evaluation moves the field one epoch later, and the uses beneath follow by the engine's normal lag (volume 6
schedules the drift; §4.11's ladder moves the survivors). A fallen island casts nothing from the epoch after its fall; a
settled island casts from its floor altitude, so its strip draws in toward its footprint. An island that step 5 raises
has no basis until the next epoch's evaluation: it casts and receives no strip meanwhile, and a save and a load in
between compute none for it (J-V5.72); volume 6's building mask on its lattice likewise starts at the next field phase
(V06 §3.4 (8)).

### 3.16 The fall and the landfall (`fallIsland`)

One `convert` event (§4.11), as a declared sequence of batches, whatever its author (the engine or a hand; J-V5.11,
J-V5.64):

1. **Footprint.** The island's projected land face in world coordinates (`anchor` + the local ring).
2. **Covered set.** Every ground entity of which at least `landfall.coverShare` of its face or band lies inside the
   footprint, of the kinds of the table below (each read by its face, band or footprint as the table's first column
   says), less every locked or pinned entity and the plots that hold one (kept content, as §3.3's fixed obstacles; the
   ruin laps around them).
3. **Relocation first.** Every use in a covered building or reservation is relocated by §4.11's rule for a step that
   empties a building (a dossier-backed use by the solver's `dossier` request; *Dossier content across epochs*), every
   household by §4.7's overflow pass inside the event — in an epoch, into volume 6's displaced pool with cause `island`
   (V05 §3.15) — the households of the deck plots the table brings down included, before their deck falls. **Another
   island's access under the footprint** (J-V5.83): every `SkyAccess` of another island whose foot or ground landing yard
   lies in the covered set is re-landed in the same batch as a move re-lands it (V05 §4.2) — a slanted access to the
   nearest admissible foot outside the covered set within its row's run; a vertical access, whose foot is its top's world
   point, re-kinded at its top by V05 §3.10 (3) among the admitted kinds whose foot can stand outside it — its new
   landing opened by a positive placement (§7.7's `placeAt`); where no kind fits, F-SKY-ACCESS's ladder runs to its last
   rung (the access dropped, reported; the island keeps at least one access where any rung passes, else it is reported
   `unreachable`), each a listed fate ("<island>'s hoist moved clear of the fallen rock").
4. **The ground** — every covered kind takes the outcome of this one table; no edge of the ground's streets and plots is
   added or removed (the tombstoned deck plots' lines excepted, which go with them), so the plan survives under the
   rubble, ready for regrowth. Each change is a listed fate.

| covered kind (by) | outcome (cause `convert:fallIsland`) | drawn by | cleared or restored by |
|---|---|---|---|
| building (its footprint) | demolished | — | regrowth |
| L3 plot (its face) | use vacant with the `ruin` treatment | §8.2's `fallen` row over the member | regrowth; volume 6's clearance (V06 §3.5.2) |
| reservation — plaza, market, precinct, park, castle bailey (its face) | left vacant under the rubble, the same treatment | the `fallen` row | as a plot |
| hinterland `LandUse` (its face) | kind `quarry`, owner `commons`, `source` the landfall decoration (§2.7): a surface rubble working that sources no underground quarry system, no mine–cavern breach and no dig-down draw (item 2; volume 4's quarry rules read only a `quarry` without a `source`) | the `fallen` row; the quarry's own land-use row | volume 6's quarry face `retypeLand` |
| way (its corridor band) | surface `rubble`, routing class `track`, the class before the fall written to `Way.formerClass` unless the way already carries one (a lane volume 6 downgraded keeps its own; J-V5.73) | its state rows | volume 6's landfall clearance restoring the class from `formerClass` and clearing it (V06 §3.5.2), or a hand `setAttr` of `class` (V06 §2.8) |
| wall stretch (its band), tower, gate of any kind — `main`, `postern`, `water`, `gatehouse`, `castle` (its footprint) | §4.11's wall ladder to `breached` over the covered length, written into the enceinte's `condition` (§2.7, a stretch-state profile, §2.2 rule 5): a knot at each end; a run already `dismantled` kept (the ladder only steps down); the `breached` length extended to take in the chord along the trace of every covered tower's and gate's footprint, so every covered tower's vertex and gate's junction lies inside a `breached` run, and a tower or gate covered while its stretch is not has `breached` written over its footprint's chord alone — a `Tower` or `Gate` carries no state of its own and draws and stands again with its run (J-V5.79); the record's `lifecycle` `decayed` while any run is not `standing` | §8.2's `decayed` row: the gap glyph over each `breached` run | volume 6's repair of a ring in use (V06 §3.7.5) |
| bridge, pier, mole or quay-platform deck (its deck) | `ruined` (the deck falls; under a bridge the river stays one L0 face and the way's crossing is closed) | its state rows | `repairRaised`'s deck restoration (§2.7, item 246) |
| deck plot on a covered deck (its face) | falls with its deck by item 110 (d)'s least disruption: households relocated first (3), building demolished, the plot **tombstoned with the deck** as volume 6 does for a struck deck (V06 §3.19.5 (3)) — never a vacant plot left on a ruined deck; listed as a fate, never a member | nothing | a repaired deck re-offers its faces, the buildings raised on them new lineages (V06 §3.19.5 (1), (4); V02 §3.16 (7)) |
| raised span (its deck band's plan projection, in a ground part ≥ 1) and raised-way pier (its footprint) | `ruined`; a span whose pier is ruined is `ruined` with it (arches fall with their piers, whatever their own cover); a raised way with a ruined span has its crossing closed — it has no junctions with the ways beneath, so the ways' `track` fallback does not apply to it, and the ways beneath it are touched only where the footprint covers them; an aqueduct ruined this way loses its terminal's supply, a listed fate ("the fountain in <square> runs dry"), read by volume 6's raised-way pass at its first `raisedWays` step after the fall — the same epoch for an epoch's fall (V06 §3.19.2 (4)), the next epoch for a hand `convert`; the vaults beneath a span follow its state by volume 4's rule (this volume writes nothing underground) | item 110's state rows (§8.2) | `repairRaised` (V06) |
| `RetainingWall` stretch, generated or drawn (its band) | `decayed` over its covered length — §2.7's `state`, the `breach` op's result (V06 §3.19.4 (3)), a stretch-state profile (§2.2 rule 5); a state, no edge removed; keyed by the wall's lineage address, so a re-derivation of a generated wall keeps it `decayed` | its state rows | an epoch's restoration re-derives it (V06 §3.19.4 (6)); a drawn wall is restored by hand |

5. **The landfall record.** One ground `Decoration` of kind `fallen_island` (§2.7) whose `members` (`DecorationMember[]`;
   J-V5.76) are the covered plots, land uses, reservations, ways and retaining walls, each by its `Id`, and the covered
   raised spans and raised-way piers, each by its raised way and item 244's support keys (`{ raised, span: [a, b] }`,
   `{ raised, support }`) — never a deck plot — `source` the island's address and `name` the grammar's fallen form of its
   name. Each member leaves the list in the batch of the op that ends its cover (volume 6's member-leaving `setAttr`,
   V06 §3.5.2; J-V6.108; V05 §2.6's `members` row). §8.2's `fallen` row draws its rubble over the members' faces and
   bands — a raised member's the span's deck band in plan or the support's `ring`; every other covered kind draws by the
   table's third column. Parts of the footprint over water leave nothing on the ground and are reported ("sank in <water
   body>").
6. **The salvage ledger** is credited with `stone` = the covered land area × `landfall.stoneM3PerM2` m³, `from` the island
   (§2.7's `MaterialLedger`), so later building within the haul radius draws on it and §4.11's salvage demand weighs
   clearance near it.
7. **The sky.** Every entity of the island's arena — its own retaining walls and its bridges' deck plots included — and
   its accesses become tombstones (cause `convert:fallIsland`); each ground landing's use closes (`closeUse`), the
   landing house left vacant to step down the ladder; the island record stays live at its address with `state:
   'fallen'` and `landfall` set, its slot freed.
8. **Undo** restores all of it from the event's undo record (§7.4's convert row) — each covered way's `class`, surface and
   prior `formerClass` (absent again where the fall wrote the first), each covered enceinte's `condition` profile and
   `lifecycle`, each retaining wall's and raised part's former state, and each re-landed access of another island — as
   they stood before the fall.

### 3.17 Failure ladders (the F-SKY rows of §5.2) — never a whole-map retry

| class | where | detection | ladder (in order) | test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-SKY-FILL | S7b (5) | the fill cannot reach `minAreaHa`; an institution's row does not fit | take the next eligible band → try the next reason candidate (V05 §3.4) → close the slot, rows returned to the demand, reported | T-V5-4 |
| F-OUTLINE (base) | S7b (5) | the island's outline is not simple | §5.2's rungs | §13.2's outline property test |
| F-SKY-PLACE | S7b (5) | no candidate passes V05 §3.7 (2) (inside the frame, clear of the other islands, reachable) | halve `gapM` → admit `core` at weight 0.3 → shrink `A` by 20 % and redraw the outline, at most twice → close the slot, rows returned, reported (the reachability test is never relaxed: an island placed by rule always has an admissible access site) | T-V5-5 |
| F-SKY-TERRAIN | SKY | the river's descent stalls or cannot reach the rim | double the tilt → carve a straight bed along the axis to the rim → no river, reported | T-V5-7 |
| F-SKY-ACCESS | SKY, SKY-F, a move, `fallIsland`'s re-landing | no admitted kind at a top; no land foot (a foot or yard meeting a deck face, a raised-way pier or a raised deck's plan within `deckGapM` counts as none); a slanted access under `lineClearM` over a raised deck or raised-way pier; a landing attach unreached by a walkable part-0 way (on the island, or on the ground after S8) | the next direction step of `accessSiting.turnStepCdeg` either way, up to ± `turnMaxCdeg` → the other kinds of the row set → a foot at the nearest admissible land point within twice the row's run → the attach repair lane on its arena (V05 §3.12) → the access dropped, reported (an island keeps at least one where any rung passes; one with none is reported `unreachable` — never an island placed by rule at generation, V05 §3.7 (2)) | T-V5-8 |
| F-SKY-UNIT | the unit run | households the unit run cannot house; an outline face below one district's size (no district) | volume 1's F-UNIT ladder first; the rest re-placed on the ground by §4.7's overflow pass as a relocation, reported | T-V5-9 |
| F-SKY-CLEAR | SKY-F; each epoch's `sky.clearance` (V05 §3.15 (7)); a ground edit beneath (V05 §4.3) | a caster placed after SKY (a ridge, a tower, a crown; a raised part's deck, parapet, channel wall or raised-way pier; a retaining wall's high-side top; a levelled platform; a stepped building's highest massing tier) stands above `groundRefCm` under the solid | raise `groundRefCm` to it keeping `elevationCm`, and raise `elevationCm` only where V05 §3.9 (4)'s floor then fails (a listed fate); `shadeBasis` untouched, reported `shade basis behind: clearance` → a lock holds `elevationCm`, the conflict listed (V05 §3.15 (7)) → at P0, V05 §3.9 (5)'s allowance fallback | T-V5-10, T-V5-21 (h), T-V5-31 (f) |
| F-SKY-CRYPT | SKY (the institution claim), every re-claim, SKY-F's re-test at the levelled datum | no claim site of an `always`-crypt instance passes the crypt test (V05 §3.10 (5)) | the best site by score with the half-sunk crypt (volume 2's basement ladder: full → half → raised), a listed fate → never a missing crypt | T-V5-26 |
| F-SKY-SHADE | the shade evaluations (V05 §3.19 (6)) | a strip's window passes the receiver raster's frame and margin; a sample's shadow is capped (V05 §3.19 (1)) | clip the window to the raster (beyond it there is no field to shade), reported as the clipped share → nothing further: a strip never fails and never stalls the field | T-V5-19 |
| F-SKY-LANDFALL | `fallIsland` | covered content is locked or pinned; a covered use has no alternative site | kept as content the ruin laps around; the use stays in its building, which then stands in the rubble, reported | T-V5-14 |
| F-SKY-HANDOVER | S7b (5) | an institution going up holds an S3 reservation and no hand-over tag is demanded on the ground | the row's `else` (`park`, re-typed with owner `commons`) → never an ownerless reservation | T-V5-4 |

### 3.18 Determinism — every draw on an existing concern (J-V5.12)

| concern | key | creation index | what it draws |
|---|---|---|---|
| `island.place` | the town's lineage | `slot:k`, `reason:k` | the slot openings; the reason |
| `island.place` | the island's lineage (with the epoch component at an epoch) | `template`, `bearing`, `outline`, `place`, `relief`, `river`, `underside.kind`, `underside.depth`, `underside.apex`, `access:k`, `dwell` | the shape, its bearing, its displacement, the placement draw, the own relief class, the river draw, the underside, the access kinds, the dwell spans |
| `terrain.noise` | the island's `Terrain` | per §6.1 | its fBm |
| `water.river` | the island's `Terrain` | per §6.1 | the river's meander knots |
| `field.chance` | the island | per §6.1 | its ε |
| `naming` | the island; the landfall decoration | per §6.3 | names |

**A removed slot draws nothing** (V05 §3.2 (6)): its `slot:k` and `reason:k` indices are skipped, and every other slot's
creation indices are unchanged, so removing an island never moves another island's draws. `sky.clearance`,
`skyAccessObstacles`, the removed-ordinal skip, the strips (V05 §3.19) and the route distance (V05 §3.20) draw nothing:
they are pure functions of committed state (the strips of the persisted `shadeBasis`, never the live island), the model
sampler and `dmath` (§6.5), the strips reading the tiers without their roughness. The island's gradient (V05 §3.11)
draws only its ε on `field.chance`. **Every choice by score or order breaks its ties the same way:** among islands by
slot, then address; among sites and lattice cells by lattice y, then x; among districts by address. The tiers'
roughness uses `dmath` noise keyed by the island's render key — view data outside every digest (§6.2) and the same on
every engine (§6.5). The part number is minted once and recorded in the address (V05 §2.1); `raiseIsland` keys the
island by the town's lineage, the slot and the epoch (J-A8.61), so the same history raises the same island. §6.1's
distinctness test runs a sky-island fixture; §13.3's goldens gain the two sky fixtures of T-V5-15.

### 3.19 The island's shade strip (item 102 on the island's side; J98's underside)

`islandShadeStrip(doc, island, samples, onto) → IslandShadeStrip` (`@plat/sky`, V05 §2.3a) — a pure function, no draw:

1. **The samples.** §4.5's model sampler — its sun positions at the site latitude on the equinox, its sample points per
   32 m field cell, its point-major layout, its reach cap of 8 × the caster's height shared by every model-side source,
   and its digest — read as it stands (J-V5.42, J-V5.44). Each sun gives the horizontal unit vector ŝ toward the sun in
   the model frame (x east, y north) and the shadow reach `c = min(cot altitude, 8)`. Below, "sample `s`" is one sun and
   "the cell's point" one of the sampler's points. Wherever a reader counts "the samples a cell is blocked for" (the
   clerk, the shade map's three areas, the CSV, T-V5-19), it takes the mean over the cell's points of the suns blocked
   at each — with one point, the count itself.
2. **The solid — from the caster's `shadeBasis`, never the live island** (J-V5.47). The tiers without roughness —
   `undersideTiers(basis, { rough: false })`, the view's own function (V05 §3.9 (2)) evaluated on the basis's rim polygon,
   underside, anchor and elevation with the roughness term at 0, so the model never reads view data (J-V5.30); an island
   without a basis has no solid here and casts nothing: tier 0 the rim from the basis's `surfaceTopCm` (V05 §3.9 (4)) down
   to `elevation − rim`, tiers 1 … n down to the apex (none for `flat`). Each tier's ring is rasterised once into a bit
   mask of `shade.ringMaskM` cells in the island's local frame (scanline fill; a cell is in when its centre is), with the
   tier's span [`b_i`, `t_i`] in centimetres above the ground datum.
3. **The receivers.** Onto the ground: the field raster's 32 m cells (§4.5), each at each of the sampler's points `x` in
   it and the ground's height `g(x)` there (bilinear on the committed terrain raster **as the field reads it — S1's
   heights with the T2 terrain strokes, unlevelled**; item 110 (a)'s levelling is a platform datum, never a receiver
   height (§4.7), so a strip computed at S10a, before S10 and S11 level anything, equals the one a load rebuilds after
   them; J-V5.65). Onto an island: the receiving island's 32 m lattice over its land face at the receiver's own
   `shadeBasis` (its anchor and elevation), each at the island's terrain height there plus that datum — a receiver
   without a basis receives no strip; only an island whose apex lies above the receiver's surface top casts onto it — an
   island never shades upward.
4. **The test.** Point `x` is blocked at sample `s` when the ray from (`x`, `g`) toward the sun meets a tier. For each
   tier with `t_i` > `g` (one wholly below the receiver blocks nothing), in order from tier 0 down: the ray's horizontal
   track across the tier's span runs from `x + (max(b_i, g) − g) · c · ŝ` to `x + (t_i − g) · c · ŝ`; it is walked in
   steps of at most `shade.stepM`, both ends included, each point moved into the island's frame (less `anchor`) and looked
   up in the tier's mask; the first hit sets bit (point · suns + `s`) and ends that point's test for that sample. All
   arithmetic is integer centimetres and `dmath` (§6.5), in a fixed order: samples in the sampler's order, cells
   row-major, points in the sampler's order, tiers 0 → n.
5. **The window.** Only cells that can be blocked are tested, sample by sample. The corridor is the solid's bounding box
   (the union of its tier rings) swept away from the sun by `ŝ · c` × [`b_n` − `g_max`, `t_0` − `g_min`] (`g_min`, `g_max`
   the terrain's extremes over the frame — a safe bound), in receiver cells, clipped to the receiver raster
   (F-SKY-SHADE). **Each corridor cell is first pre-tested in O(1) at its own height** `g(x)`: its ray's track across the
   solid's full span, from `x + (b_n − g) · c · ŝ` to `x + (t_0 − g) · c · ŝ`, must meet the solid's bounding box, else
   the cell is skipped without a tier walk — so in mountainous relief the low cells far from the island cost one
   segment–box test each, and no blockable cell is missed (the pre-test is exact for the box, which contains every tier;
   J-V5.59). The strip's stored `window` is the bounding box of the cells that passed the pre-test, over the samples.
6. **When — the shade evaluations, the one schedule** (J-V5.27, J-V5.71, J-V5.72). A **shade evaluation** first writes
   the `shadeBasis` of every island it covers from the island as it then stands (`by` naming the evaluation, `epoch` the
   document's current epoch), then computes every strip from the bases. There are four, and no other writer of a basis or
   a strip:
   (a) **`generation` / `rederive` — SKY's last step**, at generation and at any re-derivation that runs SKY: every live
   island's basis, then the strips onto the islands and `shadeSky`; the strips onto the ground follow at S10a from the
   same bases.
   (b) **`rederive` — S10a's start**, at a re-derivation starting after SKY and at or before S10a: before step (i) and
   every hook, the bases of every live island, then the strips onto the islands — never by pass 2, so no hook of S10a
   reads a strip built from the old bases.
   (c) **`epoch` — each epoch's S6 re-evaluation**, for every island not fallen or removed, from the island as it stands
   when the epoch begins (V05 §3.15).
   (d) **`update` — Update shade now** (V05 §4.2), for its one island.
   **The strips onto the ground are computed once per evaluation, at their first read** — volume 1's S9b at S10a's
   `afterDrivers` hook, between step (i) and field pass 2, where it is registered (V01 §3.5.5), else field pass 2 — by the
   one pure call, and **held** for pass 2's fold and every later reader of the same evaluation (the hood pins' shade gate
   and `DistrictContext.shade.islandShare`, V01 §3.8.2, §2.3): S9b's strips and pass 2's are the same held strips, bit for
   bit (T-V5-21 (h)). An island without a `shadeBasis` — raised or declared since the last evaluation — casts and
   receives no strip until the next evaluation writes one. The strips are computed whatever `w_sun` is (0 included) — the
   fold multiplies by the weight — so the shade view always reads held strips (V05 §5.1). **Never** by an edit, a scoped
   regeneration of an island or SKY-F: an island edit leaves every basis and every strip where they were until one of
   these evaluations, and on load or replay the strips are rebuilt from the persisted bases (§2.9). **A ground terrain
   edit** (T2 on the ground key, a terrain conversion of §4.11) moves the receiver, not the caster: over its effective
   scope it re-reads every strip's bits there from the unchanged bases, with the field it recomputes, so the session's
   strips always equal a load's rebuild (T-V5-21 (h)); item 110 (a)'s levelling moves no receiver at all.
7. **The fold into the field** (J-V5.38). For each ground cell, each bit of the daylight term's mask — pass 1's terrain
   and wall shade and, after an epoch, volume 6's building shade mask on the same layout (V06 §3.4 (6)) — is OR-ed with
   the same bit of every strip covering the cell, so a point shaded by a hill, a house and an island at the same sample
   counts once; the term's shade share is the mask's popcount ÷ `bits` (§4.5). The **island-free value** — the same
   composition with the strips left out, composed at an epoch's evaluation and never stored — is what the supply law reads
   at an epoch (V05 §3.15 (1)); at generation S7b (5) reads pass 1, which has no island bits by construction (item 102
   (c)). **On an island** the same fold makes `shadeSky`: the strips onto it OR-ed, sample by sample, with — after an
   epoch — volume 6's building shade mask run on the island's 32 m lattice, the island's own buildings casting onto its
   cells by the same law and cap (item 102 (b); J-V5.33; V06 §3.4 (8)).
8. **The basis.** Each strip records the §6.2 hash of the bases it read (V05 §2.3a). The shade map view (volume 9) and the
   clerk compare the caster's with the hash of the same fields read from the live island: equal — the field shows the
   island where it hangs; different — the field still reads it where it hung at the last evaluation, the view draws the
   current strip as pending (V05 §5.1; the one reader of the live island's shade) and the clerk answers "The ground
   beneath will catch up the next time the town changes over time", offering **Update shade now** (V05 §4.2, §4.5).
9. **What it amounts to** (the owner's "not a hard and fast rule"). The strip only sets bits; the weight is §4.5's
   `w_sun` (item 111 (c)'s climate cell sets it per climate row). With the sampler's default at latitude 50° and the
   equinox the samples stand at 6.4°, 18.7°, 29.5°, 37.2° and 40° (computed from the solar geometry): the five midday
   ones reach 1.2 to 1.8 times a caster's height, the mid-morning and mid-afternoon ones about 3 times, the first and last
   are capped at 8 times. So an island whose underside hangs 100–160 m above its ground blocks the cells just north of its
   footprint for about three to five of the nine samples and sweeps far to the west and east for one sample each at the
   day's ends: its under-hang is a crescent north of it (south of it in the southern hemisphere), lowered by a third to a
   half of item 102's all-day amount. A `root` underside lengthens the crescent under the low samples and a `flat` one
   shortens it — J98 carried into the model as it is into the picture (V05 §5.3). This paragraph is a derivation from the
   defaults, PLAUSIBLE until T-V5-19 measures it.

### 3.20 The siting rulebook on and across the island (items 103 and 104 where they reach islands)

The one placement solver (§4.7 S10b) places everything on an island, in the unit run, with item 103's rulebook as every
other district reads it; this volume adds only what the rulebook cannot know — that the island is reached by a ride, and
what an island never carries.

1. **The route distance** `skyRoute(doc, from, to) → { lengthCm: Cm; access: Id }` (`@plat/sky`; the implementer of
   §4.7's distances between arenas; J-V5.36). From a point `p` on island I to a class `c` on the ground: the least, over
   I's admitted accesses `a`, of `d_I(p, attach_a) + transferM + run_a + d_G(foot_a, c)` — `d_I(p, attach_a)` the island
   leg, the straight distance × the rung's detour factor (§4.7's `street` law for a point-to-point leg); `transferM` the
   route's allowance for the wait and the loading (`routing.transferM`); `run_a` the access's run (V05 §2.2); and
   `d_G(foot_a, c)` the base's mode distance from the foot to the class (§4.7 S10b's *Supply distances by mode*): the
   `street` distance (the ground's class distance raster for `c` read at the foot, × the detour factor) where the foot and
   the class stand on the same side of the town wall, else the `road` distance through the gate by which the class is
   reached. A class on the island itself is read on the island's own class distance rasters, which the unit run's S10b
   builds as the base's does. `access` is the access of the least sum (ties by access ordinal), which the clerk names
   (8). From a ground point `q` to a class on an island: the same sum taken the other way; inside the pooled S10b —
   before the island's own placement exists — the island's class stands at the island's land centroid
   (`d_I(attach_a, centroid)`), so the ground pool reads only what exists before it (§4.1's laziness contract); after
   generation (post-build requests, epochs) the island's placed plots are used. Between two islands: through the ground,
   both legs and two transfers. A `river` or `sea` supply edge never crosses arenas (its distance is ∞): the island's
   conjured river and spring pond carry no trade and **satisfy no NEEDS row** — a class needing running water, a river
   bank or a harbour finds none on an island (J-V5.23; V05 §3.5 (3) (a)).
2. **Admitted accesses.** An access in state `open` (its `unserved` reading included); never a `closed` or `ruined` one.
   A `secret` access admits only the pulls of classes whose clientele is clandestine (item 103 (a)); a `guarded` access
   only its institution's own supply edges and the law-and-enforcement classes' rows.
3. **Which families read it.** The route distance serves SUPPLY (the supply edges under the `street` and `road` modes),
   CLIENTELE (the island's `dist.market`, `dist.plaza` and `dist.gate`, V05 §3.11), COMPETITION and COVERAGE (the
   distance between members; the route to the ground passes every walking radius, which is why a walking-radius row
   counts the island as one unit, V05 §3.5 (3)), ALIGNMENT (law against crime, sacred against profane, elite against
   common: people walk and ride) and every signature pair of item 103 (b) whose two ends stand on different arenas. The
   nuisance families (EMITS against SENSITIVE-TO; upwind and downwind) read the straight distance in three dimensions —
   the horizontal distance and the elevation between the two points — since smoke and smell rise and noise carries
   through the air; upstream and downstream never cross arenas.
4. **What an island never carries** — the rows of `IslandRule.carry.never` (V05 §3.5 (3) (a)); what may split is (11)'s.
5. **The landings.** `sky_landing` and `sky_landing_top` are placed by their accesses (V05 §3.10), never by a pull; their
   siting facts (V05 §2.4) act on their neighbours through the families, so noise-sensitive homes keep a little away from
   a hoist house and the classes that serve the island gather near its landing yard by the route distance.
6. **Custom institutions** (item 104). A custom kind on an island reads its resolved profile as any class does; the
   island institution rows take it by its scene archetype (V05 §3.4). Nothing custom can stall the sky: an unplaceable
   preference is reported with the closest options, as item 104 (d) requires.
7. **The island's own placement is not the solver's** (J-V5.34). V05 §3.7's score places the island as one body at S7b
   (5), before any plot exists, so it reads no family; its purpose terms are the island-scale reading of those
   institutions' profiles, and volume 11 writes their values with the profile as their provenance.
8. **"Why is this here"** (item 103 (e); V05 §4.5): a use on an island names the rows that pulled it, and a pull that
   crossed arenas names the landing it went through and the route length ("near the mill below, by the east hoist, 340 m
   by route").
9. **Supply chains across the island** (item 104 (h); J-V5.39). An ACTIVE custom chain (item 104 (b)) — and, for a
   dossier-backed town, each of the dossier's `activeChains[]`, its processors resolved by kind family (item 105 (i)) — is
   placed as one connected thing by the base's chain rules; the island changes five things and nothing else.
   (a) **What goes up.** The sky fill never carries a chain's RESOURCE node — a resource sits at its terrain feature on
   the ground (item 104 (h) (1)); the island's conjured river and spring pond carry no trade (1) and are never a chain's
   feature — nor its PROCESSING nodes, whose demand stays on the ground with the chain's working steps (V05 §3.5 (3)). Its
   FINAL SELLER and its CONSUMERS follow V05 §3.5 (3)'s proportional share like any class. The one exception is the
   island's own institution (V05 §3.4): a custom kind carried as a `tower`, `palace` or `cathedral` that is also a chain
   node, at any step, stays on its island and keeps every link by the route.
   (b) **The links cross by the route.** Every link between a node on an island and a node on the ground is a supply edge
   of mode `street` measured by `skyRoute` (1) — so the chain's last ground node leans toward the landing foot whose route
   to the island's node is shortest, and the island's node toward that landing's top. Item 104 (h) (2)'s natures act on
   the route as on a street: a perishable link's short reach holds its ground end close to the landing; a bulky link's
   water and main-road preferences never cross arenas (a `river` or `sea` leg is ∞, (1)); a valuable link's security pull
   counts a `guarded` landing as a secure site and an `open` public one as none.
   (c) **Order.** Placement runs upstream first (item 104 (h) (4)). At generation the island's unit run reads a ground
   supplier at the block whose S10a allocation holds it (§4.7 S10b's *Supply distances by mode*), and the ground pool
   reads an island node at the island's land centroid (1); a post-build request and an epoch read the placed plots on both
   arenas. A chain's final seller on an island stands at a smaller route distance to its market than the chain's resource
   does, in at least the share the base's ground-only bar declares for the same chain (T-V5-24).
   (d) **Shape** (item 104 (h) (3) as revised by item 107 (e)). A chain whose in-town nodes lie on two arenas sits close at
   street scale on the ground and STRETCHES through its landing, the landing one step on its road; it is never read as
   close across the arenas. It is named a quarter only past item 72's quarter-identity threshold, or by the dossier or
   the author — and then on the ground (volume 1's reading); no quarter is ever assigned to it by rule.
   (e) **Endpoints, services, change, declarations.** An import or export endpoint's store stands at the ground's gate or
   quay facing the partner (item 104 (h) (6)), never at a landing. A custom service `providedBy` an island institution
   sits inside it on the island (item 104 (h) (5)). A chain that deactivates leaves its island nodes where they stand,
   their pulls removed; one that activates places its new nodes against the standing ones, upstream first, an island node
   by the solver's positive placement on the island's arena (§7.7's `placeAt`) — never a unit run (V01 §3.10.3 (6)), never
   by moving, resizing or re-deriving an island (item 104 (h) (7)). An author's map-side declaration of a custom kind onto
   an island (item 104 (a) (1)) is T6's `assign` with `criteria.sky` (V05 §4.2), keyed by the kind's `localUid`, and is
   held as every declaration is — it may place a processing node up, the author's word outranking (a), with the fate
   "this chain now crosses the hoist" listed at its dry run.
10. **The supply web across the arenas** (item 105 (a)–(e); J-V5.50). Every link binds, pulls and explains across an access
   exactly as on the ground, with the route as its distance and the access as the place where its end is felt:
   (a) **Binding.** Each consumer member binds to its NEAREST provider member of the good by the edge's mode (item 105
   (a), item 107 (c)), measured by `skyRoute` when the two stand on different arenas, ties by provider address.
   (b) **Both ends pulled, at the access.** An island consumer counts toward its ground provider's weighted centre (item
   105 (b)) at the **foot** of the access its route used, weighted as its link; a ground consumer bound to an island
   provider counts at that access's **top** — never at a world position beneath the island, where no goods go.
   (c) **Orientation, several outputs, hubs** (item 105 (b)–(e)). A class's orientation and its per-good pulls act across
   the arenas through the same substitution; a balanced class's "junction where its input and output routes meet" may be
   a landing — its foot on the ground, its top on the island — scored, never forced.
   (d) **Status.** A link of one of the dossier's `activeChains[]` that is `impaired` or `vulnerable` weakens by item 105
   (i)'s status table on the route as on a street (9).
11. **Split works and shops across the arenas** (item 105 (h); J-V5.51). A split class carried by V05 §3.5 (3) (c) is one
   item on two plots on two arenas: its works on the ground by its nuisance and input rows, its shop on the island by its
   output and clientele rows, the shop's `Use.branchOf` naming the works' use; the route between them is its own link.
   Every lifecycle path covers both, as item 105 (h) requires: creation by S10b as one item (the pooled S10b places the
   works, the island's unit run the shop, reading the works at its S10a block by (12) (a)); a state, closure or removal of
   the institution reaches both; a relocation moves each by its own rows; a fall or a delete of the island relocates the
   shop (the works stays); undo restores both; the author or the clerk may merge them back onto one plot by a map-side
   declaration (on the ground; up only by an author's held T6 declaration, with the fate listed); migration: an absent
   `branchOf` is no split. Every split across the arenas is reported.
12. **Order, the district term and spread, per arena** (items 105 (f), (g) and 107; J-V5.52).
   (a) **Order and relaxation.** At generation each arena runs item 105 (f)'s order and its one bounded relaxation on its
   own: the pooled S10b on the ground, the unit run's S10b on each island. Each reads **only the other arena's proxies** —
   the ground reads an island's classes at its land centroid ((1)), the island reads a ground provider at the block its
   S10a allocation holds ((9) (c)) — so neither order waits on the other and both relaxations are byte-stable whatever
   the worker's interleaving (T-V5-22 (j)). After generation, both read the placed plots on both arenas, the relaxation
   of one arena holding the other's placements fixed.
   (b) **The district term** (item 105 (g); J-V5.75). S7b (1)'s supply term reads a sky pseudo-district as one consumer
   location at its island's **land centroid's world point** (`anchor` + the outline's centroid, fixed by S7b (5)'s
   placement, V05 §3.7) (§4.5 S7b (1)) — the proxy the ground pool reads for an island's classes ((1)) — since the landing
   feet are sited only at SKY, after S7b. Its carried classes' links are scored there against the providers already
   allocated, by the centroid distance volume 1's χ̃ reads at S7c (V01 §3.2 (5)) — no route, since no access exists yet —
   at the term's low weight, never past J-A72.46's dispersal.
   (c) **Near enough** (item 107 (b)). A cross-arena link's satisfaction plateaus on the route length: full within the
   good's `comfortM` of the summed legs (`transferM` counted inside), decaying to 0 at `maxDistanceM`.
   (d) **Spread** (item 107 (a), (d), (f)). Coverage counts an island's districts as districts (V05 §3.5 (3); J-V5.37), and
   a spreading class's same-kind spacing applies inside each island district as inside a ground district; an everyday
   service's link to its supplier across the arenas keeps its generous `comfortM`, so an island apothecary is never pulled
   down to the herbalist below.
   (e) **Quarters never assigned** (item 107 (e)). No island district is made a quarter by rule. A dossier QUARTER record
   (item 108 (a)) or district-defining record (item 72) binds to ground districts when records bind, before any island
   district exists; the author may bind one to an island district by T5 with `sky` and the record's kind (V05 §4.2), held
   with its fate.

### 3.21 The rim as a barrier (item 106 where it reaches islands; J-V5.49)

An island is barrier all round: its rim is a sheer drop, and its only approaches are its landing yards.
1. **The rim is a closure arc.** For item 106 (a)'s closure and every "enclosed" or "sealed" test, the island's `rim`
   chain is a natural barrier of barrier kind `sheer` — one of the six closure-arc kinds (§2.7's `BarrierKind`; J-VI.3) —
   its defensibility class volume 11's `walls.barriers` row keyed `sheer` (wall `none`): closure only, no wall. The clerk's `closure` answer
   names it as barrier `sheer` (volume 10's `WhyAnswer.closure`).
2. **Generation never walls an island.** No stage lays an enceinte on a sky arena; the island's defence is its rim and
   its landings. A prison island's is its rim plus its `guarded` landing, whose role already admits only its
   institution's and the law-and-enforcement classes' rows (V05 §3.20 (2)).
3. **An author's wall on an island** (T3 `wall` in one of its **enclosure** kinds with the `key` parameter naming the
   island; `drawWall` with `key`, V05 §4.2) is an `Enceinte` of closure `barrier` on the island's arena — T3's
   `retaining` kind and a T2 terrace stroke's edge are never enclosures and lay an island `RetainingWall` instead (V05
   §3.22 (4)): its approachable arcs are the landing yards' frontages — each landing yard is closed across its inner side
   by one stretch on the rim strip's `setback` line, both ends anchored on the rim (a tower at each anchor, item 106 (a)'s
   anchor rule) and a gate at the yard's attach — and every other arc of the rim is closed by the barrier, with zero wall
   edges. The tool snaps the stroke's ends to the rim and shows the rim arcs as part of the circuit (item 106 (g)); a
   stroke drawn along the rim away from a landing is bent at its dry run to the landing stretches only
   (`bend.wall.drop`, V05 §4.2), and a stroke that crosses the island's interior is an inner wall laid as drawn (a
   castle's or a close's, §4.8's furniture). The stretch profile (item 106 (c)) grades each stretch: the full curtain
   across a landing yard, the strongest towers where it meets the rim. Adding or removing an access re-evaluates the
   circuit by item 22's least disruption: a new landing gets its stretch and gate, a removed one's stretch is removed
   (listed fates).
4. **The clerk** explains an unwalled rim: "the drop is the wall here" (V05 §4.5).
5. **The no-op pin.** With item 106's `barrier` closure and the stretch profile off, an author's island wall is the base's
   ring along the rim strip's line, byte-identical to the base (T-V5-27).

### 3.22 Item 110 on, under and against the island (the owner's "they go in")

The base carries item 110 (a)–(d) (§2.7, §4.3, §4.7); volume 1 carries terraced wards and the unit run's levelling,
volume 2 stepped massing and deck buildings, volume 4 the vaults under raised ways, and volume 6 all of it over epochs.
The island meets item 110 in the places below; each rule here is this volume's, and none of it changes a ground value
while the island setting is off.

1. **Casters under an island.** Everything item 110 raises above the ground is a caster to the island's clearance: a
   raised part's deck top with its parapet, or an aqueduct's channel wall, over the deck's plan, and its raised-way piers
   (item 110 (b)); a retaining wall's high-side top and a levelled platform's datum where fill raises it (item 110 (a));
   a stepped building's highest massing tier, read from volume 2's stack envelope (item 110 (c); V02 §3.15.2). They are
   read at SKY for what exists then (V05 §3.9 (3); every generated raised part, §4.3's slot), at SKY-F for what the pool
   and the unit runs placed (V05 §3.9 (5)), after every epoch's growth (`sky.clearance`, V05 §3.15 (7)), and by any edit
   that adds one beneath (V05 §4.3). Their heights are volume 11's raised-way, terrace and massing-tier rows; the caster
   allowance's P0 measurement runs with them on (V05 §3.9 (5)).
2. **The island yields, never the city** (J-V5.68). No raised part, retaining wall or building is moved, lowered or
   refused because an island floats above it: the island's `groundRefCm` rises to clear it and its elevation follows
   only where the floor needs it. The raised-way generator treats an island's projected footprint as open air; only the
   accesses constrain its courses ((3)). A caster removed beneath an island re-reads `groundRefCm` and never lowers the
   island (V05 §4.3).
3. **The accesses and the decks** (J-V5.60, J-V5.61).
   (a) **Feet on part-0 land only.** Admissible ground for a foot, a landing yard and S7b (5)'s reachability transform
   excludes every raised-way pier footprint, every raised deck's plan dilated by `accessSiting.deckGapM` and every deck
   face of item 110 (d) (V05 §3.7 (2), §3.10 (3)–(4)); "the nearest way" is a walkable part-0 way.
   (b) **Rope lines clear the decks.** A `ropeway` or `lightBridge` keeps `accessSiting.lineClearM` over every raised deck
   and raised-way pier its segment crosses, at SKY and after (V05 §3.10 (3)).
   (c) **The accesses as overhead obstacles** (§4.3). Every raised part laid after SKY reads `skyAccessObstacles(doc)`
   (V05 §2.3b) through its two callers: item 110 (b)'s course solver — at an epoch's raised-way pass (V06) and at a new
   stage's S4 in an epoch (no generation stage after SKY lays a raised part, §4.3's slot) — which keeps the deck, its
   parapet and its raised-way piers out of every column dilated by `deckGapM` and under every segment by its `clearCm`,
   re-routing by its own ladder and otherwise not building the part (reported "would cut <island>'s hoist"); and T4
   `road`'s `raised` dry run (and the aqueduct class), which re-routes or, failing that, ends the course at its last
   ground vertex before the column (`bend.raised.hoist`, J-VI.4 (f)). An access placed or moved later (`placeSkyAccess`,
   a move, `setAttr(ground.at)`) meets the standing raised parts by (a) and (b) at its own dry run.
   (d) **No raised way reaches an island.** A raised part's two ends are ramps or stairs joined to ground vertices (item
   110 (b)); a T4 `raised` or aqueduct stroke that ends on an island's rim makes no way and is bent into a sky access
   (V05 §4.2; J-VI.4 (d)), so II.1's "sky: no portals, no roads in or out" stands. **No raised part stands on an
   island**: item 110 (b)'s decks live in ground parts ≥ 1, and the sky layer has one part per island (V05 §2.1); a T4
   `raised` or aqueduct stroke on an island's key is bent (J-VI.4 (e)). **No access is a way** (V05 §2.2).
4. **The island's own retaining walls** (J-V5.69). Volume 1's unit run levels island ground by item 110 (a)'s thresholds
   (`terrace.slopeMin`, `terrace.stepMinCm`, volume 11's rows, read unchanged), which on the island's gentle dome and
   quarter-amplitude echo (V05 §3.8 (3)) bite mainly on an `own` relief of a steep class; a terraced edge is laid only
