   where the island's relief class — `terrainEcho.reliefClass` for `own`, `plain` for `echo` and `none` (V01 §3.17.7
   (2)) — asks for terraces. Each retaining wall it raises — and each one an author draws with T3's `retaining` kind or as
   a T2 terrace stroke's edge with the island's `key` — is a `RetainingWall` on the island's arena (§2.7: a chain on the
   arena whose terrain it levels). **Never on the rim strip or along the rim:** the levelling stops at the rim strip's
   `setback` line, and a retaining stroke that meets the rim strip or runs along the rim is bent inward at its dry run
   (`bend.retaining.rim`, J-VI.4 (g)) — every point of the stroke lying in the rim strip moves along the island's radial
   direction to 1 m inside the strip's `setback` line, the other points unchanged (V09 §4.20.9). It is never an
   enclosure, never snapped to the rim and never a member of a barrier circuit (V05 §3.21 (3)). It draws in the island's
   frame and is displaced with it under `offset` (§8.4), its shadow on its low side; it tombstones with the island at a
   fall (V05 §3.16 (7)) and exports in the island's `sky` collection (V05 §5.5). The surface top and SKY-F's crypt re-test
   read the levelled heights through `levelledGroundCm` (V05 §3.9 (4), §3.10 (5)). A way that crosses it becomes a stair or
   a ramp by item 110 (a)'s way-class rule, on the island as on the ground.
5. **What the raised-way generator reads of the sky** (§4.3; J-V5.63).
   (a) **Endpoints bind to reservations.** An elevated road's endpoints are the reservations — the citadel's site, a great
   temple's or cathedral's precinct — never the institutions in them. S7b (5)'s hand-over keeps the reservation (V05
   §3.5), so a road raised to a precinct whose cathedral later rose to an island stands, and its reason reads through the
   hand-over ("links the citadel and the old cathedral close, whose cathedral rose to <island>").
   (b) **Ground arena only.** At an epoch and in every post-build request, the generator reads ground-arena entities only
   (`PlatKey.layer` `'ground'`, any part) — for its endpoints and for its evidence — never a sky ward, an island
   institution or an island landmark. The test is by arena, never by a list of `sky-*` kinds.
   (c) **The water need by arena.** At generation the aqueduct's need reads the S2 population whole, since S7b (5) has not
   run — a small, deterministic overestimate (a full island carries at most 3 × `H_d` households). At an epoch it reads
   the ground households only: an island's households are never in a ground water need, its water being its own — its
   conjured river or spring pond (V05 §3.8) and any well the unit run sinks on the island's own arena, which reads nothing
   of the ground — and no aqueduct serves an island.
   (d) **Ground hydrology only.** The aqueduct's source search reads S1's springs and rivers on the ground terrain (an
   oasis's spring among them, item 111); an island's conjured river, spring pond and waterfall are never "a higher spring
   or upstream river", and no aqueduct terminal, basin, cistern or fountain stands on an island.
6. **Stepped massing and deck plots on the island.** An island institution may take volume 2's stepped rows (a stepped
   temple, a terraced palace, a tiered tower) through its composition and class rows, as on the ground; the surface top
   reads each point's highest massing tier (V05 §3.9 (4)), and a cathedral's crypt lies under its lowest massing tier,
   inside underside tier 0 (V05 §3.10 (5)). The island's bridges over its conjured river (V01 §3.10.3 (4)) may carry deck
   plots by item 110 (d)'s mechanism, and they fall with the island (V05 §3.16 (7)).
7. **Removals.** A removed generated raised way, retaining wall or deck plot beneath an island holds by §2.8's
   suppression; this volume adds only the island's own form — an island or an access that stays removed (V05 §3.2 (6),
   §3.10 (1), §4.2).
8. **The landfall** covers raised spans, raised-way piers, retaining walls and deck plots (V05 §3.16's table).
9. **The no-op pin and the declared shift** (J-V5.67). With item 110's terrace, raised-way and deck-plot rows off, every
   island value — its altitude, ground reference, accesses, strips and landfall — is byte-identical to this volume without
   item 110. With them on at their defaults (the owner's default at CITY and METROPOLIS), both sky goldens of T-V5-15
   change once, a declared shift naming both fixtures.

### 3.23 Edges — every mechanism at its limits (principle (11))

Every mechanism of V05 §2–§5 has a defined outcome at the limits of its inputs, a rung on its failure ladder (V05 §3.17;
§5.2's order: check, repair locally, retry that piece, simpler shape, substitute, leave out and report — never a
whole-map retry) and a test. The rules below cover whole edge classes; the tables give every other case.

**(a) Degenerate geometry** (§5.1's general rule). Every sky step that commits geometry — the outline, the arena's L0
batch, the rim strip and claims, the ground landing claims, the tops' `splitThrough`, the resize's buffer, the landfall's
state writes — commits through kernel transactions: a degenerate result (a zero-length or collinear rim run, a
self-touching outline, a zero-area face, coincident points) is refused by the kernel (§3.1's `E_DEGENERATE` family) and
taken by the step's row of V05 §3.17: an outline by F-OUTLINE's rungs, then F-SKY-PLACE's shrink and redraw, then the
slot closed; a rim strip that would self-touch (a crescent's horn narrower than 2 × `rimStripM`) is laid on the
horn's inner offset where it exists and omitted along the horn where it does not, the strip face then several faces,
none cut or plotted; a landing yard of zero depth (a top on a horn) by F-SKY-ACCESS's next direction step; a claim with
no site by the institution floor (V05 §4.2) at SKY by F-SKY-FILL's next reason candidate. **As input:** an underside tier
whose ring falls below 1 % of the land's area ends the stack (V05 §3.9 (2)); a `flat` underside has tier 0 alone; a
tier ring the erosion empties is no tier; a strip with no cell passing the pre-test is an empty strip (`cells` 0,
`window` empty) and sets no bit; two feet that coincide keep `footGapM` apart by the gap rule; a raised stroke of zero
length makes no way and emits nothing; a landfall footprint wholly over water leaves nothing on the ground ("sank in
<water body>") and its covered set is empty — the island still falls (V05 §3.16 (7)); a footprint partly beyond the
ground frame covers only what lies inside it, the rest reported as over water is. **The draw and the exports skip a
degenerate mark** — a zero-area land face, a zero-length rim run or access line, an empty strip — and never fail a tile
or a file. Test: T-V5-33 (a).

**(b) Rungs.**

| rung | the sky |
|---|---|
| THORP, HAMLET, VILLAGE (the village product) | no island: the gate is `none` at every village rung (V05 §3.2 (1)); SKY registers and writes nothing; the tools' sky parameters are hidden and a T5 `sky` declaration is bent to a ground quarter (`bend.island.gate`, V05 §4.2) |
| TOWN | as the village rungs (the gate); an epoch whose population crosses down from CITY makes every island's target 0 — they wane, settle and fall over their dwells (V05 §3.15 (6)); a document at TOWN may still carry `fallen` and `removed` records, which draw a landfall and nothing respectively |
| CITY | whole: at most three islands, `areaHaMax` 10 ha, every rule of V05 §3 |
| METROPOLIS, to its population cap | whole at the METROPOLIS row (`areaHaMax` 20 ha); at the cap nothing grows further: the supply law saturates at `h` = 1, the count at three, the fill at `fill.householdCapDistricts` × `H_d`; the budgets are V05 §6's METROPOLIS column |
| a standalone neighbourhood document | islands off (V01's J-V1.36); the sky parameters hidden |

Test: T-V5-34 (a).

**(c) Toggles and presets.** The islands **setting** off: no allocation, SKY writes nothing, the ground byte-identical to
the realism golden (T13-1); on with the gate failed: the report's sentence (V05 §3.2 (5)). The **view toggle** (the sky
`LayerSpec.visible`) hidden: the islands exist and carry their households, no sky mark, label, light or slab is drawn and
the hidden layer's casters light nothing on the visible layers (§8.4), so the drawn ground carries no island shadow while
the field's strips (model data) are unchanged and no digest moves (T15-1). **Occlusion** `opaque`, `ghost`, `offset`
and a per-island override: V05 §5.2–§5.4. **The parity presets** (`MFCG`, `FTG`) hold the setting off (J-V5.25); every
shipped sheet still resolves every sky semantic. **The no-op pins:** `w_sun` 0 (the strips computed and held, the field
unchanged; T-V5-19 (c)); item 103's coverage weight 0, items 105 and 107's weights at 0 (T-V5-22 (b), (k)); item 106's
closure off (T-V5-27); item 110's rows off (V05 §3.22 (9)); item 111's oasis chance, water pull and climate cell at their
neutral values (T-V5-37); the sampler at four points per cell (§4.5's fallback: the strips' `bits` and the OR follow,
T-V5-21 (f)). **The shade map view** on and off: the view reads the held strips and computes a pending strip only while
shown (V05 §6). **The underground layer** on or off changes nothing of the sky (item 2). **Exports:** the model and
interchange exports (`.plat`, JSON, the compact twin, CSV) carry the islands whatever the view; the picture and scene
exports take the layer set the export request names (§11.1), the view's visibility by default. Test: T-V5-34 (b).

**(d) No input, one input, and the caps.**

| mechanism | none | one | the cap |
|---|---|---|---|
| the record (V05 §2.1) | no island: `removedSlots` empty, no sky part minted | one island: slot 1, part 1 | three live islands; `nextSkyPart` unbounded (a spent part is never reused) |
| the access record (V05 §2.2) | an island with no access after F-SKY-ACCESS's last rung: reported `unreachable`, its landings none, its households kept, `skyRoute` ∞ to the ground so every cross-arena pull falls to its fallback (the class served on its own arena, or unserved and reported) | one access: `main`; deleting it is bent to a move (`bend.access.last`) | generated ≤ `accessCount.max` per island; hand-placed unbounded (V05 §2.3b; J-V5.84), each foot `footGapM` apart |
| the unit inputs (V05 §2.3) | an island with no landing and no claim: `claims` and `landings` empty, the run lays its fabric unreached by any attach, SKY-F reports F-SKY-ACCESS's last rung | one landing: one attach the fabric must reach | ≤ `accessCount.max` landings and one claim |
| the strip (V05 §2.3a) | no island, or no island with a basis: no strip, the field's pass 2 equals pass 1's daylight term alone | one island: its strips onto the ground and onto any lower island | three casters; the window clipped to the raster (F-SKY-SHADE) |
| the obstacles (V05 §2.3b) | no live access: `[]`, the solver unconstrained | one entry | V05 §2.3b's size rule |
| the rule tables (V05 §2.4) | a table version without a cell reads its default; an empty `institutions` list offers `prosperity` alone; an empty `access` row set admits no kind — every top falls to F-SKY-ACCESS's last rung and the island is reported `unreachable` | one row: the draw takes it with certainty | the rows as V05 §2.4 types them |
| the supplies (V05 §3.3) | no built district cell: `S_town` 0, `h_town` 0, no island; no carried household: `S_carried` 0 and `A` 0, the slot closes | one cell or one household: its value | `h` clamps at 1 |
| purpose (V05 §3.4) | no candidate: the slot closes `no supply` or `nothing to carry` | one candidate: chosen with certainty | three slots |
| the fill (V05 §3.5) | no eligible band: the slot closes (F-SKY-FILL) | one band, or one household: the fill stops at the first of its stop causes | `fill.householdCapDistricts` × `H_d`; 100 batches (T-V5-4) |
| shape and placement (V05 §3.6–§3.7) | no candidate passes: F-SKY-PLACE | one candidate: taken | `topK` best; three islands `gapM` apart |
| the arena (V05 §3.8) | no ground river: no conjured river, a spring pond by its draw; `none` terrain: the dome alone | one crossing site: one span if it qualifies | the frame grows by §3.2 (9) for an area past 1.5 × `areaHaMax` |
| the underside and altitude (V05 §3.9) | a `flat` island: tier 0 alone, no root; no caster beneath: the ceiling is `casterAllowanceM` | one tier | `underside.tiers` tiers; `maxDepthM`; `altitudeM`'s top, floored above it only where the root needs it |
| access siting (V05 §3.10) | no admitted kind at a top: F-SKY-ACCESS | one top | `accessCount.max`; ± `turnMaxCdeg` of direction steps |
| the field (V05 §3.11) | a lattice with no cell (land all rim strip): no gradient, `field` the institution's or the carried band's `v`, the unit run makes no district (F-SKY-UNIT) | one cell: the richest carried band's inner range's midpoint | the island's lattice (≤ 400 cells) |
| the unit run (V05 §3.12) | no district (an outline face below one district's size): F-SKY-UNIT, the allocation re-placed on the ground | one district | three districts |
| lights (V05 §3.13) | a rim shorter than one spacing: one orb at the rim's first vertex; `h_carried` 0: every magical light 0 | one access: one lantern | ≤ 60 lights per island |
| names (V05 §3.14) | no nearby district, no river: the name from the reason and the altitude class alone | one island | three island names, unique town-wide (§9.6) |
| epochs (V05 §3.15) | no island and every slot empty: step 5 draws | one island | a century of epochs: every rate per year, bounded by the dwell spans |
| the landfall (V05 §3.16) | an empty covered set: nothing on the ground, the island falls | one covered entity | every ground entity under a 20 ha footprint (V05 §6's `fallIsland` row) |
| the route (V05 §3.20) | no admitted access: ∞ | one access: its leg | three accesses per generated island; hand-placed accesses add legs, the least sum still one pass over them |
| the rim barrier (V05 §3.21) | no landing: an author's wall has no stretch, the rim closes the circuit alone | one landing: one stretch and one gate | one stretch per landing |
| the clerk (V05 §4.5) | a map with no island: `why` on the sky answers with the gate or the report's sentence | one island | three islands |
| marks, labels, exports (V05 §5) | no island: no sky group, no sky collection feature, no sky scene | one island: one group, one scene | three islands and their accesses |

Test: T-V5-33 (b), (c).

**(e) Missing, empty or contradictory data — the dossier included** (items 20, 20 (h), 22). No dossier: no node term, the
magic circumstance the recipe's; a dossier with no `site.magic` resolves `magic` by §7.7's default and the gate reads it;
`site.magic` `none` with the setting on: the setting refused (the one dossier-owned refusal, V05 §4.4); a dossier change
to `none` at an absorb: every island's target 0 at the next epoch — they wane, never vanish; a `magical_node` whose state
is unknown to the port is on §7.7 item 1's ignore-list and adds nothing; two nodes add one bonus; a dossier palace whose
government admits no palace (`requires.government` fails): the palace stays on the ground, its row not a candidate —
never a contradiction, since the island moves where a dossier institution stands, never whether it exists; a custom
institution whose archetype matches no row stays on the ground; `activeChains[]` empty: no chain rule acts; a dossier
population below the CITY band: the rung gate, `gated: rung`; the dossier removing an island's institution: V05 §3.15
(1b), under a lock J-V5.82; the dossier removing a building an author pinned on an island: the use leaves (truth), the
pinned building stays vacant where it is (a pin holds geometry, never a dossier use), listed. **An older document**: every
missing field reads as V05 §2.6's migrate column states. **A missing table cell** reads its default. **Contradictory
edits**: a lock on an island and an epoch's wane — held (J-V5.10); a declaration where `h_town` = 0 — held against its
supply, listed. Test: T-V5-35.

**(f) Save, load, undo, redo, regenerate and migrate at each state.** Each island state (`floating`, `waning`, `settled`,
`fallen`, `removed`) and each edit of V05 §4.2 persists by V05 §2.6's rows: a save and a load reproduce the model digest,
the field's rasters and every strip byte for byte (the strips from the persisted bases, never the live island); undo and
redo of every event restore the model and the field exactly, a redo re-running the event's fold; an **epoch undone after a
save and a load** restores every island's record, basis and strips, the landfall's writes and the households' channels
from the epoch event's undo record (§7.4); a Regenerate and a Re-derive from S7b, SKY or S10a keep every held value
(declarations, locks, removals) and rewrite the rest by V05 §2.6's regenerate column; a migrate from an older schema runs
§7.5's chain, the island-basis step once. The unit inputs, the route and the obstacle list are derived and never
persisted. Test: T-V5-35 (b).

**(g) Combinations — where two units' features meet an island at their limits** (J-V5.86).

| combination | outcome | rung | test |
|---|---|---|---|
| a raised way under an island's shade | the raised part is a caster to the clearance (V05 §3.22 (1)) and, as item 102's daylight caster (§4.5), its pass-1 shade and the island's strip are OR-ed per sample, counted once; `sky.clearance` raises `groundRefCm` over a part the epoch built, never `shadeBasis` | F-SKY-CLEAR | T-V5-31 (b), T-V5-36 (a) |
| a cliff or steep ground under the shade | each receiver point at its own unlevelled height; the corridor bound reads the frame's extremes and the pre-test is exact per cell (V05 §3.19 (5)); a cliff under the footprint raises `groundRefCm` by its terrain height | F-SKY-SHADE | T-V5-36 (a) |
| a cliff or steep ground under a landfall | no L0 edge touched; covered plots, ways and retaining walls on both levels by their cover share in plan, as V05 §3.16's table | F-SKY-LANDFALL | T-V5-36 (b) |
| a coast, a harbour or a moat under the shade | water cells receive bits and hold no plot; `S_town` excludes water, harbour-basin and moat cells (V05 §3.3); the fold changes only land cells' values | — | T-V5-36 (a) |
| a coast, a harbour or a moat under a landfall | water faces, shores and harbour works other than decks (quay walls, sea walls, harbour chains) are not covered kinds: untouched, the rubble reported sunk ("sank in <water body>"); a covered pier, mole or quay-platform deck is ruined by the table | F-SKY-LANDFALL | T-V5-36 (b) |
| a moat under a sky access | a moat is water and a reservation: never admissible ground, so a top over it takes a slanted kind or the next direction step | F-SKY-ACCESS | T-V5-36 (c) |
| a bridge or a deck plot under the shade | a deck plot reads the field at its cell like any plot; the deck's height is not a receiver height (the unlevelled raster, V05 §3.19 (3)) | — | T-V5-36 (a) |
| a retaining wall or a terraced ward under the shade | the receiver is unlevelled; the wall's own daylight shade (§4.5) and the strip OR-ed | — | T-V5-21 (h) |
| a terraced ward under a sky access | a foot on a levelled platform is part-0 land; its ground height is `levelledGroundCm` there, so the run ends on the terrace; a yard that would meet a retaining wall's band slides along the spine as it does off a deck, else the foot moves as a gap violation does | F-SKY-ACCESS | T-V5-36 (c) |
| a terraced ward, a square, a composed building or a planned building under a landfall | V05 §3.16's table: retaining walls `decayed`, the square's reservation vacant under the rubble (its market use relocated first), a composed building demolished with its composition (volume 2), a building's plan gone with it (volume 3's plan is derived) | F-SKY-LANDFALL | T-V5-36 (b) |
| an oasis under the shade (item 111) | the oasis is water; its grove and gardens take the strip's shade share as any plot; §4.5 composes the daylight term (its weight item 111 (c)'s climate cell) and the water term | — | T-V5-37 |
| the ground frame's edge under the shade, an access or a landfall | the strip clipped (F-SKY-SHADE); a slanted foot only on the raster's admissible cells inside the frame, else the next rung; a footprint partly beyond the frame covers only what lies inside it (a) | the named rungs | T-V5-33 (a) |
| a neighbourhood close-up holding a ground landing | the landing yard is a reservation hole and the `sky_landing` use a positioned row, both among V01 §3.9's fixed inputs, kept by the close-up | — | T-V5-36 (c) |
| volume 4's vaults under an island | the underground takes no sky rule (item 2); an arch vault's span is a caster to the clearance, its vault follows the span's state at a landfall (V04); a foot is never on a vault's front, which lies under a deck plan the admissible ground excludes | — | T-V5-36 (c) |
| a lock under the shade | a locked island's basis is still written at every evaluation (a derived copy, not a held path); a locked ground block's field value moves and its plots do not re-derive (§7.2) | — | T-V5-36 (d) |
| an undo across the shade | an island edit's undo leaves every basis (the edit wrote none); undoing an event whose evaluation rewrote a basis — an epoch, Update shade now, a Re-derive — restores the basis, the strips and the field cells | — | T-V5-21 (b), T-V5-36 (d) |
| a landfall saved and reloaded | the decoration, its members, every covered state, `formerClass` and `condition` persist in the model digest; the load reproduces them and the fall's undo after the load restores the ground exactly (the undo record persisted with the event, §7.4) | — | T-V5-35 (b) |
| a landfall over another island's access foot | V05 §3.16 (3): the access re-landed in the same batch, else F-SKY-ACCESS's last rung (J-V5.83) | F-SKY-ACCESS | T-V5-14 |
| a dossier removal against a locked island | V05 §3.15 (1b) under a lock (J-V5.82) | — | T-V5-14 |
| a sky clearance against a lock | V05 §3.15 (7): `elevationCm` held, the conflict listed, the island exempt from the clearance invariant | F-SKY-CLEAR | T-V5-31 (f) |

## 4 Editing

### 4.1 The declarations — item 101's twelve tools, no thirteenth

Every island edit is an existing tool with at most one new parameter (§7.1's elegance rule):

| tool | on the sky | the events it emits |
|---|---|---|
| T1 `intend` | the islands setting (off / on) and the count (`auto`, 0–3) | `adjust('layers.islands')`, `adjust('islands.count')` — both Re-derive from S7b with their fate report (§7.8) |
| T2 `ground` | an island's terrain, water and rim, with the `key` parameter naming the island; a terrace stroke on the island, whose edge lays an island `RetainingWall` (V05 §3.22 (4)) | `setField(terrain)`, `drawWater`, `moveCourseKnot` on its river, `warp` on its key (`fromLevel 0` moves the rim); item 110 (a)'s terrace form with `key` |
| T3 `wall` | a wall on an island, with `key`: in an enclosure kind a barrier-closure enceinte walling only the landing yards (V05 §3.21); in the `retaining` kind an island `RetainingWall`, bent inward past the rim walk when drawn on or along the rim (V05 §3.22 (4)) | `drawWall` with `key` (its kind item 110 (a)'s) |
| T4 `road` | a stroke from a ground point to an island's rim; with `raised`, bent into an access; with the aqueduct class, ended at its last ground vertex outside the footprint; on an island's key, `raised` dropped and the aqueduct class made a fountain pin (J-VI.4 (d)–(e)) | `placeSkyAccess` (the kind from the stroke's class, or chosen by the dry run by V05 §3.10's rules); a bend emits the bent declaration |
| T5 `quarter` | a floating quarter: the `sky` parameter | `quarter` with `sky` — a sugar event expanding to `convert raiseIsland` (declared), an `assign` of the kind to its district and `setAttr(name)` |
| T6 `place` | an institution onto an island | `assign` with `criteria.sky` (and optionally the island) |
| T8 `fill` | the prosperity, density and greenery brushes on an island, with `key` | `setField` with `key` — stroke overrides on the island's lattice (§7.2) |
| T9 `shape` | the island's altitude, area, position, occlusion, underside and field (a target mean); an access's kind, role, state, knowledge and foot; the rim's handles | `setAttr`; `moveVertex` on rim vertices |
| T10 `hold` | the island or its paths; New random on the island; **Update shade now**; the delete of an island or an access (volume 9's delete row) | `lock`, `pin`, `revert`, `regenerate` with the island's scope; `regenerate` with the scope `shade` and the island; `delete` (folded as `convert removeIsland` on an island) |
| T11 `name` | the island's name and label | `setAttr(name)`, `moveLabel`, `hideLabel` |
| T12 `dress` | the sky layer's visibility and occlusion | `setLayer('sky', …)` |
| history | the island conversions by hand | `convert` (`resizeIsland`, `waneIsland`, `settleIsland`, `fallIsland`, `refloatIsland`, `raiseIsland`, `removeIsland`); `reclearIsland` is the epoch's own and has no tool |

Every declared thing is held (§7.2): a declared island's outline and position, a declared altitude or area, a placed
access.

**No tool for the shade or the route.** The strips, the sky receivers' shade and the route distance are derived and carry
no edit kind. §4.5's `w_sun` and this volume's `shade`, `routing` and `casterAllowanceM` cells are table values
(`editTable`, §7.6, re-deriving from their first reader stage — SKY for `casterAllowanceM`, S10a for `shade`, S10b for
`routing`); T8's prosperity brush (`setField`) overrides the field beneath an island as it does anywhere (item 102 (f));
and **Update shade now** (V05 §4.2) brings the ground under one island's old and new strips up to date at once.

### 4.2 The events and their semantics

The events are §7.1's — `placeSkyAccess`, `quarter`'s `sky` parameter (its points world points: the island's projected
outline or seed), the `key` parameter of the point-form events (`drawWay`, `drawWater`, `drawGreen`, `drawBoundary`,
`drawFootprint`, `drawWall`, `placeLight`, `setField`; their points in that arena's frame, the tool converting a world
point by the island's anchor at the dry run, so a later move never invalidates the event) and `regenerate`'s scope
`{ kind: 'shade', island }` — with §4.11's `ConversionOp` island members (`resizeIsland`, `waneIsland`, `settleIsland`,
`fallIsland`, `refloatIsland`, `raiseIsland`, `removeIsland`, `reclearIsland`); their lifecycle rows are §7.4's. Their
semantics:

* **Pointing at an island.** Every sky hit test and every tool point on the sky layer converts the pointer's world point
  to the island's frame by the anchor — and, under `offset` occlusion, first un-displaces it (the world point less the
  island's `elevation × offsetPerMetre` offset vector, §8.4) — so a click on a lifted island's drawn house or retaining
  wall selects it and a stroke drawn over the lifted island lands where it is drawn (volume 9's hit testing, V09 §5.4).
* **Moving an island** (`setAttr(anchor)`, T9's drag; J-V5.20). The arena is untouched. The dry run re-reads
  `groundRefCm` and the altitude floor at the new position, re-checks V05 §3.7's gap and frame, and re-lands every access:
  a vertical access's foot follows its top's new world point; a slanted one keeps its foot while it stays within the
  row's run, else is re-sited by V05 §3.10 (4). A foot that leaves its landing plot closes that plot's `sky_landing` use
  (item 17's `closeUse`, the house left vacant) and opens one on the plot under the new foot by a positive placement
  (§7.7's `placeAt`). The fates list both plots. `placement.byAssignment` becomes true.
* **Resizing** (`setAttr(areaHa)` or `resizeIsland`; J-V5.7). The rim is eroded or dilated by the kernel's buffer (§3.7)
  on the island's arena to the new area within 1 % (bisection on the distance, ≤ 12 steps), keeping its shape — never
  re-drawn from the stream; a growth past 1.5 × `areaHaMax` first grows the frame (V05 §3.8 (1)). The tiers re-scale
  (`depthCm` by the square-root ratio of the areas). **The rim's L1 pieces follow the rim in the same commit** (J-V5.77).
  One island L1 batch re-lays the rim strip `rimStripM` inside the new rim and re-claims each landing yard at its top, the
  top re-seated where its direction ray `d_k` now leaves the land face (V05 §3.10 (2), (5)) and its foot re-landed as a
  move re-lands it. Each re-claimed yard is served in the same commit: its `sky_landing_top` plot re-cut on the new yard
  (the old one tombstoned) and its use opened there by a positive placement (`placeAt`), the plots the yard covers
  re-derived by §7.3's local operator on the island's arena, and SKY-F's checks and repairs (V05 §3.12) run on the
  island, so a way meets every attach. The institution claim is kept where it still fits the land face less the re-laid
  strip and yards; else it is re-searched by V05 §3.10 (5)'s score and crypt re-test over the sites inside the district
  that holds it — so every district keeps its kind — and that district is re-derived by its scoped re-derivation from S8
  with the new claim a hole and the old claim's land its own (V01 §3.10.3 (6) (c)'s claim re-search, which releases the
  institution from V01 §3.9's fixed inputs), whose S10b places the institution on the new claim: its use relocated there
  (a dossier-backed one by the solver's `dossier` request, V05 §4.4), its old building demolished, a listed fate. That
  district is re-derived whether or not it receives a band face, and once where it does. **A resize never leaves the claim
  without a site:** the area is floored at the least area (the same bisection) at which some site in that district still
  fits; a hand request below the floor is bent to it (`bend.island.claimFloor`), and an epoch's `resizeIsland` (V05 §3.15
  (2)–(3)) stops at it, listed in the epoch's report. **Shrinking:** every plot that loses at least half its face to the
  eroded rim, the re-laid strip or a re-claimed yard is cleared (its households relocated on the island first, then to
  the ground), the rest re-cut with their neighbours fixed (§4.7 S10 (7)); every way that crosses the re-laid strip line
  is cut back to it (the strip is never cut); no district is re-derived but the claim's where the claim is re-searched;
  and volume 1's `plantRimWalk` (V01 §3.10.3 step 4) re-plants the rim walk on the re-laid strip in the same commit.
  **Growing:** the land between the old and the new strip lines — with whatever an old yard held that its re-claim does
  not, less the re-claimed yards — is cut into faces by extending, in the same L1 batch, each district boundary way that
  meets the old strip line across the band to the new one along the band's normal there (the same class); each face
  joins the district of its nearest rim stretch (ties by district address), and every district that receives a face is
  re-derived by its own scoped re-derivation from S8 (V01 §3.10.3 (6) (c)) — never a unit run, which would re-cut every
  district, and never a change of the island's district count; `plantRimWalk` then re-plants the rim walk whole, once
  for the resize, since the walk is one island-level pair that no district's re-derivation writes.
* **Altitude** (`setAttr(altitudeCm)`): the record alone; a value below V05 §3.9's floor is bent to the floor
  (`bend.island.altitude`).
* **Underside and occlusion** (`setAttr(underside.*)`, `setAttr(occlusion)`): the record alone (`underside.source` becomes
  `edit`); an underside change that would break the floor raises the altitude, listed as a fate.
* **Field** (`setAttr(field.prosperity)` — a declared target mean, `declared.field`: the gradient shifted uniformly to
  meet it, V05 §3.11 (5); `setAttr(field.density)`, `setAttr(field.greenery)`; and the island brushes, `setField` with
  `key` and a prosperity, density or greenery stroke, as §7.2's stroke overrides on the island's lattice over the
  gradient): the island's plots whose value crosses a cut past τ re-derive (§7.2's rule for a field brush), on the island
  only.
* **Reshaping the rim** (`moveVertex` on a rim vertex, `warp` on the island's key with `fromLevel 0`): §7.3's local
  operator on the island's arena moves the rim, and the rim's L1 pieces follow it as a resize's do (above; J-V5.77) — the
  strip re-laid inside the new rim, each landing yard on a moved stretch re-claimed at its top (a pinned `sky_access`
  vertex never moves), the institution claim kept or re-searched inside its district; a reshape that would leave the claim
  no site there is bent back along each moved vertex's drag to the furthest position that keeps one (bisection, ≤ 12
  steps; `bend.island.rimStop`). Each stretch that moved outward is a growing resize's band — its faces, cut at the
  extended district boundary ways, joining the district of their nearest rim stretch (ties by district address), every
  district that receives a face re-derived by its own scoped re-derivation from S8 — and each that moved inward a
  shrinking resize's, which gives no face and re-derives no district but the claim's, where the claim is re-searched (V01
  §3.10.3 (6) (c)); `plantRimWalk` then re-plants the rim walk whole, once for the reshape; `areaHa` is rewritten to the
  new land area.
* **New random on the island** (`regenerate` with the island's scope, `newRandom`): the outline's displacement, the
  terrain (with an `own` relief's class), the underside (unless edited) and the river re-derived under the next reroll,
  SKY's island steps re-run (the rim strip, the landing yards and the institution claim re-laid, V05 §3.10 (5)), the
  island's districts re-laid by volume 1's unit run (V01 §3.10.3 (6) (a)–(b); each district's template kept where its
  lineage is, V01 §3.3.4), then SKY-F (V05 §3.12), which rewrites `SkyIsland.districts`; the anchor, the area, the
  altitude, the allocation and every access's foot kept; each vertical access's top re-seated at the new rim's point
  nearest above its foot, and where the new rim no longer overhangs it the access becomes a `ropeway` from the rim point
  nearest the foot. The altitude floor (V05 §3.9 (4)) is re-checked after the underside is re-drawn: a deeper root that
  breaks it raises `elevationCm` to the floor (a listed fate). The ground is byte-identical (the seam exception is
  empty); `shadeBasis` is untouched.
* **Declaring an island** (T5 with `sky`). At its dry run: the gate (V05 §3.2 (1)) must hold — where the rung admits no
  island or the setting is off, the declaration is bent to a ground quarter of its kind (`bend.island.gate`; a dossier
  whose `site.magic` resolves `none` refuses it, V05 §4.4) — and a declaration where `h_town` = 0 is allowed only with the
  fate `held against its supply` confirmed (J-V5.10); the points form gives the projected outline (a lasso), a disc of the
  kind's area at one point, or the routine fitted to two to four corners; the slot is the lowest free (the lowest held
  slot when none is free, released by this event, V05 §3.2 (6)); the fill is V05 §3.5's, taken by **relocating** the
  richest households from the ground (never re-deriving it) — each household moved up is a relocation, its ground
  building left vacant — all listed as fates; V05 §3.8–§3.14 then run on the new island, which has no `shadeBasis` until
  the next shade evaluation: it casts and receives no strip meanwhile, and Update shade now on it writes one at once. In
  the creation prefix the declaration is read ahead by S7b (5) as a forced slot (V05 §3.2 (3)), so no relocation is
  needed.
* **Placing an institution on an island** (T6: `assign` with `criteria.sky`): the solver places it on the island named,
  else on the island whose reason is `institution` and whose row matches, else the dry run offers T5's declaration. A
  custom kind is named by its `localUid` (item 104 (a) (1): the map-side declaration, stored in the map document, never
  in the dossier); where it is a resource or processing node of an active chain, the dry run lists the fate "this chain
  now crosses the hoist" and the route lengths of its links before and after (V05 §3.20 (9) (e)).
* **Accesses.** `placeSkyAccess`'s dry run checks the kind against the `magic` circumstance and the row's limits (a kind
  that does not fit is bent to the nearest admitted kind in V05 §3.10's order, `bend.access.kind`), inserts the top vertex
  on the rim, claims the island's landing yard (the plots it covers re-derived by §7.3's local operator on the island's
  arena, V01 §3.10.3 (6) (c)) and opens the ground landing by a positive placement on the plot under the foot. `delete`
  of an access reverses all three; deleting an island's last access is bent into a move to the next admissible site
  (`bend.access.last`). **A deleted generated access stays deleted**: the delete's fold appends its ordinal to
  `SkyIsland.removedAccesses`, which every later re-siting skips and counts (V05 §3.10 (1)); where it was the `main`
  access, the lowest surviving generated ordinal takes `main` in the same event (a listed fate), so an island always has
  a main access while it has any; the delete's undo removes the ordinal. A placed access's delete leaves no ordinal (it
  was declared, and nothing re-sites it). The foot of a placed or moved access meets the standing raised parts by V05
  §3.22 (3) (a)–(b) at its dry run, a foot they block moving to the nearest admissible foot (`bend.access.foot`).
  **`setAttr(kind)`** on an access re-runs the same check at its dry run — the `magic` circumstance, the row's altitude
  and horizontal-run limits, a `guarded` access a `hoist` only — a kind that does not fit bent to the nearest admitted
  kind (`bend.access.kind`); an admitted change re-seats nothing but the landing buildings (the top's and the foot's use
  rows take the new kind's building). **`setAttr(ground.at)`** re-lands the access as a move does: the old landing plot's
  `sky_landing` use closed, a new one opened by a positive placement on the plot under the new foot, both plots listed as
  fates, the horizontal run re-checked. An island wall's circuit follows every access change (V05 §3.21 (3)).
* **The shade beneath follows** (item 102 (e); J-V5.27). Every event that changes what an island's strip would read — a
  move, an altitude, a resize, a rim reshape, an underside, New random on the island, a declaration, a delete, a
  `convert` — leaves `shadeBasis`, the ground's field and every strip where they were: its dry run lists the fate "The
  ground beneath will catch up the next time the town changes over time. Update it now?", and the shade map view draws
  the new strip as pending (V05 §5.1). A Re-derive from S10a or an earlier stage runs a shade evaluation (V05 §3.19 (6)).
* **Update shade now** (`regenerate` with the scope `{ kind: 'shade', island }`; T10, and the fate's own button) — the
  shade evaluation `update` of V05 §3.19 (6) (d), for that island alone: its `shadeBasis` is written from the island as
  it stands (`by: 'update'`, the document's current epoch), its strips onto the ground and onto the islands beneath it are
  recomputed — and, where it had no basis, the strips onto it from the islands above it — and the ground's field is
  recomputed over the union of the old and new strip windows — the cells of the other islands' strips re-read from their
  unchanged bases — with the blocks there whose value crosses a cut past τ re-derived by §7.2's rule, their fates listed.
  It writes only its island's basis. The prosperity brush stays for hand changes.
* **Deleting an island** (T10's delete on an island, volume 9's delete row; the clerk; J-V5.21, J-V5.62). The dry run
  turns the delete into **`convert removeIsland`**: the island is removed without a landfall — its households relocated
  to the ground by §4.7's overflow pass (in an epoch's history, as any hand conversion, inside the event), its landing
  uses closed, its arena archived as `fallIsland` archives it, a split shop's use relocated to the ground (V05 §3.20
  (11)) — and its record stays live at its address in state `removed`, `removed` written (`seq`, `generated` from its
  lifecycle cause) and its `slot` held closed (V05 §3.2 (6)). It draws, labels and exports nothing but its `.plat` record
  (V05 §5.1, §5.5). Where the ground cannot house its households, the dry run offers instead `adjust('islands.count')`, a
  Re-derive from S7b. The fate list says, in volume 9's shared words, "The town won't raise this island again; undo
  brings it back". `convert fallIsland` is the ruinous alternative. A removed island is undone only by the removal's
  undo; a T5 `sky` declaration re-fills its slot with a new island only when no slot is free, never resurrecting it, and
  no `adjust('islands.count')` re-opens it (§2.8's lift rule). A floating, waning or settled island may be removed; a
  `fallen` one may not — its slot is already free and its landfall is ground content, which the ground's own edits
  clear; a `removed` one is already removed (the delete is a no-op, listed).
* **Bringing an island down** (`convert fallIsland`, the history tool; the clerk's "bring it down"). The dry run lists
  V05 §3.16's fates, one per covered entity; a covered way's reads "rubble; a track until the town clears it (was
  <class>)", `<class>` the class the fall writes to `Way.formerClass`, or the one the way already holds there. The
  event's undo restores V05 §3.16 (8)'s state.
* **A raised or aqueduct stroke to an island** (J-V5.61). A T4 stroke that ends on an island's rim with `raised` or the
  aqueduct class never makes a way (II.1: no roads in or out). Its dry run bends it (J-VI.4 (d); item 101 R2 refuses only
  a dossier fact or a lock): a `raised` stroke becomes `placeSkyAccess` with the stroke's ends as top and foot and the
  kind V05 §3.10's rules admit there — a `ropeway` for a slanted stroke, a `hoist` where the foot lies under the rim, a
  `lightBridge` where `magic` admits it (`bend.raised.island`); an aqueduct stroke ending on a rim ends at its last ground
  vertex outside the island's footprint (`bend.aqueduct.island`). A `raised` or aqueduct stroke wholly on an island's key
  is bent — `raised` dropped, the way drawn on the island's ground (`bend.raised.onIsland`); the aqueduct made a fountain
  pin at its end (`bend.aqueduct.onIsland`; J-VI.4 (e)). A `raised` stroke on the ground whose course would cut an
  access's column or pass within `lineClearM` under its rope line is re-routed by the dry run through
  `skyAccessObstacles`, or, where no re-route exists, ended at its last ground vertex before the column
  (`bend.raised.hoist`; J-VI.4 (f); V05 §3.22 (3)).
* **A retaining wall or terrace on an island**: V05 §3.22 (4) (`bend.retaining.rim` on or along the rim).
* **A wall on an island** (T3 with `key`): V05 §3.21 (3) (`bend.wall.drop` along the rim away from a landing).

**The bends** (item 101 R2; J-VI.4 (a)–(g); every one a bend with its line, never a refusal — the one refusal is
DR27's dossier-owned one, V05 §4.4). Each key is a row of volume 9's `bend.*` table (V09 §4.9) and of volume 10's `bend`
category; the lines are this volume's words:

| key | bends | line |
|---|---|---|
| `bend.island.gate` | a T5 `sky` declaration where the rung admits no island or the setting is off → a ground quarter of its kind | "{reason}; this quarter stands on the ground." |
| `bend.island.fourth` | a fourth island (V05 §3.2 (3)) → a district joining the nearest open island | "At most three islands float here; this quarter joins {island}." |
| `bend.island.count` | an integer count below the forced islands (V05 §3.2 (4)) → the forced count | "{n} islands are placed by hand here; the count is {n}." |
| `bend.island.altitude` | an altitude below the floor (V05 §3.9 (4)) → the floor | "{island}'s rock hangs {m} m deep; it floats at least {h} m up." |
| `bend.island.claimFloor` | a resize below the institution floor → the floor | "{island} keeps room for its {institution}; shrunk to {area} ha." |
| `bend.island.rimStop` | a rim reshape that would leave the claim no site → bent back | "{island} keeps room for its {institution}; the rim stops here." |
| `bend.access.kind` | an access kind the magic circumstance or the row's limits do not admit → the nearest admitted kind | "A {kind} can't serve {island} here; made a {kind2} (fits here: {kinds})." |
| `bend.access.last` | deleting an island's last access → a move to the next admissible site (alternative: "Place a different access") | "An island keeps one way up; moved to {site}." |
| `bend.access.foot` | a foot a standing raised part blocks → the nearest admissible foot | "The {deck} is in the way; the landing moved {m} m to {site}." |
| `bend.raised.island` | a `raised` stroke ending on a rim → `placeSkyAccess` (alternative: "Draw a road to its foot") | "A road cannot reach a floating island; a hoist, rope line, stair or bridge of light can." |
| `bend.aqueduct.island` | an aqueduct stroke ending on a rim → ended at its last ground vertex outside the footprint | "An island's water is its own; the aqueduct ends here." |
| `bend.raised.onIsland` | a `raised` stroke on an island's key → `raised` dropped | "An island carries no raised way; its bridges cross its river on their own decks." |
| `bend.aqueduct.onIsland` | an aqueduct stroke on an island's key → a fountain pin | "An island's water is its own; a fountain stands where the aqueduct would end." |
| `bend.raised.hoist` | a `raised` stroke across an access's column with no re-route → ended before it | "This would cut {island}'s hoist; it ends here." |
| `bend.retaining.rim` | a retaining or terrace stroke on or along the rim → moved inward (V05 §3.22 (4)) | "The rim walk stays level; the wall stands inside it." |
| `bend.wall.drop` | an enclosure stroke along the rim away from a landing → the landing stretches only | "The drop is the wall here." |

### 4.3 Locality and undo

| edit | effective scope | outside it, byte-identical |
|---|---|---|
| altitude, occlusion, underside, field and the island brushes, name, terrain and water on the island, a wall on the island, New random on the island | the island's arena and record | the ground and every other island (seam exception empty) |
| a resize, a rim reshape | the island's arena and record, plus the ground landing plots its re-landed feet name (their designations only) and the ground buildings that take the households a shrink relocates | everything else; every other island |
| an access placed, moved or deleted; an island moved; an island deleted | the island's arena and record, plus the ground landing plots its fates name (their designations only) | everything else |
| an island declared after generation | the new island's arena, plus the ground buildings its relocations vacate and the landing plots | everything else |
| a ground edit whose effective scope meets an island's projected footprint — a raised way, a retaining wall or a building added, raised or removed beneath it included (its dry run reading `skyAccessObstacles`, V05 §3.22 (3)) | the ground edit's own scope, plus that island's `groundRefCm` and, where V05 §3.9's floor requires it, its `altitudeCm` (a listed fate); a removal beneath never lowers the island — its `elevationCm` kept, `altitudeCm` re-read | the island's arena |
| `fallIsland` | V05 §3.16's covered set and the island's arena, plus another island's accesses the fall re-lands (V05 §3.16 (3)) | everything else |
| `removeIsland` | the island's arena and record, plus the ground buildings that take its households and its landing plots (their designations only) | everything else; the other islands' slots and draws |
| `reclearIsland` (an epoch's) | the island's record (`groundRefCm`, `altitudeCm`, `elevationCm` where raised) | everything else, the island's arena and `shadeBasis` included |
| Update shade now | the island's `shadeBasis`; the ground's field over the union of the island's old and new strip windows and the blocks there that cross a cut past τ; the strips onto the islands beneath it and, where it had no basis, the strips onto it | everything else, every other island's basis included |

No island edit writes the ground's field or any `shadeBasis`: the strips keep their basis (V05 §3.19 (6)), so every row
above holds for the field's rasters as for the ground's geometry, before and after a save and a load (§2.9); Update
shade now is not an island edit but a scoped shade evaluation. Every event undoes by its inverse through §7.4's fold, by
§7.4's rows for `placeSkyAccess`, `regenerate` with the scope `shade`, `convert removeIsland` and `reclearIsland`.
`Document.nextSkyPart` never decreases, so an undone island's part stays spent.

### 4.4 The dossier — DR27 and coherence

§7.7's row **DR27** (J-VI.13; ID-DR) traces the islands to the dossier — its rule V05 §3.2–§3.10, §3.15 (1b) and §3.20
(9)–(12), its stream `island.place`, its totality bar §7.7's. What each of its inputs feeds here: the population the
rung (DR23) and so the gate; `wealth.label` the field (DR6) and so `S_town`; the households' bands `S_carried` (DR7);
the institutions (DR9, DR24) the reason rows — a palace, cathedral, great temple, prison or mage's instance, a custom
institution by its resolved scene archetype and `landmarkLevel` (item 104 (a)); the institution ladder records, by their
step type, what may go up (item 108 (a): a singular or SIZE record; COUNT, GUILD and QUARTER members counted across both
arenas); the district-defining records the fill's held shares (item 72); `government` a palace's eligibility (DR11);
`site.magic` the `magic` circumstance — the gate and the access kinds; `site.deposits` the node term (V05 §3.3); the
active reviewed custom chains (item 104 (b)) and `activeChains[]` (item 105 (i)) the nodes the fill keeps on the ground
(V05 §3.5 (3)) and the links' status (V05 §3.20 (10) (d)). The setting itself is the map's declared choice, never a
dossier path.

**Coherence** (J-V5.19). Where the dossier's `site.magic` resolves to `none`, turning the setting on is refused (F-EDIT
`dossier-owned`: "the dossier's world has no magic"), and a dossier change to `none` at an absorb makes every island's
target 0 at the next epoch — they wane, never vanish (item 22). A dossier institution on an island stays a dossier fact:
a fall, a delete or a resize that removes its building first relocates it (§4.11's *Dossier content across epochs*).
**The reverse** — the dossier removes the institution an island carries (a palace, a cathedral, a custom tower) —
removes the institution at once (item 20 (h): its building vacated, its uses closed), leaves the island standing, and
re-reasons the island at the next epoch by V05 §3.15 (1b) (a locked island by J-V5.82's rule). Until an epoch runs (a
document without history), the absorb's report lists the island as "awaiting a new purpose", and the clerk offers T6
(another institution onto it) or T5's kind change as ghosts. A `magical_node` deposit that turns `depleted` or `removed`
at an absorb drops the node term at the next epoch's evaluation — the islands' supply falls by the law and they wane,
never vanish; one that turns `discovered` adds it at the next epoch (V05 §3.3).

### 4.5 The clerk's view

**Registration.** `@plat/sky` registers one `WhyProvider` (V10 §2.13) with `kinds` `SkyIsland`, `SkyAccess` and the
`fallen_island` decoration, answering from the committed model and the report, filtered by the caller's key. The
prosperity field's `drivers()` (V10's `FieldDrivers`) gives the daylight term one entry as §4.5 defines it and, beside
it, one attribution per island whose strip covers the point: `{ driver: 'daylight', delta, sources: { caster: Id |
'terrain' | 'walls' | 'buildings'; samples: number }[] }`, each source's `samples` the sample bits it blocks at the point
and the entry's `delta` the OR-ed total × `w_sun` ÷ `bits` (a sample blocked by a hill and an island is listed under both
and counted once, J-V5.38) — the `sources` list is this volume's addition to volume 10's `FieldDrivers.terms`. Pulls
across arenas reach `PlacementSolver.explain` (volume 10's `Explanation.terms`) with the access `skyRoute` returned, so
every cross-arena term names its landing.

The reader (§11.2's `query`) lists each island in the gazetteer with its name, reason, state, area, altitude, supply and
accesses. `why` answers:

* **on an island** — its gate, its slot's draw against `chance · h_town`, its reason's candidates and weights (a custom
  institution's matched archetype named), its fill's bands and their shares of its land, its stop cause, the trades it
  left on the ground by `carry` (each with its reason: heavy works, noxious, a need an island cannot meet, a chain's
  working step) and the split shops it carried, the coverage instances it took per island district, the S3 reservation
  it handed over and to whom ("the old cathedral close — now the mother church"), its cathedral's crypt ("cut into the
  island's rock; the largest crypt in the town"; half-sunk where the rock is too thin), its placement's class shares, its
  reachability and score, its underside's kind, and its strip — how many hectares of ground it shades for at least one
  (`cells`), three and five samples, when the ground last took its shade (`evaluated`) and whether the field still reads
  it where it hung (the basis, V05 §3.19 (8)) — filtered by the caller's key (a `secret` service hoist to a prison is
  withheld); on an island the clearance raised, "raised clear of the aqueduct" (or of the viaduct, the terrace wall, the
  stepped temple) naming the caster and the epoch; on a removed island or access, "removed by you" with the event, worded
  by `removedSlots(doc)`'s `generated`, and what alone would re-open its slot (undo, or a new island drawn when no other
  slot is free — never a new island count); on a locked island held without its institution, that fate (J-V5.82);
* **on the ground under an island** — each island whose strip covers it and for how many samples, the term's share of the
  plot's value, and whether the ground has caught up;
* **on a use** on an island, or on a ground use pulled by one — the rows and the links that pulled it (the good, the bound
  provider or consumers, the orientation, item 105 (j)) and the landing each cross-arena pull or link went through with
  its route length (V05 §3.20 (8), (10)); on a split shop, its works below and the route between them (V05 §3.20 (11)); on
  a node of a chain that crosses an island, the chain, the node's step in its flow, the landing its link crosses and why
  its working steps stayed on the ground (V05 §3.20 (9));
* **on an island's rim** with no wall — "the drop is the wall here" (V05 §3.21); **on a landfall** — the island that
  fell, when, and what it broke by V05 §3.16's table (the walls breached, the ways buried, the arches and raised-way piers
  ruined, the terrace walls broken, the shops that fell with their bridge, the fountain an aqueduct no longer feeds, the
  hoists of another island moved clear) with the clearance still owed; **on a bent edit** — the bend's line (V05 §4.2's
  table) and the access it made where it made one; **on the town supply** — the `magical_node` deposit when the node term
  acts, "the node beneath the old ruins" when the report's `ruin.node` line names it (V04 §3.5.0a (9); V05 §3.3).

A clerk's proposals are these events as ghosts: "a floating palace over the river" is T5 with `sky` and the palace
kind; "raise it higher" T9's `setAttr(altitudeCm)`; "let the ground catch up" Update shade now; "bring it down" `convert
fallIsland` with V05 §3.16's fates. It never writes a dossier fact and meets DR27's refusal as any author does.

## 5 Rendering, labels, exports

### 5.1 Marks (S14; every sky coordinate is `anchor` + the local one)

| semantic | kind | what | bands |
|---|---|---|---|
| `sky.island.land` | `area` | the land face, in the style's `sky.ground` role (one step lighter than the ground's block tone in `ink`; the patch tone of `hatched`) | all |
| the island's fabric | as any district's | ways, blocks, plots, buildings, greens, water — `layer: 'sky'`, `z` above the ground | as the ground's |
| `sky.island.rim` | `outline` | the rim chain with the `cliff` bank program's outward ticks | all |
| `sky.island.shadow` | `shadow` | rung 3: the projected tier stack (V05 §5.3); `hatched`: the patch's drop shadow, as the Neighbourhood look draws its lone patch (`research/findings/vg-ng-devlogs.md` §B3.5; `research/findings/watabou-extra-a.md` §3) | all |
| `sky.island.underside` | `area` | `offset` occlusion only: the tier stack's silhouette swept along the offset vector — the rock hanging beneath the lifted island | all |
| `sky.island.tether` | `outline` | `offset` only: the true ground footprint dashed, and a hairline from the lifted centroid to the true one | district and finer |
| `sky.fall`, `sky.mist` | `glyph`, `area` | the waterfall's lip on the rim; a soft mist disc on the ground below it | district and finer |
| `sky.island.shadeStrip` | `outline` | the shade map view only (volume 9's thematic view of item 102 (f)), **outlines only — the veil of shade shares is volume 9's alone**: each island's held strip onto the ground at its basis as one solid ink line around the cells it blocks for at least three of the samples, traced by marching squares over the mask at the field cell, labelled with the island's name along it | city and district |
| `sky.island.shadePending` | `outline` | the shade map view only, while the caster's `shadeBasis` differs from its live island (V05 §3.19 (8)): the strip the island would cast now, its three-sample area as a dashed ink line — so a dashed line always means "where the shade will move" | city and district |
| `sky.access.<kind>` | `glyph` + `ribbon` | hoist: a glyph at the top and at the foot, joined only in `offset`; stair: the same glyphs, and in `offset` the helix of V05 §2.2 as a stepped spiral line; rope line: a hairline from the top's world point to the foot; bridge of light: a glowing ribbon with `light` marks along it at night | district and finer (the foot glyph at the city band) |

**States** (§8.2's `states`): the base's island rows stand, the `fallen` row drawing its rubble over the faces and bands of
the `fallen_island` decoration's members (V05 §3.16 (5)); a settled island's buildings draw their own existence rows;
an access reads `closed` (a bar glyph), `unserved` (alpha 0.5) and is not drawn once `ruined`; every other kind the
landfall covers draws by its own state row, as V05 §3.16's table's third column names it; the deck plots tombstoned with
their ruined decks draw nothing. A `removed` island draws nothing, labels nothing and has no shadow. The island's own
retaining walls (V05 §3.22 (4)) draw by the base's retaining-wall semantic in the island's frame, on the sky layer's list,
displaced with the island under `offset`, their low-side shadow cast by the light pass on the island's sky surface layer.

**Slots and sheets** (§8.2). The sky layer composes its own list with §8.2's layer order; every shipped sheet (`ink`,
`lit`, `hatched`, `night` and the parity presets, which carry the rows though their recipes hold islands off) resolves
every sky semantic, so the loader never refuses one:

| semantic | layer-order slot (§8.2) | per-family facts (the values are volume 11's palette rows, row 52) |
|---|---|---|
| `sky.island.land` | 3 `ground.block` | role `sky.ground`: in `ink` one step lighter than `ground`; in `hatched` the patch tone; in `lit` and `night` the `ground` albedo lit by the light pass |
| `sky.island.underside` | 2, with the water (under the land, so the lifted land covers its top) | role `sky.ground` darkened one step, `ink` outline; `offset` only |
| `sky.island.rim` | 8 shore marks | the `cliff` bank program's ticks in `ink` |
| `sky.island.shadow` | 10 projected shadows | role `shadow` (rung 3); `hatched` the patch's drop shadow |
| `sky.island.tether` | 17 boundary treatments | `ink` hairline, dashed; `offset` only |
| `sky.fall`, `sky.access.*` | 19 towers and gate blocks | glyphs in `ink`; a bridge of light's ribbon in role `light` |
| `sky.mist` | 8 shore marks (after water) | role `water` lightened, alpha 0.5 |
| `sky.island.shadeStrip`, `sky.island.shadePending` | 25 live layers | `ink` lines, solid and dashed, label in role `label` |

The style role **`sky.ground`** (§8.2's *Roles*) is the land of a sky island: never `ground` itself, so an island reads
against the ground beneath it in every family. The island's own fabric resolves by the ground's semantics.

### 5.2 Occlusion

`ghost` by default (§8.4), `opaque` and `offset` as the sky `LayerSpec` sets them or an island's own `occlusion`
overrides. In `offset` the island's marks, labels and shadow-free silhouette draw displaced by `elevation ×
offsetPerMetre`, while its cast shadow stays where the sun truly puts it; the sky base is **lit at the island's true
position** and drawn displaced (V05 §5.3 (2)), and every pointer and tool point on it is un-displaced before the anchor
conversion (V05 §4.2).

### 5.3 Light and shadow — the underside's shadow (the owner's J98)

1. **The slab list** (§8.4 (a)) holds, per island and **under every occlusion mode** — `opaque` as well as `ghost` and
   `offset`, so an island is never written into the ground's surface layer as a column, whose shadow would start at the
   footprint's edge and lose the gap and the profile — underside tier 0 and underside tiers 1 … n of V05 §3.9 (2), each
   its world ring, its top and its underside, so the island's shadow is the shadow of its whole rock: a `root` casts a
   long tapering tail under a low sun, a `flat` only its rim; the ground right beneath is shaded from above yet lit from
   the side through the gap under the root; and the penumbra widens with the distance from the underside, so a higher
   island's shadow is softer (§8.4 (c)'s law).
2. **The sky surface layer** (§8.4 (f); J-V5.16). Where an island's drawn land face touches a tile, the tile worker rasters
   that tile's sky marks into a second base and a second surface layer (the island's terrain at its datum, its roofs,
   towers and crowns), each with its max-height pyramid. The light pass lights the sky base over the sky surface layer —
   its rays reading the ground's pyramids, the other islands' slabs and its own island's surface — and the ground base
   over the ground surface layer with the whole slab list; then composes: `opaque`, the lit sky over its coverage;
   `ghost`, the lit sky × (1 − `ghostAlpha`) over the lit ground × `ghostAlpha`; `offset`, the lit ground everywhere and
   the lit sky over its displaced coverage. The CPU reference and the GPU pass run the same law. A tile no island's land
   touches takes no second base, but it is **re-lit where an island's shadow falls on it** — the shadow reaches up to 8 ×
   the island's height from its footprint. **The global light's window** (§8.4 (c)) reads the surface layers' own top
   (`surfaceLayerTopCm`, §8.1), without the slabs, and each slab whose shadow sweep crosses the ray's plan is tested exactly
   over its own reach (J-V5.56), so an island lengthens no ground ray's window; a tile culls the slabs whose sweep's
   bounding box misses it.
3. **Rung 3** (the vector rung; J-V5.55): the island's shadow is the union over its underside tiers of each tier
   projected in the **`prism` form** — its ring swept along the sun vector from its underside's offset `b · c` to its
   top's `t · c`, then unioned — whatever `vector.projected` says for other casters, since an island is a floating solid;
   the rim slab sweeps from its base to the surface top. So a `root`'s thin lower tiers join into one tapering tail
   instead of a row of beads. Only when §8.4's rung-3 budget fallback fires is the `drop` form used, and then the tiers
   are subdivided so that no tier's sweep `(t − b) · c` exceeds its ring's inradius, which keeps successive drops
   overlapping.
4. **The cursor sun under an island** (§8.4 (d); J-V5.14). The cursor sun stands at `H = h₀ · D · s`; at close zooms that
   can fall below an island's top. **Whenever the sky layer is visible and the document holds an island that is not
   fallen**, `H` is `max(h₀ · D · s, 1.5 × the list's surfaceTopCm above the view's ground)` — whatever is in view — so
   no sun ever stands below a floating island and panning never changes `H`; the path and fixed suns are unchanged.
5. **The picture and the model agree.** The light pass's shadow (from the rough tiers, at any sun) and the model's strip
   (from the smooth tiers, at the sampler's fixed suns, V05 §3.19) read one solid: under the path sun set to the equinox
   at a sample's hour, the style's `sun.latitudeDeg` set to the site latitude, the rung-3 shadow of the smooth tiers on
   flat ground covers exactly the cells the strip marks for that sample, within one field cell at the boundary (T-V5-20)
   — so the shade map and the drawn shadow never disagree about where an island's shade falls.

### 5.4 Labels

The island's `quarter` label draws at the city band along its long axis (§9.3's area rule on its land face), its
districts' labels from the district band, an institution's `landmark` label, a landfall's `place` label; accesses carry
no label, and neither does a strip (the shade map view's legend names the islands). Candidates of sky entities are held
in the island's local frame and translated by S14 (§9.1). §9.2's candidate set: sky rows only while the sky layer is
visible; under `opaque`, a ground row whose anchor lies inside a visible island's drawn land face is no candidate; under
`ghost` both are candidates, the sky rows at 1.5 × their priority; under `offset`, sky candidates at their displaced
positions at the stop. An island too small for its label at a stop draws none there (§9.2's fit test).

### 5.5 Exports (§11.1's rows)

| format | the sky |
|---|---|
| `.plat`, JSON schema | island records with local geometry and `anchor` (a `removed` record with its `removed` field and no arena geometry, the arena in the archive); `SkyAccess` records; `removedAccesses`; every field's tier |
| compact twin | world coordinates (anchor added), `layer` and `elevationCm` per sky entity |
| GeoJSON | FeatureCollection `sky`: one feature per island — its projected land face, with address, name, reason, state, `areaHa`, `altitudeM`, `elevationM`, supply, `placement.over`, the underside's kind and depth — then its entities in world coordinates with `elevationM`, its accesses as LineStrings (top → foot) with kind, role and run, and its strip as three MultiPolygon features (`kind: 'shadeStrip'`, `samplesAtLeast` 1, 3 or 5, `basisCurrent`); the island's own retaining walls as §11.1's `retaining` LineStrings, in world coordinates, plus `elevationM`; a removed island is not exported; a landfall in the `ground` collection |
| SVG | `<g id="layer.sky">`, one `<g id="sky<part>">` per island; the shadow group; the underside and tether in `offset` exports; in an export of the shade map view, `<g id="sky<part>.shade">` with the strip's solid outline and any dashed pending outline (volume 9's veil in its own group) |
| PNG / tiles | the sky layer's own transparent PNG (§11.1's per-layer PNGs); lit by V05 §5.3 at the export's sun |
| VTT | one scene per island in its local frame, its elevation recorded; the rim a wall that blocks movement and not sight; each landing yard a note linked to its ground landing's note in the ground scene |
| glTF | per island a node at (`anchor`, `elevation`): its terrain draped over the land face, the rim wall down by the rim thickness, the underside lofted between the underside tier rings (each resampled to `export.gltfRingPoints` points by arc length from its point nearest the island's bearing; a `flat` island's rim slab closed by its base), its retaining walls as walls on its terrain, its buildings and trees, the river sloped, the fall as a mist quad `export.mistM` tall; hoist ropes and rope lines as thin cylinders, a stair's steps as boxes laid along its helix (V05 §2.2) with a slab at each landing, a light bridge as a translucent ribbon; `extras` with address, reason and state |
| CityJSON | the island as PLAT's extension object `+FloatingIsland` (its top TIN and its underside solid, attributes as GeoJSON's), its buildings as `Building`s based at its elevation, accesses as `+SkyAccess` lines — PLAT's extension schema carries both types (§11.1) |
| CSV / statistics | island count, areas, altitudes, carried households by band with each band's share of the island's land, supplies, states, district counts; per island its strip's area blocked for at least one (`cells`), three and five samples, the mean prosperity inside its three-sample area and in the same district's cells outside it (item 102 (g)'s quantity), whether its basis is current, the trades left on the ground by `carry` with their reasons, the split shops carried, the coverage instances taken per island district, the S3 reservation handed over and its taker, the crypt's form, the count of cross-arena pulls and links by landing; the node term when it acts; per active chain (custom or `activeChains[]`) that crosses an island, its island nodes, the landing its links cross and their route lengths; per landfall, every covered entity by V05 §3.16's table's kind with its new state, the deck plots tombstoned with their decks, and the accesses of another island it re-landed; per island and epoch, each clearance raise (`reclearIsland`) with the caster that caused it; the removed islands and access ordinals |
| skeleton file | declared islands (T5 with `sky`) and placed accesses (T4) as declarations |

Knowledge: a `secret` access is withheld from every key that does not admit it (§9.2's filter, §11.1's *Knowledge*). The
strip is thematic: the PNG and tile layers, VTT, glTF and CityJSON carry the island's physical shadow through the light
pass and its solid, never the strip (J-V5.35).

## 6 Performance budgets

All PLAUSIBLE until M6-sky measures them (V05 §8), on §12.1's reference machine; the sky is off on the one-click path, so
§12.3–§12.5's laws are unchanged there.

| work | CITY, three islands | METROPOLIS, three islands | basis |
|---|---|---|---|
| S7b (5): `S_town`, slots, reasons, fills | ≤ 3 ms | ≤ 5 ms | one mean over ≈ 24 000 / 62 000 field cells; ≤ 100 fill batches per slot |
| S7b (5): outlines and placement | ≤ 8 ms | ≤ 20 ms | the 64 m class raster once (≈ 6 000 / 15 600 cells); ≈ 15 600 / 40 000 candidates × ≤ 30 / 60 outline cells |
| S7b (5): the reachability transform (V05 §3.7 (2)) | ≤ 0.5 ms | ≤ 1 ms | one two-pass chamfer transform over the class raster, the raised parts' raised-way piers, dilated deck plans and deck faces rasterised into it once (≤ 40 raised parts at METROPOLIS); one read per rim cell of a candidate |
| SKY, per island | ≤ 7 ms | ≤ 10 ms | one L0 and one L1 batch; terrain ≈ 4 000–16 000 cells and `hydro` ≈ 1 700–6 500 on a 520–1 010 m frame (a disc to a grown spindle, V05 §3.8 (1)); the river; the claim search (≤ 2 000 sites at 10 m, each an O(1) fit and the crypt test); the ground landing claims |
| SKY: the island's gradient field (V05 §3.11) | ≤ 0.5 ms | ≤ 1 ms | ≤ 200 / 400 lattice cells: two ranks, one sort, the band map, ε and the three-box blur |
| the unit run, per island (volume 1's rate) | ≈ 8–12 ms, on the generation worker concurrent with the pool | ≈ 12–18 ms | ≤ 3 districts' worth, ≈ 2–5 % of the town's plots |
| SKY: the ground reference and the altitude, per island | ≤ 1 ms | ≤ 1 ms | ≤ 300 / 800 field cells under the projected solid, each a terrain read and a ceiling from the placed casters and the allocated tower rows |
| field pass 2: the strips onto the ground, per island (and per epoch) | ≤ 4 ms | ≤ 6 ms | ≈ 4 600 cell-sample tier walks at METROPOLIS's largest island (the low samples' long sweeps dominate), each ≤ 7 tiers × ≤ 20 steps of O(1) mask reads, most ending at the first hit, after the corridor's O(1) pre-test per cell (V05 §3.19 (5)); ring masks ≤ 60 000 / 160 000 bits, built once from `shadeBasis`; measured on the flat B-CITY fixtures and on a mountainous METROPOLIS fixture (relief class `mountainous`, island over a valley) |
| SKY's last step: strips onto islands and `shadeSky` | ≤ 1 ms | ≤ 1 ms | ≤ 500 lattice cells per receiving island, only under a higher island |
| the route distance (V05 §3.20) | ≤ + 5 % of the unit run's S10b and of the pooled S10b of the districts holding a landing | same | ≤ 3 accesses × 3 raster reads per pull that crosses arenas |
| SKY-F | ≤ 3 ms | ≤ 4 ms | the clearance check over the projected faces; ≤ 60 lights per island |
| **the model's critical path** | **+ ≤ 55 ms p50, ≤ 68 ms p95** | **+ ≤ 84 ms p50, ≤ 104 ms p95** | the unit runs hidden by the pool while the generation worker's concurrent work (hinterland unit + islands) stays below the pooled time; the ground's work falls by the carried share |
| S14, per island | ≤ 4 ms, ≤ 5 000 marks | ≤ 6 ms | the district rate on its fabric plus rim, shadow, access and slab records (≤ 7 slabs, ≤ 256 vertices each) |
| rendering, a tile an island covers | ≤ + 30 % of that tile's raster and light-pass time | same | the second base and surface layer (V05 §5.3) |
| rendering, the light pass on tiles no island's land covers | ≤ + 5 % against the same map without islands | ≤ + 5 % with three islands | the window without slabs and the per-tile slab cull (V05 §5.3 (2)); measured at P3; **fallback:** cull the slabs per tile by the exact union of their shadow sweeps (not the bounding box), re-measured |
| `setAttr` altitude, occlusion, underside, name | ≤ 5 ms | ≤ 5 ms | the record, a marks delta and a slab delta |
| a move | ≤ 65 ms | ≤ 70 ms | 5 ms plus two post-build placements (§12.6's 30 ms each) |
| a resize, a rim reshape | ≤ 5 ms + §12.6's scoped regeneration of each receiving district and of the district whose institution claim is re-searched where it receives no face (at most k ≤ 3 in all; V01 §6's sky row) + §12.6's scoped-regeneration law once over the plots the event re-cuts outside those districts (each re-claimed yard's covered plots by §7.3's local operator; a shrink's or an inward stretch's cleared and re-cut plots, §4.7 S10 (7); J-V5.81) + §12.6's 30 ms per re-claimed landing yard's top placement (`placeAt`, one per access, ≤ `accessCount.max`; a reshape's on the moved stretch's yards only) + §12.6's 30 ms per re-landed foot's placement | same | the buffer bisection (≤ 12 steps), one island L1 batch re-laying the strip and yards, the claim re-search (≤ 2 000 sites at 10 m, SKY's rate) only where the claim no longer fits; the re-cut plots' one scope — a yard's 12 × 14 m touches ≤ 3 plots, a shrink's the plots its eroded band and re-laid strip line cross; `plantRimWalk` once (two `Greenery` records) |
| New random on an island | ≤ 40 ms | ≤ 55 ms | SKY plus its unit run |
| `placeSkyAccess`, an access `delete` | ≤ 35 ms | ≤ 40 ms | an island L1 batch, a local re-derive and one placement |
| declaring an island after generation | ≤ 120 ms | ≤ 160 ms | the fill with its relocations, SKY, the unit run |
| `fallIsland` | ≤ 80 ms | ≤ 110 ms | relocations, demolitions, the archive; + §12.6's 30 ms per re-landed access of another island (V05 §3.16 (3)) |
| `IslandEpochStep`, per island | ≤ 2 ms plus its conversions at the rows above | same | the epoch's strips are the field pass 2 row |
| `sky.clearance`, per island and epoch (V05 §3.15 (7)) | ≤ 1 ms | ≤ 1 ms | the epoch scope's elements under the projected solid (≤ 300 / 800 field cells), each a footprint test and a top read; no geometry written |
| `skyAccessObstacles` and its callers' tests | ≤ 0.05 ms per call at ≤ 9 entries; linear in the entries beyond (≈ 0.005 ms per entry) | same | the raised-way solver tests each candidate span against the entries in O(entries), inside its own budget (§4.3); hand-placed accesses add entries (V05 §2.3b) |
| the shade map view's pending strip after an island edit | ≤ the field pass 2 row, computed only while that view is shown | same | never on the edit's own path |
| Update shade now | ≤ the field pass 2 row for one island + the field's recomputation over the two windows (≤ 2 ms) + §12.6's rate for the blocks it re-derives | same | a scoped evaluation, never a whole pass |
| memory | ≤ 5.2 MB for three islands (the strips ≤ 30 KB each, `shadeBasis` ≤ 2.5 KB each) | ≤ 9.3 MB (≤ 60 KB each; bases ≤ 2.5 KB each) | an island's arena ≈ 0.5 MB per district's worth; its rasters ≤ 0.1 MB; the sky surface layers only on covered tile positions, inside §8.8's canvas budget B |
| document | ≤ §2.9's 320 B per island building, plus ≤ 2 KB per island record and ≤ 2.5 KB for its `shadeBasis` (the rim copy ≤ 256 vertices × 8 B, the rest ≤ 0.5 KB) | same | |

The strip rows assume the sampler's default (one point per cell); at four points per cell (§4.5's fallback) they scale by
four — ≤ 16 ms at CITY and ≤ 24 ms at METROPOLIS per island — and the memory row's strips by two and a half (two bytes
per cell become five). The chain rule of V05 §3.20 (9) and the node term add no measurable work: the one is a filter on
the sky fill and the route reads already costed, the other one addend per evaluation. **Pre-decided fallbacks:** the
strip row missed → `shade.stepM` 8 → 16 m and `shade.ringMaskM` 4 → 8 m, re-measured (T-V5-20's one-cell agreement
re-checked), then the samples' reach cap for the strips 8 → 4, recorded as a declared shift of the term; the model rows
missed at M6-sky → the placement lattice coarsens 40 → 80 m and the island units join the pool as units (pool size ≥ 1),
re-measured; the rendering row missed → the sky surface layer at half resolution (rung 2's method), then rung 3 for the
sky base only, reported in the view telemetry; any other row missed is re-declared at its measured value for the owner's
veto (D-18). Volume 6 sums its century from these rows by citation (V06 §6.2).

## 7 Tests and acceptance bars

§13's families; each bar's class — **C** conformance, **P** parity, **O** outcome — in brackets. These bars take over the
base's island clauses as §13.2 lists them (T10-1, T13-1, T15-1 kept; T102-2's island clause is T-V5-19 – T-V5-21).

* **T-V5-1 — the gate** [C]. TOWN and every village rung never carry a sky allocation; a CITY with the setting off
  carries none and its ground digest equals the realism golden (T13-1); the `MFCG` and `FTG` presets carry none; a
  dossier-bound CITY whose `site.magic` resolves `none` refuses the setting (F-EDIT `dossier-owned`).
* **T-V5-2 — the count** [C]. 500 CITY seeds at the target at `wealthLevel` 0.5 and 1: every count 0–3 within ± 3 s.e. of
  the slot law at each seed's own `h_town`; slots never skip; `islands.count` 0–3 give exactly that many open slots on 200
  seeds each (their supply permitting, each empty slot reported).
* **T-V5-3 — the supply** [C, O]. V05 §3.3's calibration bars at P0; `A` monotone non-decreasing in `h_town` and
  `h_carried` over the rows (T10-1); `S_carried` equals the mean of the carried households' band values to 10⁻⁹.
* **T-V5-4 — the fill** [C]. On 500 CITY seeds with islands: ground plus sky households equal the roster's total exactly;
  carried households by band never include a band poorer than a band left uncarried; the stop cause is one of V05 §3.5's
  three; the fill converges within 100 batches; a slot below `minAreaHa` closes and its rows return; an institution slot
  carries exactly one instance of one of its tags and its staff, never a COUNT, GUILD or QUARTER record or member; no S3
  reservation is left without an owner by an institution slot (100 %: the hand-over instance or `park`, F-SKY-HANDOVER
  counted); no household a dossier district-defining record's raised share holds is carried (100 %).
