* **T-V5-5 — placement** [C, O]. 1 000 islands on B-CITY: no island without an assignment or declaration has more than
  10 % of its outline cells over `core`; 0 islands within `gapM` of another; 100 % inside the frame inset; ≥ 80 % of
  prisons ≥ 1 000 m from the plaza and ≥ 80 % of palaces within 1 000 m of it; every F-SKY-PLACE rung counted in the
  report; 100 % of islands placed by rule have a rim cell on admissible ground or within an admitted slanted row's run of
  it, including on the lake, estuary and sea fixtures; on a fixture with a row carrying `near: 'richest'`, its island's
  purpose term reads the district of highest prosperity mean.
* **T-V5-6 — outline and arena** [C]. 10 000 sky outlines per template: simple (§13.2's outline property), area within 1 %
  of `A`; each arena's rim is one closed chain carrying `rim`; exactly one void and one land face at L0 before water;
  every SKY commit record writes only its island's key and the ground's landing claims; for every template at every
  aspect its row admits, 100 % of outlines grown by the buffer resize to 1.5 × `areaHaMax` lie inside their frame.
* **T-V5-7 — terrain and the conjured river** [C]. On 500 islands with a river: its mean bearing within ± 15° of the
  ground river's; 0 non-monotonic beds; every river ends at the rim with `fall`; its width inside its clamp;
  F-SKY-TERRAIN's rungs counted.
* **T-V5-8 — access** [C]. Every island placed by rule has ≥ 1 access at generation (0 % `unreachable`); a declared island
  has ≥ 1 or is reported `unreachable`; every hoist and stair foot lies on land in an admissible face; every ground
  landing plot fronts a way or has its trail; rope lines and bridges of light within their rows' horizontal runs; every
  stair's helix ends at its top with a landing every `stair.landingM` of rise; no stair or bridge of light where `magic`
  is `none`; `guarded` only on a prison; after SKY-F every island landing attach is met by an island way, and 100 % of
  ground landing attaches are met by a walkable part-0 way after S8; the ground landing of a `secret` access is fronted
  and withheld from every key that does not admit it (100 %). **With item 110** (on 500 CITY and METROPOLIS seeds with
  islands and item 110's rows on, and on hand-built fixtures): 0 raised decks or raised-way piers inside any access
  column or any landing yard dilated by `deckGapM` (100 %, whether the raised part came before SKY or after it — an
  epoch's raised-way pass, a T4 stroke); every rope line and bridge of light ≥ `lineClearM` over every raised deck and
  raised-way pier it crosses; 0 feet and 0 landing yards on a deck face; 0 landing attaches met only by a raised deck or
  an aqueduct; a T4 `raised` stroke ending on a rim leaves 0 ways on the sky arena and places 1 access of an admitted kind
  (`bend.raised.island`), an aqueduct stroke to a rim and a `raised` or aqueduct stroke on an island's key are bent with
  their keys' lines, and a `raised` stroke across an access's column is re-routed or, where `skyAccessObstacles` offers
  no re-route, ends at the last ground vertex before it (`bend.raised.hoist`). **Many hand-placed accesses** (J-V5.84):
  on a fixture island with twelve `placeSkyAccess` events, `skyAccessObstacles` returns one entry per live access, every
  foot `footGapM` from every other (a placement inside the gap bent by `bend.access.foot`'s rule to the nearest
  admissible point), a raised stroke beneath is routed clear of all of them, and the obstacle test's time grows linearly
  with the entries (V05 §6).
* **T-V5-9 — the unit run's contract** [C]. The rim strip is never cut or plotted; `SeamReport.portalsTotal` = 0; the
  island's district count equals V01 §3.10.3 (2)'s count for its allocation read with `districtHa`; an island with
  `h_carried` = `h_town` = 1 has 3 districts at METROPOLIS and at least 2 at CITY on ≥ 90 % of seeds; every island
  district's kind is a `sky-*` kind of V05 §2.4 and every fabric hint a valid `FabricStrategyId`; on 500 seeds, 0
  households re-homed from an island by S10a's band-range step, and each carried band's share of the island's plot land
  within ± 5 % of its carried share; its housed households equal its allocation's, or F-SKY-UNIT's overflow is reported;
  the runner lays no second terrain on the island and its field step reads the unit inputs' values (V05 §3.12).
* **T-V5-10 — underside and altitude** [C, O]. The apex clears the tallest caster beneath by ≥ `clearanceM` on 100 % of
  islands after SKY-F; the altitude fixed at SKY stands (no F-SKY-CLEAR raise) on ≥ 99 % of 1 000 islands at CITY and
  METROPOLIS (V05 §3.9 (5)'s measurement), measured with item 110's terrace, raised-way and deck-plot rows on at their
  defaults, and the 100 % clause holds after every epoch of a century run (`sky.clearance`); each underside tier's ring
  lies inside the one above it translated by the apex step, with decreasing area; each profile's measured radius ratio
  (the tier rings' equivalent radii ÷ R) within 0.05 of ρ(z_i) at every tier.
* **T-V5-11 — lights and names** [C]. Rim orbs at 40 m ± 1 m; the keel light's intensity 4 000 × `h_carried`; a settled
  island's magical lights 0; every island named; its `quarter` label drawn at the city band at the reference viewport on
  100 % of visible islands.
* **T-V5-12 — labels and occlusion** [C]. Under `opaque`, 0 ground labels drawn inside a visible island's land face; under
  `ghost`, a sky label wins a collision against a ground label of equal priority; under `offset`, sky labels drawn at
  their displaced positions; with the sky layer hidden, 0 sky labels, 0 sky slabs, no island shadow drawn and no model
  digest changed (T15-1); every shipped sheet loads at every band with every sky semantic resolved (§8.2), and the
  `fallen` row draws over the landfall's members.
* **T-V5-13 — the underside's shadow under the fixed, path and cursor suns** (J98) [C, O]. A hand-built island (8 ha,
  `root` profile, 100 m altitude) above the golden TOWN, `ghost` occlusion (and (a)–(c) again under `opaque`), the view at
  the 1:500 stop, CPU reference and GPU pass: (a) under the fixed sun set to 15°, 30° and 60° and the path sun at three
  hours, the shadow's boundary on open ground within max(1 px, §8.4 (c)'s floor-cell diagonal) of the analytic union of
  the tiers' projections; (b) the shadow's area strictly increasing as the sun lowers; (c) the same island with a `flat`
  profile: at 15° the `root`'s shadow reaches past the `flat`'s by `depth · cot 15°` within 1 px toward the sun (toward
  the footprint); (d) the cursor sun at nine pointer positions at the 1:500 and 1:2 000 stops: the shadow falls away from
  the pointer, `H` ≥ 1.5 × the list's top, the GPU within §13.4's tolerance, and at a fixed zoom panning the island into
  and out of view changes `H` by 0; (e) at 15° a ground point under the island's centroid but outside the root's
  projection is lit (V > 0); (f) the penumbra's width at 200 m altitude exceeds that at 100 m; (g) the `flat` island with
  a 30 m rim against a 10 m rim: at 15° its shadow reaches 20 m · cot 15° farther toward the sun within 1 px (the owner's
  "thickness"); (h) at 15° the `root`'s rung-3 shadow is one connected region (the prism form); §13.4's base
  island-shadow golden stands.
* **T-V5-14 — over time** [C]. T8-6 stands (a CITY dropping below 10 000 loses its islands through waning, settled and
  fallen over their dwells); on the hook fixture a settled island refloats before any island is raised; a resize keeps
  every plot address away from the rim (100 %). **Resize and reshape:** after a growing and a shrinking resize and a rim
  reshape on the hook fixture's institution island, the rim strip lies `rimStripM` inside the new rim everywhere and
  every landing yard meets its top on it (100 %), the rim walk stands on the re-laid strip — one `esplanade` and one
  `street_trees` `Greenery` after each — 0 ways cross the strip, a growing resize re-derives exactly the districts that
  receive band faces and the district whose institution claim is re-searched, a shrink and a reshape's inward stretch no
  district but that claim's district where the claim is re-searched (every other island district byte-identical, the
  district count unchanged), after a re-search the institution stands on its new claim in one building with the old one
  demolished, and a shrink requested below the institution floor is bent to it with the claim keeping a site in its own
  district (J-V5.77). **The landfall** adds and removes no ground edge, demolishes exactly the covered set's buildings,
  keeps every locked entity, credits the ledger with the covered area × `landfall.stoneM3PerM2`, relocates every dossier
  use before its building goes; on a fixture whose footprint covers one entity of every kind of V05 §3.16's table — a
  wall stretch, a `main` gate, a tower, a bridge deck, a market reservation, a way, a hinterland field, an aqueduct span,
  a viaduct's raised-way pier and the span it carries (itself less than half covered), a generated retaining wall and a
  bridge carrying deck plots — each takes its row's outcome: the stretch `breached` over its covered length in the
  enceinte's `condition` (a knot at each end, a run already `dismantled` kept) and, the ring in use, back to `standing` at
  the next `fortification` phase (V06 §3.7.5; T-V6-11); the tower and the `main` gate inside the `breached` run, no state
  written to any `Tower` or `Gate`; the bridge `ruined`; the reservation vacant under the rubble; the way `rubble` and
  routed as `track` with its class before the fall in `Way.formerClass` — a covered lane volume 6 had downgraded keeping
  its earlier `formerClass`, 0 covered ways without one — until an epoch's clearance restores that class and clears it
  (V06 §3.5.2; T-V6-66 on the same fixture); the field a `quarry` carrying its `source`, read by no underground quarry
  rule; the covered span, the pier and the span on it `ruined`, the viaduct's crossing closed and the ways beneath it
  touched only where covered, the aqueduct's terminal listed as run dry and its lost supply read at that epoch's
  `raisedWays` step when the fall is an epoch's (the next epoch's for a hand `convert`); the retaining wall `decayed` over
  its covered length and still `decayed` after a re-derivation until an epoch's restoration re-derives it (V06 §3.19.4
  (6)); the deck plots' households relocated before their buildings are demolished and the plots tombstoned with the deck
  — 0 vacant plots left on a ruined deck, and a later repair of the deck re-offering its faces to new lineages; the
  covered spans, piers, walls, ways, plots, reservations and fields in the decoration's `members` — each span as
  `{ raised, span: [a, b] }` and each pier as `{ raised, support }` on item 244's keys, the same members after a
  re-derivation of the viaduct, each leaving in the batch of the op that ends its cover (V06 §3.5.2), the deck plots
  listed as fates, never members; a locked wall kept; each change a listed fate and all of it in V05 §5.5's per-landfall
  CSV; the fall's undo restoring every covered state and each way's class and prior `formerClass`. **Another island's
  foot** (J-V5.83): on a fixture where the footprint covers the ground landing of a second island's rope line and of its
  hoist, the rope line is re-landed outside the covered set within its run and the hoist re-kinded at its top (or dropped
  with F-SKY-ACCESS's last rung where no kind fits, the island reported `unreachable` where none remains), in the same
  batch, each a listed fate, and the fall's undo restores both accesses as they were. **The dossier's removal:** a dossier
  absorb removing an island's palace leaves the island at once without it, and at the next epoch the island takes the
  next palace instance where one exists, else becomes a prosperity island where the rich band admits it, else wanes (the
  three cases on fixtures); **under a lock** (J-V5.82), a locked island keeps `reason: 'institution'` with no
  institution and a vacant claim, the fate "held without its institution" listed at the absorb and at every epoch, and
  the re-reasoning runs at the first epoch after the lock is lifted, while a lock on `altitudeCm` alone does not hold (i)
  or (ii). Each conversion's undo restores the model byte for byte.
* **T-V5-15 — goldens and determinism** [C]. Two sky fixtures join §13.3's goldens — a CITY with a prosperity island and
  a conjured river; a METROPOLIS with a palace island and a prison island — identical on the five engines; every stream
  key distinct (§6.1's sky fixture); an island raised after a fall takes a new part; a document with an island declared,
  moved, resized, given an access and brought down replays to the session's digest. **Item 110's pin and shift**
  (J-V5.67): with item 110's terrace, raised-way and deck-plot rows off, both sky goldens are byte-identical to their
  pre-item-110 digests — a `NoOpPin` registered on V12 §3.7.8's engine, reading "off" as V11 §3.24.0's switch table (every
  `deck.offer` 0, `terrace.enabled` false, the raised-way rows off) and "byte-identical to before item 110" as its
  off-state set; switching the rows on at their defaults changes both once, recorded as one declared shift naming the two
  fixtures (raised ways and retaining walls beneath and on the islands; `groundRefCm` and the altitudes where a raised
  part stands under a solid) by V12 §3.7.6's bumps; the METROPOLIS fixture pins one raised way under an island (T-V5-31
  (a)).
* **T-V5-16 — editing** [C, O]. Every event of V05 §4.2 undoes exactly; §7.3's oracle checks byte for byte outside V05
  §4.3's scopes on B-EDIT sequences with island edits (10³ in the PR tier, 10⁴ nightly); a move keeps every address on
  the island; New random on an island keeps the ground's digest; a declaration where `h_town` = 0 carries the `held
  against its supply` fate; every bend of V05 §4.2's table is produced on its fixture with its key and line

  the raised, aqueduct, retaining and wall bends), none a refusal; the dossier-magic refusal holds; under `offset`
  occlusion a click on a lifted island's house and on its retaining wall selects that entity, and a stroke drawn over the
  lifted island lands where it is drawn.
* **T-V5-17 — exports** [C]. GeoJSON sky coordinates equal `anchor` + local exactly and validate; each island's VTT scene
  imports with no manual steps; the glTF island mesh is a closed manifold and validates; CityJSON with PLAT's extension

* **T-V5-18 — budgets** [C]. V05 §6's rows on B-CITY and B-CITY-lite METROPOLIS seeds with three islands, or their
  fallbacks applied and reported; the one-click path unchanged (§12.5).
* **T-V5-19 — the shade strip moves the field, gently** (T102-2's island clause) [C, O]. A uniform district — level
  ground, one district kind, no driver varying across it — with a hand-built island (8 ha, `root`, underside 100 m above
  its ground) over its southern edge, latitude 50°, `w_sun` at its default: (a) the mean pass-2 prosperity over the cells
  the strip blocks for at least three samples is lower than over the district's unblocked cells by the expected
  difference — `w_sun` × the mean shade share of those cells, passed through the field's smoothing computed on the same
  fixture, never assumed — within ± 10 % of it; (b) no plot's band differs by more than one from the same fixture with the
  strip left out; (c) with `w_sun` = 0 the field and the town are byte-identical to the run without the island term, and
  with no island every strip is empty and the field byte-identical to the base's pass 2 (no-op pins); (d) the strip
  identical on the five engines; (e) an island at the frame's edge reports F-SKY-SHADE's clipped share; (f) V05 §6's
  strip row.
* **T-V5-20 — the model and the picture agree; the underside shapes the strip** (J98) [C]. For each sample, the CPU
  reference's rung-3 shadow of the smooth tiers under the path sun set to the equinox at that sample's hour, on flat
  ground, covers exactly the cells the strip marks for that sample except cells its boundary crosses; a `root` island's
  strip at sample 0 reaches past a `flat` island's of the same outline by its depth × `c` within one cell along +ŝ
  (toward the footprint); a 30 m rim against a 10 m rim lengthens the `flat` island's strip at sample 0 by 20 m × `c`
  within one cell along +ŝ; the basis rim's simplification (≤ `shade.basisVertexCap` vertices) moves no strip bit on the
  fixtures.
* **T-V5-21 — order, lag and the supply** [C]. (a) `S_town` at S7b and at an epoch equal to 10⁻⁹ between `w_sun` at its
  default and 0 when only the islands' strips differ (item 102 (c)); (b) after a move, an altitude, a resize and New
  random on an island, the ground's field rasters, every plot's derivation record and the shade view's veil
  byte-identical to before — only the pending outline moves — the strip's basis stale and the fate listed; at the next
  epoch (or after Update shade now, over its two windows only) the strip recomputed from the island's new state and the
  field moved, the new `shadeBasis.by` and the strip's `evaluated.by` reading `epoch` (or `update`, with the document's
  current epoch); an undo of Update shade now restores the former basis, strips and field cells; (c) the epoch after a
  fall carries no bits from that island; (d) a document with an island moved and an epoch run replays to the session's
  field byte for byte; (e) a lower island under a higher one reads `shadeSky` > 0 on its cells in the higher one's strip
  and 0 elsewhere; (f) on a fixture where a house (volume 6's building shade mask, after one epoch) and the island shade
  the same sample point at the same sun, the OR counts it once, at the default sampler and at four points per cell alike,
  and the strip's `bits` equals the mask's; (g) the participant `sky.islands` runs once per epoch at `afterField`, after
  S6's re-evaluation, and a history run with the engine's other participants switched off draws the same island values;
  ground plus sky households equal the epoch's target after `siting` (100 %), the released households placed first from
  the displaced pool with cause `island` and the taken ones subtracted by `demandTaken`; (h) **the lag survives a save**:
  move an island, save, reload, then re-derive a block with a prosperity brush — the field raster and every derivation
  record equal those of the same session without the save, byte for byte; the same after an F-SKY-CLEAR raise and after
  a `w_sun` = 0 table (the strips still computed and held); the same on a **terraced fixture**: an island over a ward that
  S10 and S11 level with retaining walls — the strips computed at S10a equal those a load rebuilds after the levelling,
  bit for bit — and after a T2 terrain stroke under a strip (the bits re-read over its scope from the unchanged basis equal
  a load's rebuild); **a raised island**: an island raised at an epoch (after S6) and one declared by T5 between epochs,
  each saved and reloaded before the next evaluation — the load writes no basis, neither casts nor receives a strip, and
  the field raster and every derivation record equal those of the same session without the save, byte for byte; after the
  next epoch's evaluation (or Update shade now on it) each casts; a document whose `schemaVersion` predates the basis step
  loads with the basis of every unfallen island computed once, and never again; **one strip per evaluation**: with volume
  1's S9b registered, the strips S9b's manor sun factor reads and those field pass 2 folds are the same held strips, bit
  for bit, at generation and at a re-derivation from S10a (whose bases are written at S10a's start, before step (i)).
* **T-V5-22 — siting across the island** (item 103) [C, O]. On 300 CITY seeds with islands, in the siting statistics
  suite (item 103 (e)): (a) no class `IslandRule.carry.never` excludes is carried by any island (100 %) — a top-step
  emitter, a heavy processor, a wholesale or storage class, a class with a hard need for running water, a river bank, the
  harbour, a gate or a quay, an active chain's working node — and no NEEDS row is satisfied by island water; (b) a class
  with a district coverage row serves the island's districts at or above the ground's served share of districts, its
  island members spaced by its same-kind row, and with the coverage weight 0 the run is byte-identical to one without
  coverage (no-op pin); (c) island classes with a ground supply edge stand at a mean route distance to their providers
  below that under random placement on the island; (d) the ground's suppliers of island classes stand nearer the landing
  feet than under random placement; (e) a `secret` access carries no pull of a class whose clientele is not clandestine
  (100 %); (f) the crime-aligned classes stand farther from a prison's guarded hoist foot than their baseline; (g) on
  hand-built fixtures every route distance equals the sum of its legs to the centimetre (a foot outside the walls reaching
  an inner class through its gate), `skyRoute` names the access of the least sum, and a `river` or `sea` edge across
  arenas is ∞; mirroring item 105 (k) across the arenas: (h) a multi-output producer with island consumers sits nearer its
  dominant output's landing foot than its random baseline, and island consumers of a shared ground provider bind to it by
  the route; (i) split pairs across the arenas appear for split classes past the threshold, are reported, and never
  appear in the parity presets; (j) the per-arena relaxation settles within its cap, the second pass moving fewer members
  than the first, byte-stable across runs and worker interleavings; (k) with the web's weights, `comfortM` and the spacing
  at 0, the result is byte-identical to the run without items 105 and 107 (no-op pin).
* **T-V5-23 — custom institutions on islands** (item 104) [C]. A dossier custom institution of archetype `exotic-tower`

  their sites where nothing else changed; (g) a T6 declaration of a processing node onto an island is held and lists the
  "crosses the hoist" fate; zero placement failures.
* **T-V5-25 — the magical node** [C]. On 300 CITY seeds each of a dossier fixture without deposits and the same dossier
  with a `magical_node` `available`: `h_town` rises by exactly `node.bonus` (clamped at 1) wherever the interpolated value

  rename of the ruin — while on the fixture with no ruin it names the deposit and no ruin.

  the island's strip is computed identically and only §4.5's weight differs.

**Parity.** No reference has islands (II.6 (8)), so no parity number binds them. The nearest look is Neighbourhood's lone
patch — its own ground tone and a drop shadow marking its extent on an empty field (`research/findings/vg-ng-devlogs.md`
§B3.5) — so at M6-sky five island close-ups in `hatched` are shown beside five Neighbourhood patches under §13.6's
protocol as a descriptive pair set — V12 §3.9.1's `PanelSpec` for M6-sky, a descriptive NG set — rated for "reads as a
floating patch": information for the owner, never a gate.

## 8 Build plan

**Milestone M6-sky** — §16's row: after M6-U (the underground first, J122; V05 §11), its P0 spike beside M5, on M4's
hooks (S7b (5), the island conversion kinds) and volume 1's unit run (M5). Its price is this volume's estimate below,
outside the base total as a staged subject (§16's totals computed once, file f item 568). Volume 9's AP7 (the Sky group
and island menu, the island handles, Update shade now, the strips and their legend; 0.5–1 pw) is built beside P4 and
priced in §16's row of the application's staged screens, not here.

| phase | content | exit (executed) | pw |
|---|---|---|---|
| P0 — spikes | the supply calibration run (V05 §3.3); placement over 1 000 islands with the reachability filter; the unit inputs to volume 1's unit runner on a hand-built arena (with `districtHa` and the gradient: the band shares and the re-homed count); the caster allowance measurement (V05 §3.9 (5)) with item 110's rows on; the strip against the rung-3 shadow on one fixture; the save-and-reload of a lagging strip | the ⟨P0⟩ rows set or the fallbacks applied; T-V5-5's rates; the runner's contract passes T-V5-9 on the fixture; T-V5-10's ≥ 99 % clause; T-V5-20 on the fixture; T-V5-21 (h) on the fixture | 1.5–2 |
| P1 — the model | the records (§2.7, §2.2's sky levels, §2.5's sky fields, §2.9's `nextSkyPart` and the basis on load, the landfall's `LandUse.source`); `shadeBasis` and its load path; S7b (5)'s gate, slots, reasons (the archetype match and the ladder filter included), fills with `carry`, the held shares and the node term, the hand-over of S3 reservations, outline, reachability and placement; the arena with its outline-sized frame, the terrain echo, the river; the underside, the ground reference and the altitude at SKY (item 110's casters among them); the claim search with the crypt test; the gradient field; the strips from the bases on the unlevelled receivers, their fold into field pass 2 and the island-free value (§4.5); the sky receivers; F-SKY-FILL, -PLACE, -TERRAIN, -SHADE, -CRYPT, -HANDOVER; SKY's own ground data keys (§4.1), the strips held from their first read and the bases at S10a's start, the optional basis and its schema-chain step (§7.5); item 111's no-op on the island | T-V5-1 to T-V5-7, T-V5-10, T-V5-19, T-V5-21 (a), (e), (f), (h), T-V5-25, T-V5-26, T-V5-32, T-V5-33, T-V5-37; `plat calibrate register --check` green over the cells it lands (`islands.json` whole, registry row 56) | 3.75–4.75 |
| P2 — access, unit inputs and siting | access tops, feet and landing claims (ground and hinterland) with the S8 destinations (§4.6), the rim strip and its trees, the island's context vector, the `sky` request and `skyUnitInput` with `districtHa`, the kinds and the fabric prior, SKY-F with the clearance check and both repairs, the sky lights (§4.7's sky rows), names; the route distance in both S10b runs and the island's coverage units (§4.7, §4.5); the supply web, splits, per-arena order and relaxation, the district term, `comfortM` and spacing across the arenas (V05 §3.20 (10)–(12)); chains across the island, custom and `activeChains[]` (V05 §3.20 (9)); the rim as a barrier (V05 §3.21); item 110 against the accesses — admissible ground without decks and raised-way piers, `lineClearM`, `skyAccessObstacles` for the raised-way solver and T4, the island's retaining walls in the unit run's contract, the generator's reads of the sky (V05 §3.22 (3)–(5); §2.7, §4.3) | T-V5-8, T-V5-9, T-V5-11, T-V5-22, T-V5-23, T-V5-24, T-V5-27, T-V5-28, T-V5-30 (with T-V5-8's and T-V5-27's item-110 clauses), T-V5-34 (a); `plat calibrate register --check` green over the cells it lands (the sky rows of `lights.json`, registry row 51, with the `keel` row's `radiusMarginM` cell) | 4–5.25 |
| P3 — the picture | the marks with their slots and sheet rows (§8.2), the three occlusion modes with the underside and tether, the sky surface layer in the tile worker and the light pass (CPU reference and GPU) with the window without slabs (§8.4 (c)), rung 3's prism tiers, the cursor sun's floor, labels; the strip's outlines in volume 9's shade map view | T-V5-12, T-V5-13, T-V5-20, T-V5-34 (b) | 3–4.25 |
| P4 — editing | V05 §4.2's events and parameters (the island brushes, the access `setAttr` semantics, the pointer mapping in `offset`, an island wall), dry runs with the shade fate and the bend keys, Update shade now, locality, undo, DR27 and its coherence with the dossier-removal path, the clerk's `WhyProvider` with the strip, route, link and siting answers; `convert removeIsland` and the removed ordinals with their holds | T-V5-16, T-V5-29, T-V5-35 (a) | 2.25–3 |
| P5 — exports | GeoJSON (the strip features included), SVG, PNG, VTT, glTF, CityJSON's extension types, the statistics rows | T-V5-17 | 1–1.5 |
| P6 — time | `IslandEpochStep` with the household channels (V06 §2.1, §3.8.1) and the re-reasoning of an island whose institution left (under a lock included), the bases and strips re-read at each epoch and the lag, the landfall over every covered kind and another island's access, the participant `sky.clearance` with `reclearIsland` (V06 §3.2) and the ledger, by hand and on the M4 grow path; the engine-driven schedule joins volume 6's milestone | T-V5-14, T-V5-15, T-V5-18, T-V5-21 (b)–(d), (g), T-V5-31, T-V5-35 (b), T-V5-36 | 1.75–2.5 |
| **total** | | | **17.25–23.25** |

**Dependencies:** P0 needs M4's hooks and volume 1's unit runner (M5); P1 needs §4.5's daylight term with its per-sample
mask and model sampler and item 103's profile columns for the noxious exclusion (a fixture profile set until volume 11's
rows land); P1 → P2 → P3; P2's route distance needs §4.7's distances between arenas and its archetype match item 104's
profile resolution (fixtures until then); P2's chain rules need item 104 (b)'s active chains through the port (volume 8)
and volume 1's working-quarter reading (fixture chains and a stub reading until then); P1's node term needs volume 8's
deposits row (a fixture digest until then); P6's registration needs volume 6's `EpochParticipant` (a direct call from
the M4 grow path until then); P6's household channels need volume 6's `demandTaken` row and the displaced pool's cause
`island` (§4.7's overflow pass inside the conversion until then); P1's crypt test needs volume 2's basement ladder and
volume 11's crypt column (the full form and a fixture column until then); P2's web, splits and spread need items 105 and
107 in the base and volume 11's goods, balance and `split` rows (fixture rows until then), and the rim barrier item 106's
`barrier` closure and volume 11's `sheer` row; item 110's parts need §2.7's raised parts and `RetainingWall`, §4.7's
platform datums, the deck plots, volume 2's stack envelope (V02 §3.15.2) and volume 11's raised-way height and terrace
rows (fixture rows until then), and P6's `sky.clearance` needs volume 6's `afterGrowth` participant table and
`EpochScope` (a direct call after the M4 grow path's growth until then); P4 and P5 after P2; P6 after P4. **Risks, in
order:** the two-surface light pass's frame cost (P3; fallback: half resolution, then rung 3 for the sky base); the
field's power to tell rich towns from poor (P0; fallback: the `wealthLevel` blend); the unit-run contract across volumes
(P0; its requirements tested as a contract, T-V5-9); the strip's cost at METROPOLIS under the low samples (P1; fallback:
V05 §6's coarser step and mask, then the reach cap); the route distance's share of the pooled S10b (P2; fallback: island
classes stand at the land centroid for every ground-side read, post-build included); placement on awkward sites (P0;
F-SKY-PLACE's ladder); watertight lofting of rough tiers in glTF (P5; fallback: roughness 0 in the mesh only); the
gradient's band shares under the smoothing (P0; fallback: `field.bandInset` 0.1 → 0.2, then the smoothing's width halved
on the island, re-measured); the light pass on uncovered tiles (P3; fallback: the exact per-tile slab cull, V05 §6);
item 110's arches and decks under islands against `casterAllowanceM` (P0; fallback: V05 §3.9 (5)'s measured 99.9th
percentile with item 110's rows on).

**The register at the exits** (J-V5.80). Every phase that lands a registry table, or a calibratable column or cell of
one, exits with `plat calibrate register --check` (T-V12-20) green over the cells it lands. Two phases do: P1 lands
`islands.json` whole (registry row 56 — V05 §2.4's default rows, `accessSiting.deckGapM` and `lineClearM` among them,
although their readers are built at P2 and P6), and P2 lands the sky rows of `lights.json` (row 51; V05 §3.13) with the
`keel` row's `radiusMarginM` cell. The landing seat writes the entries in the same change with `plat calibrate register
--defaults --milestone V5-P1` (or `V5-P2`) — V12 §3.11's default line, `corpus` at M-CAL — for every calibratable column
of those cells that has no entry yet; a light-table column the base landed already holds one and takes none. This volume
writes no register line of its own. The three ⟨P0⟩ cells (`townSupply`, `carriedSupply`, `casterAllowanceM`) are set by
P0's spikes, which run on a spike branch with a draft `islands.json`, land no cell, and apply their fallbacks (V05 §3.3,
§3.9 (5)) before P1 lands the table; each lands as an `own` value whose provenance note names the run that set it ("set
by M6-sky's P0 run, V05 §3.3", or "…, V05 §3.9 (5)" for the caster allowance; never a `fixed:` note), and T-V5-3 and
T-V5-10 then hold it as ordinary bars at the phase exits. No frame holds an island, so the corpus stage closes each
default-line column of `islands.json` `fixed` with "no admissible frame" (V12 §3.11). Where M6-sky lands after M-CAL's
`ReconciliationRecord` is committed (J-V12.21), its default-line entries fall due at `M-CAL/sky` (V12 §3.11's *The due's
corpus run*; J-V12.75). P3's sheet rows are slots and semantics, whose values are volume 11's palette rows; P0, P4, P5
and P6 land no calibratable cell.

## 9 Decisions (each vetoable)

| id | decision |
|---|---|
| J-V5.1 | Chose to lay each island in its own local frame and stand it over the ground by one integer translation (`anchor`), over laying it in world coordinates, because a move then changes one attribute instead of warping every vertex, keeps every logged position on the island valid, and keeps every lattice point a lattice point; no rotation, since a turned island would leave the lattice (a turn is a reshape). |
| J-V5.2 | Chose sky part numbers from a never-reused document counter (`nextSkyPart`), the count-law slot a separate field, over the base's "sky parts 1–3", because an island raised after a fall would otherwise reuse a part whose addresses stand as tombstones, against §6.3's never-reused rule. |
| J-V5.3 | Chose to read both supplies on the field's absolute value with value-keyed rows, over the base's town cut, because the cuts are per-town quantiles: a town mean against its own quantile measures the distribution's shape, never the town's wealth, and a mean never reaches the field's top (RV-pc-3); the rows are calibrated at P0 with a pre-decided fallback. |
| J-V5.4 | Chose to give each slot its purpose by one weighted draw among a prosperity island and the eligible institution rows, the institution taken from the roster's own demand, over adding an institution the roster did not ask for, because the roster decides demand (§4.2 S2) and the population stays whole. |
| J-V5.5 | Chose to settle each island's outline and placement inside S7b (5), before S7b (1) allocates the ground, over placing islands after S7b, because a slot that cannot be placed must return its households before the ground's allocation, never through a second S7b run. |
| J-V5.6 | Chose to floor the altitude at the rim, the root and a 25 m clearance over the tallest thing beneath, over clamping the root's depth, because the owner asked for the rock beneath to shape the shadow (J98): a deep root lifts its island rather than being cut short. |
| J-V5.7 | Chose to resize an island by eroding or dilating its rim with the kernel's buffer, over re-drawing the outline from its stream (the base's comment), because a re-draw moves every rim point and re-cuts plots all round, while a buffer changes only a band at the rim and keeps the shape. |
| J-V5.8 | Chose hoists — a winch house and a cage on ropes, as clifftop monasteries were reached — as the default access, rope lines over water, and floating stairs and bridges of light only where the world has magic, over bridges of light everywhere, because the realism tiebreaker tempers the fantasy: the island is the one magic the setting asks for. |
| J-V5.9 | Chose to leave the island's magical lights ungated by the `magic` circumstance, over hiding them when it is `none`, because the island exists by its own setting and its magic is what holds it up; the circumstance still gates the stairs, the bridges of light and the ground's magical lights. |
| J-V5.10 | Chose to make generation and epochs obey the supply law strictly while letting an author's declaration or lock hold an island against it, with the fate `held against its supply` listed, and to keep the rung gate absolute, over refusing such a declaration or letting the rung be overridden, because everything is editable yet the base already disables the setting below CITY. |
| J-V5.11 | Chose to make the landfall a conversion of everything it covers — buildings demolished, plots and reservations left vacant under rubble, fields quarried as a surface working (its `source` the landfall, never an underground quarry), walls breached with their towers and gates, bridges ruined, ways buried and routed as tracks until an epoch clears them, the stone credited to the salvage ledger — with no edge added or removed, over cutting a new ruin face, leaving no mark, or covering plots and fields only, because it stays local, undoes exactly, keeps the old plan for regrowth, ties the fall into the ledger's salvage demand, and leaves no intact wall or open street standing in a field of fallen rock. |
| J-V5.12 | Chose to draw everything on existing concerns (`island.place`, `terrain.noise`, `water.river`, `field.chance`, `naming`) with named creation indices, over a new concern, because §6.1's registry already reserves `island.place` for the islands and no draw here reads another concern's order. |
| J-V5.13 | Chose to redraw only empty slots at an epoch, a supplied island staying, over the base's reading that a slot may close and send its island down the decline ladder, because an island falls for want of magic, never by chance while it is paid for. |
| J-V5.14 | Chose to floor the cursor sun's height at 1.5 × the display list's top whenever the sky layer is visible and the document holds an island that is not fallen, over letting the sun stand below an island at close zooms or flooring it only while an island is in view, because a sun below a floating island casts its shadow upward and reads as an error, and a floor that switched on as an island panned into view would jolt every shadow on screen mid-pan. |
| J-V5.15 | Chose an underside of nested prism tiers following a profile function (cone, bowl, root, flat), over the base's one slab and over a full mesh in the light pass, because the tiers give the rock's silhouette to the existing slab traversal at a few records per island. |
| J-V5.16 | Chose a second base and surface layer only on tiles an island covers, lit separately and composed by the occlusion mode, over lighting the ghosted ground as the island's surface, because the ground seen through a ghosted island must take its own light and the island's shadow. |
| J-V5.17 | Chose to claim the ground landing yards at SKY, before S8, touching a way spine, over placing landings after the plots are cut, because the yard then fronts a way by construction and no plot is re-cut. |
| J-V5.18 | Chose a rank gradient on a prosperity island — each carried band given its carried share of the island's land, mapped onto its own band's inner range, the richest on the high ground and the rim that looks over the city (0.6 elevation, 0.4 view) — with a small noise term, the shade and the brush on top, over one uniform value or a driver raster, because a uniform value houses only one band and S10a would re-home the rest to the ground (the owner's "prosperity is a gradient and a band"), while an island has no market, wall, tannery or low damp ground to drive a raster; an institution island stays uniform. |
| J-V5.19 | Chose to refuse the setting where a bound dossier's `site.magic` is `none`, and to keep the setting and the circumstance independent without a dossier, because a floating island would contradict a dossier's magic-less world (item 20 (h)'s coherence) while a standalone map's two toggles are the author's. |
| J-V5.20 | Chose to re-land an island's accesses after a move by closing and opening the landing designation on two ground plots each, over re-deriving the ground under the old and new feet, because a move then touches no ground geometry. |
| J-V5.21 | Chose `delete` of an island to relocate its households to the ground without a ruin, the ruinous path being `fallIsland`, over one behaviour for both, because an author removing an island and a history bringing one down are different acts. |
| J-V5.22 | Chose to release a waning island's households in reverse ordinal order into each epoch's ground demand, over moving them all at the wane, because §4.11 empties a declining town by its suffix with a lag, and a wane is a decline. |
| J-V5.23 | Chose the conjured river's width by the ground's width law on a conjured share of the ground river's flow, over a fixed width, because the owner asked for a river that echoes the ground's, and the law keeps the echo proportional. |
| J-V5.24 | Chose the prison row's requirement band to be its paymaster's (`prosperous`), over its inmates', because the owner names a prison island as possible and the town's rulers keep it aloft. |
| J-V5.25 | Chose to hold the islands setting off under the `MFCG` and `FTG` presets, because no reference has islands and the parity presets switch every extension off (item 23). |
| J-V5.26 | Chose an unbuilt rim strip of 6 m inside the rim, planted as a rim walk with a row of trees, over plots to the edge — the Neighbourhood look, whose blocks are clipped by the patch's ragged edge (`research/findings/vg-ng-devlogs.md` §B2) — because no house stands on a cliff edge (the realism tiebreaker), the strip keeps the rim legible at every band, and the trees on the rim keep the floating patch's look `docs/IMPROVEMENTS.md` S1 asks for. |
| J-V5.27 | Chose to let an island edit leave the ground's field and its strips at their basis until the next epoch or a Re-derive from S10a — the fate listed, Update shade now offered for an immediate scoped change, the basis persisted so the lag survives a save (J-V5.47) — over re-evaluating the field at every island edit and re-deriving the blocks past τ as the terrain brush does, because the owner asked that shade move the town over time by the normal lag (item 102 (e)), and a drag of an island would otherwise re-cut plots under two strips far from the island for one gesture. |
| J-V5.28 | Chose to take volume 1's partition and district count for an island's districts and drop this volume's own count and seeds, over keeping both, because the landed V01 §3.10.3 lays them by the bent cut (J-V1.17) and two counts for one island would be two sources of one fact; the inputs this volume governs are V05 §3.12's contract, which V01 §3.10.3 reads. |
| J-V5.29 | Chose to fix the ground reference and the altitude at SKY from the casters already placed, the tower rows of the allocated classes and a caster allowance, over reading the built casters at SKY-F, because field pass 2 must read the island's shade before any plot is cut and the altitude must not move after that read; the rare taller caster raises it at SKY-F with the strip's basis kept, a share measured at P0 with a pre-decided fallback. |
| J-V5.30 | Chose the model's strip as a derived record over the tiers without roughness, carrying no edit kind, over the view's rough tiers or an editable strip, because the roughness is view data keyed by the render key (§6.2), which the model must never read, and a strip is a consequence of its island — edited through the island, item 102's weight and the brush; at the field's 32 m cell the roughness is below one cell. |
| J-V5.31 | Chose a fourth island institution row, the arcane `tower`, admitted only where the world has magic and taking a custom kind of the `exotic-tower` archetype, and the archetype column on the palace and cathedral rows, over leaving custom institutions off the islands, because item 104 asks that custom content land sensibly with no hand-written rules and a tower "on its own ground, apart" is the owner's unique institution in its plainest fantasy form; it arises only when the roster or the dossier demands one. |
| J-V5.32 | Chose to leave on the ground, by the editable table `IslandRule.carry`, every trade whose profile emits smell, smoke or water pollution at the top step, the heavy processors and wholesale and storage of item 107 (a), every class with a hard need for running water, a river bank, the harbour, a gate or a quay, and an active chain's working nodes, over excluding the top-step emitters only, because an island has no downwind, downstream or extramural ground, no quay or gate, and no water that satisfies a need (its conjured river is magic's own), and the owner's rule that the tannery goes "down river" and away from homes cannot be met there. |
| J-V5.33 | Chose to let an island's own terrain cast nothing on its own context, over running the terrain shade on the island's terrain, because that relief is a gentle dome at a quarter of the ground's amplitude (V05 §3.8) whose self-shade stays below a field cell; a higher island's shade on it is real and kept, and at an epoch its own buildings cast on it through volume 6's mask on its lattice, as item 102 (b) asks of every building. |
| J-V5.34 | Chose to keep the island's own placement score (V05 §3.7) apart from item 103's relation families, over scoring the island's position with them, because the island is placed as one body at S7b (5) before any plot exists, while the families score plots against placed neighbours; its purpose rows are the island-scale reading of the institutions' profiles, aligned by volume 11. |
| J-V5.35 | Chose to export the strip as a thematic layer only — GeoJSON features, the shade map view's SVG, the statistics — over carrying it in the lit raster, VTT, glTF and CityJSON, because those formats already carry the island's physical shadow and solid, and a model day's shade share is a statistic, not a picture. |
| J-V5.36 | Chose to measure every pull that crosses arenas by the route through the island's admitted accesses — the island's street leg, a 100 m transfer, the ride and the ground's street leg, as §4.7's `road` mode runs through a gate — and every nuisance by the straight distance in three dimensions, over one straight distance for all, because goods and people ride the hoist while smoke, smell and noise travel through the air. |
| J-V5.37 | Chose to count an island as k units for coverage rows counted by district or neighbourhood (k its district count by `districtHa`), one unit for walking-radius rows and ⌈H ÷ N⌉ for household rows, over one unit for every row, because the island's districts are real districts and the owner's example is "one apothecary per district", while the ride to the ground passes every walking radius. |
| J-V5.38 | Chose to OR the islands' shade into the daylight term sample by sample, over adding their shares to pass 1's, because a cell in a hill's shade and an island's at the same hour is in shade once — an added share would count it twice and could pass the term's all-day bound. |
| J-V5.39 | Chose to keep an active custom chain's resource and processing nodes on the ground — only its final seller and its consumers going up by the carried share, the island's own institution keeping its links by the route, an author's declaration outranking the rule with a listed fate — over carrying chain nodes by the proportional share alone, because the owner asked that a chain be placed as one connected thing: splitting its working steps across a hoist breaks it at its most visible point, and an island offers no terrain feature, extramural edge or water leg for a chain's upstream steps. |
| J-V5.40 | *Superseded by J-VI.13.* Chose to leave the new derivation row's number to the integrator, over taking DR26, because volumes 1, 6 and 8 each proposed a DR26 in parallel and one number for four rows would make the derivation table's references ambiguous; J-VI.13 numbered it DR27 (ID-DR). |
| J-V5.41 | Chose to read the dossier's `magical_node` deposit as a flat, positionless addend to `h_town`, acting only where the town already supplies some magic, over a placement pull toward the node or a multiplier, because the node's position — at a ruin's centre where a ruin exists (item 109 (f)), else S12a's decoration site — is volume 4's and volume 8's and is fixed after S7b (5) places the islands, reading it would tie the islands to the underground (item 2), a multiplier would vanish exactly where a node matters most (a middling town), and the owner's "no prosperity, no islands" forbids a node lifting a town that supplies nothing. |
| J-V5.42 | Chose to state the model sampler's default (nine suns at the midpoints of nine equal slices of the equinox day at the site latitude; one point per field cell) with volume 6's per-cell layout of points × suns, over leaving it to the coherence pass alone, because the strip and volume 6's building mask must OR bit for bit and neither can be built on an unstated layout; where item 102's application states either, its values govern and both volumes read them unchanged. |
| J-V5.43 | Chose a vault in the island's own rock — the rim slab, underside tier 0, present under every underside kind — for an island cathedral's crypt, the settlement's largest, over a ground crypt or no crypt, because only that keeps both the owner's crypt rule (item 109 (k) (2) as revised, J111: "cathedral also gets crypts but cemetaries get catacombs") and item 2's "no relation with the sewer system or underground"; island churchyards are surface burial grounds only, never catacomb candidates, while island churches still count toward the catacomb count. |
| J-V5.44 | Chose to state the reach cap of 8 × the caster's height as the model sampler's rule for every source — terrain, walls, islands and volume 6's building mask — over leaving each source its own reach, because the sources are OR-ed into one mask, which must follow one reach law (§8.4's cap). |
| J-V5.45 | Chose an island district size of its own, `districtHa` (3.5 ha at CITY, 4.5 at METROPOLIS), over the town's mean district area, because with the town's mean a full CITY island is always one district and three never occur at METROPOLIS, so the owner's "a collection of neighbourhoods" could not happen; the values are about one Neighbourhood patch, calibratable. The count's plot share is volume 1's `hood.constants.sky.plotShare`, cited and never copied, and the count itself is volume 1's `skyDistrictCount(plotLandM2, districtHa)` (V01 §3.16), called and never restated; the fill's stop and the coverage overshoot read the same cell (`plotShare · A`, V05 §3.5 (2)–(3)), so an edit of it moves both volumes' counts, the fill and the overshoot together. |
| J-V5.46 | Chose to hand an S3 reservation an institution island leaves to the row's hand-over classes — the cathedral's close to the town's largest parish church (or temple), the palace's site to the town hall or a guild hall, else a park — and to skip the reservation where the creation prefix already sends the institution up, over leaving it empty or re-planning S3, because a reserved site with no owner is a hole no stage fills, the old close as the mother church's and the old palace as the town hall are the historical pattern, and the castle holds its own S3 reservation (so handing the palace's to the castle row would reserve twice). |
| J-V5.47 | Chose to persist, per island, the `shadeBasis` its strips were last computed from — written only by a shade evaluation — over recomputing the strips on load from the live island or storing the strips' bits, because the owner's lag (item 102 (e)) must survive a save and a replay (the base recomputes the fields on load, §2.9), and a basis of ≤ 2.5 KB rebuilds the strips exactly where the bits would cost up to 60 KB. |
| J-V5.48 | Chose island district kinds keyed `sky-island`, `sky-palace`, `sky-temple`, `sky-prison` and `sky-tower`, owned by this volume and adopted by volume 1, and a fabric hint that is a prior (× 3 on the hinted template at S7c) and never a forced strategy, over reusing the ground's kinds or forcing the template, because `temple` is already a ground kind (one table, one row per key), and the owner's islands should vary as neighbourhoods do while leaning toward the fitting form. |
| J-V5.49 | Chose to read the rim as a `sheer` natural barrier (closure, no wall), never to wall an island by generation, and to make an author's island wall a barrier-closure enceinte walling only the landing yards with a gate at each attach, over walling the rim or refusing island walls, because item 106 asks walls to use natural barriers and an island is barrier all round, approachable only at its landings. |
| J-V5.50 | Chose to bind consumers to providers across the arenas by the route and to count a cross-arena consumer at the access's foot (or top) for its provider's two-way pull, over binding by the straight distance or counting it at its world position, because goods ride the hoist and arrive at the landing, never at the fields beneath the island. |
| J-V5.51 | Chose to let a split class's shop go up by the carried share while its works stays on the ground, joined by `branchOf` and the route, over keeping the whole class on the ground, because item 105 (h)'s split exists exactly so a noxious works and its retail can stand apart, and a rich island's butcher's stall or taphouse is historically faithful. |

Every calibratable column P1 and P2 land takes V12 §3.11's default line through `plat calibrate register --defaults` in the landing change, and P1's and P2's exits carry the register check (V05 §8's *The register at the exits*). The rejected alternative was a line of `measure` entries due at M6-sky for the three ⟨P0⟩ cells (`townSupply`, `carriedSupply`, `casterAllowanceM`), with T-V5-3 and T-V5-10 registered as V12 `BenchBarDef`s. The choice holds for three reasons. First, P0's spikes set those cells and apply their fallbacks before P1 lands the table, so no measurement of a landed cell is left to register; T-V5-3 and T-V5-10 then hold the cells as ordinary bars, and a failure fails the phase exit. Second, a whole-bar verdict would open a supply or caster fallback on a clause about another quantity (T-V5-10's ring nesting, T-V5-3's monotonicity). Third, no corpus frame holds an island, so the default line's corpus pass closes the island columns `fixed` with "no admissible frame", which is the honest status of a fantasy table. The ⟨P0⟩ cells' provenance notes name the run that set them; `carriedSupply` carries its ⟨P0⟩ mark, since V05 §3.3 sets it at the same run. |

## 12 CONFIRMED versus PLAUSIBLE

**CONFIRMED (read text against text):** every base citation — the records, stages, fields, rules, rows, events, ladders,
invariants, budgets and bars this volume cites — in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` with files f and g applied; the owner's words
in `docs/DEFINITION.md` and `docs/OWNER-INSTRUCTIONS-2026-09-25.md` (J98 to J104, J111); the sibling volumes at their
landed text — V01 §2.3, §2.4, §3.1, §3.2 (5), §3.5.5, §3.8.2, §3.9, §3.10.3, §3.14, §3.16, §3.17.7 (2) and §6's sky row
(the unit run, `skyUnitInput`'s caller, `skyDistrictCount`, `plantRimWalk`, the claim re-search, the crossing sites, the
relief-class read), V02 §2.6 and §3.15.2, V04 §2.3, §3.5.0a (9), §3.5.1 (3) and §3.10, V06 §2.1, §2.8, §2.12, §3.1 (3),
§3.2, §3.4, §3.5.2, §3.7.5, §3.8.1, §3.19 and §6.2, V08 §2.6 and §3.5, V09 §4.9 and §4.20.9, V10 §2.13, V11 §3.1 rows 51
and 56, V12 §3.7.8, §3.9.1 and §3.11; the Neighbourhood lone patch's look in `research/findings/vg-ng-devlogs.md` §B3.5
and `research/findings/watabou-extra-a.md` §3. **Executed:** the model suns at latitude 50° on the equinox, hour angles
−80° to +80° by 20° — altitudes 6.41°, 18.75°, 29.5°, 37.16° and 40.0°, azimuths 97.7° to 262.3°, reaches 8 (capped),
2.95, 1.77, 1.32 and 1.19 — from the standard solar formulas (V05 §3.19 (9)); the crypt test's −6.8 m and a flat
island's zero root from the defaults. Clean room: no reference code, class name, word list or table was read for or
copied into this volume.

**PLAUSIBLE until executed**, each with its measurement and its pre-decided fallback (V05 §3.3, §3.9 (5), §3.17, §6, §8):
every mechanism, default row and number this volume adds — the supply rows and the node bonus, the fill with its
noxious and chain-node exclusions, the placement score and its weights, the outline and underside rows, the terrain echo
and the river's share, the access rows and limits, the landing geometry, the light rows, the dwell spans and rates, the
landfall's cover share and stone yield, the strip's cost and its gentle effect (V05 §3.19 (9)), the sampler's one point
per cell being fine enough for item 102 (g)'s island bar, the route's transfer allowance, the chain rule's measured
effects, the gradient's band shares within ± 5 % under the smoothing, `districtHa`'s district counts, the reachability
filter leaving F-SKY-PLACE's rates intact, the light pass's + 5 % on uncovered tiles, the corridor pre-test's cost on
mountainous relief, `deckGapM` 4 m and `lineClearM` 8 m, whether item 110's arches and decks sit near `casterAllowanceM`,
the cost of `sky.clearance`, how often the unit run's levelling bites on an island's gentle relief, the resize floor and
the reshape's bend-back (T-V5-14), the resize row's figures and V06 §6.2's century bounds under D-18, the obstacle test's
linear cost with many hand-placed accesses, and the budgets and estimates — until M6-sky's P0 spikes and the phase exits
measure them (V05 §8).
